GENERAL PROVISIONS
§ 90.01 DEFINITIONS.
   For the purpose of this subchapter, the following definitions shall apply
unless the context clearly requires a different meaning.
   AT LARGE. The animal is off the premises of the owner and not under the
control of any person, either by leash or otherwise.
   ANIMAL CONTROL WARDEN. Any person authorized by the city to enforce the
provisions of this subchapter in the control of animals.
   CUSTODIAN. Any person, partnership, business entity or other organization of
any type, owning, harboring or keeping an animal either permanently or
temporarily.
   FARM ANIMALS. Horses, cattle, swine, goats, sheep, poultry or other animals
commonly kept for their economic value or recreational purposes.
   HOUSEHOLDER. Any person who ordinarily exercises dominion or possession over
a residence, office, store, building, shop or other premises which is generally
deemed to be a single unit of occupancy within the city, whether as an owner,
tenant, landlord or otherwise.
   RESIDENCE. Any dwelling, abode or apartment wherein a single family
customarily resides.
   WILD ANIMALS. Any animal of a type not normally domesticated, or not
specifically mentioned elsewhere in this subchapter.
(Ord. passed 2-26-1990)
§ 90.02 PROPER CARE; HUMANE TREATMENT.
   (A)   Any person who owns, keeps or harbors any animal located within the
city, or permits an animal to enter the corporate limits of the city, shall
exercise sufficient and proper care and control over the animal at all times so
as to prevent the animal from becoming a public nuisance or otherwise violating
this subchapter.
   (B)   The custodian of any animal located within the city shall provide for
the humane treatment and care of the animal, and to include, but not limited
to, necessary shelter, food, water and medical care.
(Ord. passed 2-26-1990)
§ 90.03 DOGS AND CATS GENERALLY.
   (A)   It shall be unlawful for any custodian to possess, harbor, keep or
allow to be kept or to maintain for any purpose whatever, more than two dogs,
cats or combination thereof, over the age of 60 days at his or her residence,
business or at any other location in the city.
   (B)   Temporary permission to keep more than two dogs or cats over the age
of 60 days may be granted for periods as determined by the City Council upon
application being made thereto. The Council shall cause all such applications
to be investigated and shall not grant the temporary permission unless it is
satisfied that it shall not be a nuisance, as herein defined.
   (C)   Every female dog or cat in heat shall be kept confined in a building
or secure enclosure, or in a veterinary hospital or boarding kennel, in the
manner that the female dog or cat cannot come in contact with another animal,
except for intentional breeding purposes.
(Ord. passed 2-26-1990) Penalty, see §
90.99
§ 90.04 NUISANCE ANIMALS, BEHAVIOR.
   (A)   The owner or custodian of any animal shall prevent the animal from
committing any act which constitutes a nuisance.
   (B)   It is a nuisance for any animal to:
      (1)   Run at large;
      (2)   Frequently bark, cry or howl;
      (3)   To frequent school grounds, parks or other public places;
      (4)   To chase vehicles;
      (5)   To molest or annoy any person; and
      (6)   To damage, defile or destroy public or private property.
(Ord. passed 2-26-1990)
§ 90.05 REGISTRATION.
   (A)   No person, partnership, corporation or other entity shall keep any dog
or cat after the effective date of this subchapter unless the animal is
registered with the City Clerk. The registration shall be issued by the City
Clerk for a fee as set by City Council from time to time for each dog or cat.
Each animal need be registered one time only by each owner. The registration
shall not be transferable.
   (B)   It shall be the duty of each owner of a dog or cat to pay the
registration fee to the City Clerk within 60 days after the enactment of this
subchapter, or upon acquiring ownership or possession of any unlicensed dog or
cat or upon establishing residence in the city or upon a dog or cat reaching
the age of 63 days. The City Clerk shall cause a notice of the necessity of
paying such a registration fee to be printed in the official newspaper of the
city one time prior to March 1 each year. In the event that payment of the
registration fee due as stated herein is not received by the due date, a
penalty as set by City Council from time to time shall be added to the
registration fee. The penalty shall be collected at the time the registration
fee is paid. The owner of a dog or cat newly acquired or a new resident
establishing a residence within the city shall not be subject to the penalty
for 60 days.
   (C)   (1)   Upon payment of the registration fee, the City Clerk shall
execute a receipt in duplicate.
      (2)   The Clerk shall deliver the original to the person who pays the
fee, retaining the duplicate.
      (3)   The Clerk shall also procure a sufficient number of suitable
metallic tags, and shall deliver one appropriate tag to the owner upon payment
of the fee.
   (D)   The owner shall cause the tag to be affixed by a permanent metal
fastening to the collar of the dog or cat so registered in a manner that the
tag may be easily seen by the officers of the city. The owner shall see that
the tag is constantly worn by the dog or cat.
   (E)   In case any tag is lost, a duplicate may be issued by the City Clerk
upon presentation of a receipt showing payment of the current registration. A
charge set by City Council from time to time shall be made for each such
duplicate issued.
   (F)   Any dog or cat found unlicensed, running at large or found in
violation of any provision of this subchapter may be impounded by the Animal
Control Warden, who shall give notice of the impounding as required in this
section.
(Ord. passed 2-26-1990) Penalty, see §
90.99
§ 90.06 POUND; ANIMAL CONTROL WARDEN; RECLAIMING ANIMALS.
   (A)   (1)   The City Council may, by resolution, establish a pound and
appoint an Animal Control Warden, whose duties shall include the enforcement of
the provisions of this subchapter. The City Council may contract with the
Animal Control Warden for the collection, keeping, redemption or disposal of
any animal when taken into custody in accordance with this subchapter. The
Animal Control Warden or authorized agent shall be empowered to take into
custody and impound any animal reasonably believed to be stray or abandoned or
otherwise in violation of this subchapter.
      (2)   Upon taking up and impounding any dog or cat, the Animal Control
Warden shall forthwith give notice of the impoundment in the following manner.
         (a)   If the animal is registered with the City Clerk, the person who
is listed as the owner of the animal shall be personally notified, if possible,
by either the Animal Control Warden or a representative of the City Clerk’s
office.
         (b)   If the animal impounded is not registered, or the owner cannot
be reasonably notified, notice of the impoundment shall be made by a single
publication in the official newspaper of the city. The notice shall state the
following:
            1.   Date of impoundment;
            2.   Description of the animal;
            3.   Time and date by which the animal must be picked up;
               4.   The animal will be destroyed or disposed of if not picked
up by the time stated; and
            5.   Location where the animal may be picked up.
      (3)   No animal shall be disposed of or destroyed until at least five
days after the notice of impoundment is published. It shall be the duty of the
Animal Control Warden to dispose of each animal not claimed in a humane manner.
      (4)   No unspayed female cat which has been impounded shall be allowed to
be adopted from the pound unless the prospective owner shall agree to have the
animal spayed.
      (5)   Any dog or cat impounded hereunder may be reclaimed upon payment by
the owner to the City Clerk of a sum established by the City Council by
resolution. The owner must also comply with the registration provisions hereof.
   (B)   The following fees shall be levied for the reclaiming of any animal
from the city’s animal impound facility: as set by City Council from time to
time.
(Ord. passed 2-26-1990; Res. passed 8-13-1990)
§ 90.07 FARM ANIMALS.
   (A)   Farm animals may be kept and housed only in zoned agricultural
districts.
   (B)   All facilities for the care, confinement or keeping of farm animals
must be located at least 100 feet from inhabited dwellings, and maintained in
such a manner as to prohibit foul odors as well as unhealthy or unsanitary
conditions.
(Ord. passed 2-26-1990)
§ 90.08 WILD ANIMALS.
   Wild animals may not be kept, housed or maintained in the city by any
person, partnership, corporation or other entity for any purpose whatsoever,
except by competent medical authority for veterinary care, or under permit from
the state’s Department of Natural Resources and the City Council.
(Ord. passed 2-26-1990) Penalty, see §
90.99
§ 90.09 DANGEROUS ANIMALS.
   (A)   The following proceedings shall be followed for the destruction and
safety of animals which present a danger to public health.
      (1)   A sworn complaint shall be presented to the county’s District Court
stating the reason why the animal presents a danger to public health and
safety. The complaint shall be based on one or more of the following factors:
         (a)   The animal at any time has destroyed property or habitually
trespasses in a damaging manner on the property of persons other than the
owner;
         (b)   The animal at any time has attacked or bitten a person outside
the owner’s or custodian’s premises;
         (c)   The animal is vicious or shows vicious habits or molests
pedestrians or interferes with vehicles on the public streets; and/or
         (d)   The animal is a public nuisance as heretofore defined.
      (2)   A summons shall be directed to the owner of the animal commanding
him to appear before the Court to show cause why the animal should not be
seized by any police officer, Animal Control Warden or otherwise disposed of in
the manner authorized in this part. The summons shall be returnable not less
than two, nor more than six, days from the date thereof and shall be served at
least two days before the time of the scheduled appearance. Upon the hearing
and finding the facts true as complained of, the Court may either order the
owner or custodian to remove it from the city, or may order the owner or
custodian to keep it confined to a designated place. If the owner or custodian
violates the order, any police officer or Animal Control Warden may impound the
animal described in the order and destroy the animal forthwith. The provisions
of this section are in addition to and supplemental to other provisions of this
part. Costs of the proceedings specified by this section shall be assessed
against the owner or custodian of the animal if the facts in the complaint are
found to be true, or to the complainant, if the facts are found to be untrue.
   (B)   Any animal which bites a person shall be quarantined in the location
and for such time as may be directed by the city’s Health Officer. During
quarantine, the animal shall be securely confined and kept from contact with
any other animal. At the discretion of the Health Officer, the quarantine may
be on the premises of the owner. However, if the Health Officer requires other
confinement, the owner shall surrender the animal for the quarantine period to
an animal shelter or veterinary hospital. Any expenses incurred by the owner of
the animal in fulfilling the requirements of this section, including the Health
Officer’s fee if any, shall be borne by the animal owner.
   (C)   Whenever the city’s Health Officer determines that the prevalence of
rabies renders the action necessary to protect the public health and safety,
the Council may, by resolution, issue a proclamation ordering every person
owning or keeping a dog or cat to muzzle it securely so that it cannot bite. No
person shall violate the proclamation and any unmuzzled dog or cat during the
time fixed in the proclamation shall be subject to impoundment as heretofore
provided, and the owner of the dog or cat shall be subject to the penalty
hereinafter provided.
(Ord. passed 2-26-1990) Penalty, see §
90.99
§ 90.10 INVESTIGATION.
   For the purpose of discharging the duties imposed by this subchapter and to
enforce its provisions, any agent of the Animal Control Warden or any police
officer is empowered to enter upon any premises upon which an animal is kept or
harbored and to demand the exhibition by the owner of the animal or the license
for the animal. With or without permission, any agent of the Animal Control
Warden or police officer may enter the premises where any animal is kept in a
reported cruel or inhumane manner and demand to inspect the animal. The Animal
Control Warden or police officer may order a veterinary inspection of the
animal or animals and may, upon recommendation of the veterinary doctor, order
the medical care as may be necessary. The cost of the veterinary care is to be
paid by the animal’s owner.
(Ord. passed 2-26-1990)
§ 90.11 INTERFERENCE.
   It shall be unlawful for any person to break open the city kennel or to
attempt to do so, or to take or let any animal therefrom or to take or attempt
to take from any officer any animal taken up by him or her in compliance with
this subchapter, or in any manner to interfere with or hinder the officer in
the discharge of his or her duties under this subchapter.
(Ord. passed 2-26-1990) Penalty, see §
90.99
DEER FEEDING
§ 90.25 FINDINGS.
   (A)   Recreational feeding of deer often causes deer concentrations which
develop into depredation and other public safety problems.
   (B)   Depredation of garden crops and landscaping plants is increasing as
deer habitat decreases and deer populations increase within particular areas
and neighborhoods in the city.
   (C)   High deer populations can result in increased car/deer crashes within
the community, causing public safety concerns and negative economic impacts
such as increased car insurance premium costs.
   (D)   High deer densities also contribute to the possible spread of chronic
diseases among deer populations and Chronic Wasting Disease.
(Ord. passed 8-12-2013)
§ 90.26 PURPOSE AND INTENT.
   The intent of the City Council is not to unreasonably infringe upon the
recreational enjoyment of city residents who observe wildlife and enhance
wildlife habitat on their property. The purpose of this artificial feeding ban
is to eliminate these deer attractions which, when combined with other
management strategies, should reduce over time the depredation impacts to
adjacent residents, as well as assure the safety of the traveling public on
city streets and thoroughfares.
(Ord. passed 8-12-2013)
§ 90.27 PROHIBITION.
   (A)   It shall be unlawful for any person or group to place or permit to be
placed on the ground, or within five feet of the ground surface, either on
property owned or controlled by the person or on property owned by others, any
grain, fodder, fruit, vegetables, nuts, hay or other edible materials, which
may reasonably be expected to intentionally result in deer feeding, unless the
items are screened or protected in a manner that prevents deer from feeding on
them.
   (B)   Also prohibited are salt licks and powder or liquid deer attractants,
unless legally used for deer management control purposes. Living fruit trees
and other live vegetation shall not be considered as deer feeding.
(Ord. passed 8-12-2013) Penalty, see §
90.99
§ 90.28 EXCEPTIONS.
   This prohibition shall not apply to:
   (A)   Veterinarians, city police officers or county, state or federal game
officials, who are in the course of their duties, have deer in custody or under
their management;
   (B)   Persons authorized by the city to implement the deer management
program approved by the City Council;
   (C)   Any food placed upon the property for purposes of trapping or
otherwise taking deer where the trapping or taking is pursuant to a permit
issued by the state’s Department of Natural Resources;
   (D)   Persons feeding birds using self-enclosed feeding devices or
containers;
   (E)   The use of straw, hay or straw-related materials for erosion control,
mulching, gardening or other landscape purposes; and/or
   (F)   Persons on property with a livestock operation not intentionally
feeding deer. For the purposes of this section, LIVESTOCK OPERATION means an
operation commonly associated with the keeping of animals, including but not
limited to, the keeping of chickens raised for home use.
(Ord. passed 8-12-2013)
§ 90.29 INVESTIGATION AND ENFORCEMENT.
   For the purpose of discharging the duties imposed by this subchapter and to
enforce its provisions, any Police Department officer, other law enforcement
service or agent of the city with prior approval of the City Council is
empowered to enter upon any premises to investigate or inspect the premises for
prohibited deer feeding activities to eliminate the public nuisance. This
section may be enforced by injunction, action for abatement or other
appropriate civil remedy to eliminate a public nuisance.
(Ord. passed 8-12-2013)
URBAN CHICKENS
§ 90.40 DEFINITIONS.
   For the purpose of this subchapter, the following definitions shall apply
unless the context clearly requires a different meaning.
   BROODING. The period of chicken growth when supplemental heat must be
provided, due to the bird’s inability to generate enough body heat.
   CHICKEN. A domesticated bird that serves as a source of eggs or meat.
   COOP. The structure for the keeping or housing of chickens permitted by this
subchapter.
   EXERCISE YARD. A larger fenced area that provides space for exercise and
foraging for the birds when supervised.
   HEN. A female chicken.
   OFFICER. Any person designated by the city as an enforcement officer.
   POULTRY. Domesticated birds that serve as a source of eggs or meat and that
include among commercially important kinds, chickens, turkeys, ducks, geese,
peafowl, pigeons, pheasants and others.
   ROOSTER. A male chicken.
   RUN. A fully enclosed and covered area attached to a coop where the chickens
can roam unsupervised.
(Ord. passed 5-14-2012)
§ 90.41 PURPOSE.
   It is recognized that the ability to cultivate one’s own food is a
sustainable activity that can also be a rewarding past time. Therefore, it is
the purpose and intent of this subchapter to permit the keeping and maintenance
of chicken hens for egg and meat sources in a clean and sanitary manner that is
not a nuisance to or detrimental to the public health, safety and welfare of
the community, the keeping of other poultry is prohibited.
(Ord. passed 5-14-2012)
§ 90.42 INVESTIGATION AND ENFORCEMENT.
   (A)   Officers designated by the City Council shall have authority in the
investigation and enforcement of this subchapter, and no person shall interfere
with, hinder or molest any such officer in the exercise of the powers. The
officer shall make investigations as is necessary and may grant, deny or refuse
to renew any application for permit or terminate an existing permit under this
subchapter.
   (B)   Limitations for each single dwelling residential unit are as follows.
      (1)   No more than five chicken hens shall be housed or kept on any one
residential lot in any area of the city zoned R-1, R-2, R-3, PUD, AG with a
permit as outlined below.
      (2)   Roosters are prohibited.
      (3)   Slaughtering of chickens in the city limits is prohibited except at
a licensed processing facility.
      (4)   Leg banding of all chickens is required. The bands must identify
the owner and the owner’s address and telephone number.
      (5)   A separate coop is required to house the chickens. Coops must be
constructed and maintained to meet the following minimum standards:
         (a)   Located in the rear or side yard;
         (b)   Setback at least five feet from the rear or side property lines;
         (c)   Interior floor space: four square feet per bird;
         (d)   Interior height: four to six feet to allow access for cleaning
and maintenance;
         (e)   Doors: one standard door to allow humans to access the coop and
one for birds (if above ground level, must also provide a stable ramp);
         (f)   Windows: one square foot window per ten square feet floor space.
Windows must be able to open for ventilation;
         (g)   Climate control: adequate ventilation and/or insulation to
maintain the coop temperature between 32° to 85°F;
         (h)   Nest boxes: one box per every three hens;
         (i)   Roosts: one and one-half inch diameter or greater, located 18
inches from the wall and two to three inches above the floor;
         (j)   Rodent-proof: coop construction and materials must be adequate
to prevent access by rodents; and
         (k)   Coops shall be constructed and maintained in a workmanlike
manner.
      (6)   A run or exercise yard is required.
         (a)   Runs must be constructed and maintained to meet the following
minimum standards:
            1.   Location: rear or side yard;
            2.   Size: ten square feet per bird, if access to a fenced exercise
yard is also available; 16 square feet per bird, if access to an exercise yard
is not available. If the coop is elevated two feet so the hens can access the
space beneath, that area may count as a portion of the minimum run footprint;
            3.   Height: four to six feet in height to allow access for
cleaning and maintenance;
            4.   Gate: one gate to allow human access to the run;
            5.   Cover: adequate to keep hens in and predators out; and
            6.   Substrate: composed of material that can be easily raked or
regularly replace to reduce odor and flies.
         (b)   Exercise yards must be fenced and is required if the run does
not provide at least 16 square feet per bird. Exercise yards must provide a
minimum of 174 square feet per chicken.
      (7)   Chickens must not be housed in a residential house or an attached
or detached garage, except for brooding purposes only.
      (8)   All premises on which chicken hens are kept or maintained shall be
kept clean from filth, garbage and any substances which attract rodents. The
coop and its surrounding must be cleaned frequently enough to control odor.
Manure shall not be allowed to accumulate in a way that causes an unsanitary
condition or causes odors detectible on another property. Failure to comply
with these conditions may result in the officer removing chickens from the
premises or revoking a chicken permit.
      (9)   All grain and food stored for the use of the hens on a premise with
a chicken permit shall be kept in a rodent-proof container.
      (10)   Chicken hens shall not be kept in such a manner as to constitute a
nuisance to the occupants of adjacent property.
      (11)   Dead chickens must be disposed of according to the state’s Board
of Animal Health rules which require chicken carcasses to be disposed of as
soon as possible after death, usually within 48 to 72 hours. Legal forms of
chicken carcass disposal include offsite burial, off-site incineration or
rendering or off-site composting.
(Ord. passed 5-14-2012) Penalty, see §
90.99
§ 90.43 PERMIT REQUIRED; APPLICATION.
   (A)   (1)   No person shall own, keep, harbor or have custody of any live
chicken without first obtaining a permit in writing from the city.
      (2)   The city shall grant a permit for chicken hens after the applicant
has sought the written consent of 100% of the owners or occupants of privately-
or publicly-owned real estate that are located adjacent (i.e., sharing property
lines) on the outer boundaries of the premises for which the permit is being
requested or, in the alternative, proof that the applicant’s property lines are
150 feet or more from any house.
      (3)   Where an adjacent property consists of a multiple dwelling or
multi-tenant property, the applicant need obtain only the written consent of
the owner or manager, or other person in charge of the building. The written
consent shall be required on the initial application and as often thereafter as
the officer deems necessary.
   (B)   Any person desiring a permit required under the provisions of this
subchapter shall make written application to the City Clerk upon a form
prescribed by and containing the information as required by the City Clerk and
officer. Among other things, the application shall contain the following
information:
      (1)   A description of the real property upon which it is desired to keep
the chickens;
      (2)   The breed and number of chickens to be maintained on the premises;
      (3)   A site plan of the property showing the location and size of the
proposed chicken coop and run, setbacks from the chicken coop to property lines
and surrounding buildings (including houses and buildings on adjacent lots),
and the location, style and height of fencing proposed to contain the chickens
in a run or exercise area. Portable coops and cages are allowed, but portable
locations must be included with the site plan;
      (4)   Statements that the applicant will, at all times, keep the chickens
in accordance with all of the conditions prescribed by the officer, or
modification thereof, and that failure to obey the conditions will constitute a
violation of the provisions of this chapter and grounds for cancellation of the
permit; and
      (5)   Such other and further information as may be required by the
officer.
(Ord. passed 5-14-2012)
§ 90.44 PERMIT REQUIREMENTS.
   (A)   If granted, the permit shall be issued by the City Clerk and officer
and shall state the conditions, if any, imposed upon the permitted for the
keeping of chickens under this permit.
   (B)   The permit shall specify the restrictions, limitations, conditions and
prohibitions which the officer deems reasonably necessary to protect any person
or neighboring use from unsanitary conditions, unreasonable noise or odors, or
annoyance, or to protect the public health and safety. The permit may be
modified from time to time or revoked by the officer for failure to conform to
the restrictions, limitations, prohibitions. The modification or revocation
shall be effective after ten days following the mailing of written notice
thereof by certified mail to the person or persons keeping or maintain the
chickens.
   (C)   The granting of a permit under this subchapter does not entitle the
permit holder to any other rights as may be granted to farmers or commercial
entities relating to equipment used in the course of conducting animal
husbandry business.
(Ord. passed 5-14-2012)
§ 90.45 FEES; PERMIT ISSUANCE.
   For each residential site, the fee for a permit is as may be imposed, set,
established and fixed by the City Council, by resolution, from time to time.
(Ord. passed 5-14-2012)
§ 90.46 PERMIT TERM.
   The permit period under this subchapter shall expire one year from the date
the permit is issued. Permits must be renewed on an annual basis.
(Ord. passed 5-14-2012)
§ 90.47 PERMIT REVOCATION.
   The city may revoke any permit issued under this subchapter if the person
holding the permit refuses or fails to comply with this subchapter, with any
regulations promulgated by the City Council pursuant to this subchapter, or
with any state or local law governing cruelty to animals or the keeping of
animals. Any person whose permit is revoked shall, within ten days thereafter,
humanely dispose of all chickens being owned, kept or harbored by the person
and no part of the permit fee shall be refunded.
(Ord. passed 5-14-2012)
§ 90.99 PENALTY.
   (A)   Any person violating any provision of this chapter for which no
specific penalty is prescribed shall be subject to §
10.99 of this code of ordinances.
   (B)   Any person, partnership, corporation or other entity violating any
provision of §§
90.01 through
90.11 of this chapter shall be deemed guilty of a misdemeanor. Each day’s
violation shall be a separate offense.
   (C)   Notwithstanding anything in city ordinances to the contrary, a person
who violates any provisions of §§
90.25 through
90.29 of this chapter shall be guilty of a petty misdemeanor punishable
according to petty misdemeanor limits as established from time to time by the
state. The first violation shall result in a written warning. A subsequent
violation shall result in a citation for the petty misdemeanor offense. Each
act of violation and each day a violation occurs or continues is a separate
offense. In all cases, the city shall be entitled to collect the cost of
investigation.
   (D)   (1)   Any person violating any of §§
90.40 through
90.47 of this chapter shall be deemed guilty of a petty misdemeanor.
      (2)   If any person is found guilty by a court for violation of this
division (D), their permit to own, keep, harbor or have custody of chickens
shall be deemed automatically revoked and no new permit may be issued for a
period of one year.
      (3)   Any person violating any conditions of this permit shall reimburse
the city for all costs borne by the city to enforce the conditions of the
permit including, but not limited to, the pickup and impounding of chickens.
(Ord. passed 2-26-1990; Ord. passed 5-14-2012; Ord. passed 8-12-2013)

GENERAL PROVISIONS
§ 91.01 SOLID FUELS; EXTERNAL OUTDOOR WOOD-BURNING STOVES AND FUEL- FIRED
HEATING DEVICES.
   (A)   Definitions. For the purpose of this section, the following
definitions shall apply unless the context clearly requires a different
meaning.
      EXTERNAL SOLID FUEL-FIRED HEATING DEVICE.
         (a)   A device designed for solid fuel combustion so that usable heat
is derived for the interior of a building, and includes solid fuel-fired
stoves, solid fuel-fired cooking stoves, combination fuel furnaces or boilers
which bum solid fuel and outdoor wood burning stoves or units.
         (b)   SOLID FUEL-FIRE HEATING DEVICES do not include natural gas-fired
fireplace logs or wood-burning fireplaces or wood stoves in the interior of a
dwelling.
      PERSON. An individual, partnership, corporation, company or other
association.
      STACKS or CHIMNEY. Any vertical structure incorporated into a building,
or upon an outdoor solid fuel-fired heating device, and enclosing a flue or
flues that carry off smoke or exhaust from a solid fuel-fired heating device;
especially that part of such a structure extending above a roof.
   (B)   Requirements. No outdoor wood-burning stoves or solid fuel-fired
heating devices designed and intended, and/or used, for the purpose of heating
the principal structure or another accessory structure on the premises are
allowed to be installed within the city.
   (C)   Non-conforming use.
      (1)   The lawful use of any existing wood-burning unit or solid fuel-
fired heating device existing at the time of the effective date of this section
may be continued, although the use may not conform to the provision of this
section.
      (2)   No pre-existing, non-conforming wood-burning unit or solid fuel-
fired heating device shall hereafter be extended, enlarged or expanded.
      (3)   At such time as the useful life of a non-conforming wood-burning
unit or solid fuel-fired heating device has elapsed or would need to be
repaired to function properly, the unit cannot be replaced and must be
abandoned, not used and removed from the property immediately.
   (D)   Officers and enforcement. The City Building Inspector, Zoning
Administrator, Fire Chief, Police Chief and/or their designees, or designated
officials, shall enforce the provisions of this section.
(Ord. passed 1-28-2008) Penalty, see §
91.99
§ 91.02 OPEN BURNING.
   (A)   Adoption by reference of state statutes. Except as herein specifically
addressed or modified, the following provisions of state law are hereby adopted
by reference: M.S. §§ 88.01 to 88.195, as they may be amended from time to
time.
   (B)   Definitions. For the purpose of this section, the following
definitions shall apply unless the context clearly requires a different
meaning.
      BUILDING MATERIAL. Lumber, wood shakes and other wood products, but shall
not include composite shingles, tar paper, insulation, wall board, wiring or
other similar smoke producing materials.
      CAMPFIRE. A fire set for cooking, warming or ceremonial purposes, which
is not more than three feet in diameter by three feet high, and has had the
ground five feet from the base of the fire cleared of all combustible material.
      COOKING FIRE. A fire contained in -a charcoal grill, camp stove or other
device designed for the purpose of cooking.
      DEMOLITION DEBRIS. Burnable building material generated from demolition
of buildings or structures.
      GARBAGE. Discarded material resulting from the handling, processing,
storage, preparation, serving and consumption of food.
      HAZARDOUS WASTES. No person shall conduct, cause, or permit open burning
of hazardous waste, as defined in state law, and applicable Commissioners
rules.
      HEATING DEVICES. Furnaces or another devices which burn fuel oil, coal,
natural gas, propane, wood or used petroleum products and which conform to all
local, state and federal environmental and safety regulations, intended to
provide heat.
      OPEN BURNING. The burning of any matter which is not contained within a
fully enclosed, fire box or container, and whereby the resulting combustion
products are emitted directly to the atmosphere without passing through a
stack, duct or chimney.
      PROHIBITED MATERIALS. Rubber, plastics, chemically-treated materials or
other materials which produce excessive or noxious smoke including, but not
limited to, tires, railroad ties, chemically treated lumber, composite
shingles, tar paper, insulation, composition board, sheetrock, wiring, paint,
paint filters and oil.
      RUBBISH. Non-putrescible solid waste, such as paper, cardboard, yard
clippings and other natural matter not including garbage.
      SOLID WASTE. Garbage, refuse, sludge from a water supply treatment plant
or air containment treatment facility, and other discarded waste materials and
sludges, in solid, semisolid, liquid or contained, gaseous form, resulting from
industrial, commercial, mining and agricultural operations and from community
activities.
      WILDFIRE. A fire requiring suppression action, burning any forest, brush,
grassland, cropland or any other vegetative material.
   (C)   Restrictions on burning. No person shall cause, allow or permit open
burning of any prohibited materials, hazardous wastes, solid wastes, demolition
debris, motor vehicles, building materials, garbage or rubbish within the city.
   (D)   Exceptions.
      (1)   Open burning is allowed for the following exceptions only,
providing a permit has been issued by the Fire Warden:
         (a)   Campfires and/or cooking fires located within the city, where
fire rings or cooking devices designed for the purpose of cooking or heating
are used;
         (b)   The Rushford Park System shall be inspected annually by the Fire
Chief or the Chief’s designee for use by campers and visitors. Individual
campers will not require separate permits;
         (c)   Ceremonial fires conducted by Scouts, Service Clubs and school
personnel;
         (d)   Ground thawing for utility repair, underground utility
construction and preparation of burial grounds;
         (e)   Bona fide instruction and training of firefighting personnel and
for the testing of fire- extinguishing equipment;
         (f)   Fires set for the elimination of a fire hazard which cannot be
abated by another practicable means; and
         (g)   The burning by city staff of trees, brush, grass and other
vegetative matter in the maintenance of municipal property.
      (2)   Exemption to conduct fires under this section does not excuse a
person from the consequences, damages or injuries which may result therefrom,
nor does it exempt any person from regulations promulgated by the State
Pollution Control Agency, State Department of Natural Resources or any other
governmental unit exercising jurisdiction in matters of pollution or fire
hazard regulations.
   (E)   Permits. The city’s Fire Chief, or a person designated by the Fire
Chief, shall be responsible to issue burning permits. No permit for a fire
issued by any person or agency other than the Fire Chief or the Chief’s
designee shall be valid.
      (1)   Permit form and fee. Application and permit forms shall be prepared
by the Fire Chief. Fees for fire permits may be set from time to time by
resolution of the City Council, but where a particular fire will, in the
discretion and judgment of the Fire Chief, require supervision by Fire
Department personnel, the standard rate of an amount set by City Council from
time to time per hour will be charged to help defray the city’s actual expense
of such supervision.
      (2)   Length of permit. A permit, once issued, shall be for such period
of time as is stated on the permit.
      (3)   Extension of permit. The Fire Chief may grant up to one extension
of a permit, each for a time span not longer than the duration of the initial
permit, without requiring an additional permit fee.
      (4)   Type of permit. The permit must set the conditions for starting and
burning the fire and must also list all the materials which may be burned under
the permit.
      (5)   Permit holder.
         (a)   The permittee must have the permit on his or her person at all
times and must remain with the fire until it is completely extinguished.
         (b)   The permit must be given to a Forest Officer, Fire Warden or
other peace officer when requested for inspection.
      (6)   Site of permit. No person may start or cause a fire to be started
on any land that is not owned by him, her or them or under his, her or their
legal control without the written permission of the owner or lessee of the
land, even if he, she or they have obtained a permit from the Fire Warden or
other authority.
(Ord. passed 8-26-1996) Penalty, see §
91.99
FIRE CODE

   (B)   (1)   The Chief of the Fire Department may detail the members of the
Fire Department as inspectors as shall from time to time be necessary. The

      (2)   The examination shall be open to members and non-members of the

indefinite term with removal only for cause.

   (B)   Wherever the term “corporation counsel” is used in the Minnesota

storage of liquefied petroleum gas is restricted, are hereby established by
City Council from time to time.

§ 91.19 EXPLOSIVES AND BLASTING AGENTS; STORAGE; LIMITS.
   The limits referred to in the state’s Uniform Fire Code, in which storage of
explosives and blasting agents is prohibited, are hereby established by City
Council from time to time.
(Ord. passed 4-4-1977)
§ 91.20 APPEALS.
   Whenever the Chief shall disapprove an application or refuse to grant a
permit applied for, or when it is claimed that the provisions of the Code do
not apply or that the true intent and meaning of the Code have been
misconstrued or wrongly interpreted, the applicant may appeal from the decision
of the Chief to the City Council within 30 days from the date of the decision
appealed.
(Ord. passed 4-4-1977)
§ 91.21 PERMITS; NEW MATERIALS, PROCESSES OR OCCUPANCIES.
   The City Clerk, Zoning Administrator and the Chief shall act as a committee
to determine and specify, after giving affected persons an opportunity to be
heard, any new materials, processes or occupancies, which shall require
permits, in addition to those now enumerated in the Code. The Chief shall
publish a notice in the legal newspaper of the city prior to each meeting of
the committee and distribute copies thereof to interested persons.
(Ord. passed 4-4-1977)
§ 91.99 PENALTY.
   (A)   Any person violating any provision of this chapter for which no
specific penalty is prescribed shall be subject to §
10.99 of this code of ordinances.
   (B)   Any person violating the provisions of §
91.01 of this chapter shall be guilty of a misdemeanor.
   (C)   Any person violating any provision of §
91.02 of this chapter is guilty of a misdemeanor and, upon conviction, shall be
sentenced to a fine and/or imprisonment in accordance with law.
   (D)   (1)   Any person who shall violate any of the provisions of §§
91.15 through
91.21 of this chapter hereby adopted or fail to comply therewith, or who shall
violate or fail to comply with any order made thereunder, or who shall build in
violation of any detailed statement or specifications or plans submitted and
approved thereunder, or any certificate or permit issued thereunder, and from
which no appeal has been taken, or who shall fail to comply with such an order
as affirmed or modified by the City Council or by a court of competent
jurisdiction, within the time fixed herein, shall severally for each and every
such violation and non-compliance respectively, be guilty of a misdemeanor,
punishable by a fine as set by City Council from time to time and by
imprisonment for a period not exceeding 90 days. The imposition of one penalty
for any violation shall not excuse the violation or permit it to continue; and
all such persons shall be required to correct or remedy the violations or
defects within a reasonable time; and, when not otherwise specified, each day
that prohibited conditions are maintained shall constitute a separate offense.
      (2)   The application of the above penalty shall not be held to prevent
the enforced removal of prohibited conditions.
(Ord. passed 4-4-1977; Ord. passed 8-26-1996; Ord. passed 1-28-2008)
CHAPTER 92: CLEAN INDOOR AIR
Section
   
92.01   Title
   
92.02   Purpose and findings
   
92.03   Authority and jurisdiction
   
92.04   Definitions
   
92.05   Smoking prohibited
   
92.06   Exceptions
   
92.07   Proprietors and persons in charge; responsibilities; retaliation
   
92.08   State law compliance and complement
 
   
92.99   Penalty
Cross-reference:
   Tobacco Regulations, see
Chapter 111
§ 92.01 TITLE.
   The title of this chapter shall be the “City of Rushford Clean Indoor Air
Ordinance”.
(Ord. passed 11-12-2019)
§ 92.02 PURPOSE AND FINDINGS.
   (A)   Smoke from commercial tobacco is a leading cause of disease in
nonsmokers and a major source of indoor air pollution. There is no safe level
of exposure to commercial tobacco smoke. Secondhand smoke causes heart disease,
lung cancer, respiratory infections, decreased respiratory function, low birth-
weight or pre-term deliveries and other health problems. Neither the separation
of smokers and non-smokers, nor the introduction of new ventilation systems,
can eliminate the health hazards caused by secondhand smoke.
   (B)   Electronic delivery devices, more commonly referred to as electronic
cigarettes or e-cigarettes, closely resemble and purposefully mimic the act of
smoking as users inhale aerosolized liquid nicotine and other substances heated
by an electronic ignition system. Their use in workplaces and public places
where smoking is prohibited creates concern and confusion and leads to
difficulties in enforcing smoking prohibitions.
   (C)   Scientific research has shown negative health effects related to
secondhand exposure to e-cigarette aerosol. In 2014, the American Society for
Heating, Refrigeration and Air-Conditioning Engineering published a risk
assessment of electronic delivery devices and concluded that “e-cigarettes emit
harmful chemicals into the air and need to be regulated in the same manner as
tobacco smoking”. They can contain nicotine and detectable levels of known
carcinogens and toxic chemicals, like nitrosamines and diethylene glycol, a
toxic chemical used in antifreeze. In December 2016, the Surgeon General
released a comprehensive review of the public health impact of e-cigarettes on
U.S. youth and young adults, finding, in pail, that the nicotine contained in
e-cigarettes can cause addiction and can harm the developing adolescent brain.
In Minnesota, a 2017 Youth Tobacco Survey by the Minnesota Department of Health
revealed that 46.2% of non-smoking youth had been exposed to secondhand smoke
over the past seven days.
   (D)   Restricting smoking in places of work and public places can help to
reduce youth exposure to secondhand smoke and model smoke-free behavior.
   (E)   The only way to completely protect workers and the public from the
health threats of secondhand emissions from electronic cigarettes and other
tobacco products is to create 100% smoke-free workplaces and public places.
   (F)   Minnesota home rule and charter cities have the power to adopt
ordinances that are more stringent than the state’s Clean Indoor Air Act, being
M.S. §§ 144.411 to 144.417, as they may be amended from time to time, to
protect individuals from secondhand smoke under M.S. §§ 144.414, subd. 5(b),
and 144.417, subd. 4, as they may be amended from time to time.
   (G)   Accordingly, the City Council finds and declares that the purpose of
this chapter is to:
      (1)   Protect the health, safety and welfare of the people of the city
from the adverse effects of secondhand smoke and aerosol; and
      (2)   Affirm that the right to breathe clean air has priority over
allowing people to smoke or use electronic delivery devices.
(Ord. passed 11-12-2019)
§ 92.03 AUTHORITY AND JURISDICTION.
   Pursuant to M.S. §§ 144.414, subd. 5(b), and 144.417, subd. 4, as they may
be amended from time to time, this chapter shall apply throughout the city.
(Ord. passed 11-12-2019)
§ 92.04 DEFINITIONS.
   For the purpose of this chapter, the following definitions shall apply
unless the context clearly requires a different meaning.
   CITY FACILITIES. All enclosed areas, including buildings and vehicles,
owned, leased or operated by the city; all outdoor property adjacent to the
buildings and under the control of the city, including lawns, courtyards and
green space; and other areas open to the public for recreational purposes under
control of the city, such as fairgrounds athletic fields, beaches, gardens,
parks, plazas, skate parks, swimming pools, trails and outdoor picnic areas.
CITY FACILITIES do not include streets and sidewalks used only as pedestrian or
vehicular thoroughfares, unless otherwise prohibited by this section or other
law.
   ELECTRONIC DELIVERY DEVICE. Any product containing or delivering nicotine,
lobelia or any other substance intended for human consumption through the
inhalation of aerosol or vapor from the product. ELECTRONIC DELIVERY DEVICE
includes, but is not limited to, devices manufactured, marketed or sold as e-
cigarettes, e-cigars, e-pipes, vape pens, mods, tank systems or under any other
product name or descriptor. ELECTRONIC DELIVERY DEVICE includes any component
part of a product, whether or not marketed or sold separately.
   INDOOR AREA. All space between a floor and a ceiling that is bounded by
walls, doorways or windows, whether opened or closed, covering more that 50% of
the combined surface area of the vertical planes constituting the perimeter of
the area. A wall includes a retractable divider, garage door or other physical
barrier, whether temporary or permanent. A standard (0.011) gauge window screen
with an 18 by 16 mesh count is not considered a WALL.
   MINNESOTA CLEAN INDOOR AIR ACT. M.S. §§ 144.411 through 144.417, as they may
be amended from time to time.
   PLACE OF EMPLOYMENT. Any indoor area where one or more individuals perform
any type of service for consideration of payment under any type of contractual
relationship, including, but not limited to, an employment relationship with or
for a private corporation, partnership, individual or government office. This
term includes any location where one or more individuals gratuitously perform
services for which individuals are ordinarily paid.
   PROPRIETOR or PERSON IN CHARGE. The person or entity that ultimately
controls, governs or directs the activities within the public place, place of
employment or city facility regardless of whether the person or entity is owner
or lessee of the public place, place of employment or city facility. The term
may apply to a corporation as well as an individual.
   PUBLIC PLACE. Any indoor area used by the general public, including, but not
limited to, restaurants, bars, any other food or liquor establishment, retail
stores and other commercial establishments, schools and other educational
facilities, hospitals, nursing homes, auditoriums, arenas, meeting rooms and
common areas of rental apartment and condominium buildings.
   SMOKE or SMOKING. The inhaling, exhaling, burning or carrying of any lighted
or heated cigar, cigarette, pipe or any other lighted or heated product,
whether natural or synthetic, containing, made, or derived from nicotine,
tobacco or other plant, that is intended for inhalation. SMOKING shall include
carrying or using an activated or turned on electronic delivery device.
(Ord. passed 11-12-2019)
§ 92.05 SMOKING PROHIBITED.
   No person shall smoke:
   (A)   In public places or places of employment;
   (B)   Within 25 feet of entrances, exits, windows and ventilation intakes of
public places and places of employment; or
   (C)   In or on all city facilities.
(Ord. passed 11-12-2019) Penalty, see §
91.99
§ 92.06 EXCEPTIONS.
   Smoking of traditional, non-commercial tobacco by a Native American as part
of a traditional Native American spiritual or cultural ceremony is allowed. For
purposes of this section, a NATIVE AMERICAN is a person who is a member of an
Indian tribe, as defined in M.S. § 260.755, subd. 12, as it may be amended from
time to time.
(Ord. passed 11-12-2019)
§ 92.07 PROPRIETORS AND PERSONS IN CHARGE; RESPONSIBILITIES; RETALIATION.
   (A)   The proprietor or other person in charge that owns, leases, manages,
operates or otherwise controls the use of a public place, place of employment
or city facility shall make reasonable efforts to prevent smoking by:
      (1)   Posting appropriate signage on or immediately inside of all outside
entrances to the public place, place of employment and city facility. Signs
must be placed at an unobscured location, at a height that will be easily seen
by persons entering the establishment;
      (2)   Refusing to serve or service any person acting in violation of this
chapter;
      (3)   Ensuring that no ashtrays, lighters, matchbooks, smoking papers,
pipes (including hookahs) or similar smoking paraphernalia are provided in any
area where smoking is prohibited; and
      (4)   Asking any person who smokes in an area where smoking is prohibited
to refrain from smoking, and, if the person does not refrain from smoking after
being asked to do so, asking the person to leave. If the person refuses to
leave, the proprietor shall handle the situation consistent with lawful methods
for handling other persons acting in a disorderly manner or as a trespasser.
   (B)   No proprietor, or other person or entity in charge, of a public place,
place of employment or city facility may retaliate or take adverse action
against an employee or other person who, in good faith, reports a violation of
this chapter.
(Ord. passed 11-12-2019) Penalty, see §
91.99
§ 92.08 STATE LAW COMPLIANCE AND COMPLEMENT.
   This chapter is intended to complement and go beyond the state’s Clean
Indoor Air Act. Nothing in this chapter authorizes smoking in any location
regulated by any other laws or applicable regulations.
(Ord. passed 11-12-2019)
§ 92.99 PENALTY.
   (A)   A person of or over the age of 21 who violates any provision of this
chapter shall be guilty of a petty misdemeanor and subject to a fine as set by
City Council from time to time for the first offense. Each violation and every
day in which a violation occurs or continues, constitutes a separate offense. A
person of or over the age of 21 who violates any provision of this chapter
within one year of a previous violation may be subject to misdemeanor
prosecution. Persons under the age of 21 who violate any provision of this
chapter may only be subject to non-criminal, non-monetary civil penalties such
as tobacco-related education classes and diversion programs. The city will
consult with local public health, parents, children and other interested
parties to determine an appropriate penalty for persons under the age of 21 in
the city. The penalty may be established by ordinance and amended from time to
time.
   (B)   In addition to the penalties provided in division (A) above, the City
Attorney may bring a civil action against a proprietor or other person in
charge of a public place, place of employment or city facility to enjoin
repeated or continuing violations of this chapter.
(Ord. passed 11-12-2019)
CHAPTER 93: PARKS AND RECREATION
Section
   
93.01   Northend Campground policy
§ 93.01 NORTHEND CAMPGROUND POLICY.
   (A)   All fees are to be paid in advance. Campers are required to register
at Pam’s Corner Convenience Store (507-864-7949) during normal work hours
Monday through Sunday.
   (B)   Campers will display a copy of their registration slip at their camp
site, on their vehicle or on their tent. Checkout time is 2:00 p.m.
   (C)   The city’s Police Department will periodically check the campground to
ensure campers are registered.
   (D)   In case of an emergency, campers should call 911. In case of non-
emergency problems, campers should contact City Hall (507-864-2444) Monday
through Friday, 8:00 a.m. to 4:30 p.m. After normal work hours, campers may
call the Public Works Department cell phone (507-450-2924).
   (E)   Services available at campground include:
      (1)   Electrical, water and sewer hookups at sites 1 through 7;
      (2)   Water hydrant availability depends on weather; hydrants will be
turned off in cold weather;
      (3)   Waste water dump station at site; and
      (4)   Restroom with shower facility.
   (F)   Campers are responsible for proper disposal of trash and garbage.
Campfire rings must be used for recreational fires using wood materials; no
burning of trash or garbage is allowed.
   (G)   Campers are responsible for controlling noise that unreasonably annoys
or disturbs property owners in the area. This generally means noise must be
kept under control between 10:00 p.m. and 7:00 a.m.
   (H)   Tent Campers are allowed in designated sites only. Extra vehicles must
park on vacant areas in the westerly section of the park and not congregate on
the roadway or entrance. Each tent must pay a fee.
   (I)   Fees shall be as set by City Council from time to time.
   (J)   The campsite is laid out as follows.
CHAPTER 94: NOISE CONTROL
Section
   
94.01   General prohibition
   
94.02   Types of prohibited noise; participation
   
94.03   Enforcement
   
94.04   Noise impact statements
 
   
94.99   Penalty
§ 94.01 GENERAL PROHIBITION.
   No person shall intentionally make or cause to be made any distinctly and
loudly audible noise that unreasonably annoys, disturbs, injures or endangers
the comfort, repose, health, peace, safety or welfare of any person or
precludes his, her or their enjoyment of property or affects his, her or their
property’s value. This general prohibition is not limited by the specific
restrictions of the following sections.
[https://export.amlegal.com/media/5f96a454829628dad3923165be401283a1782260/
IMAGES/0-0-0-1322.png]
 
(Ord. passed 4-9-2001) Penalty, see §
94.99
§ 94.02 TYPES OF PROHIBITED NOISE; PARTICIPATION.
   (A)   Horns, audible signaling devices and the like. No person shall sound
any audible signaling device on any vehicle, except as a warning of danger, as
required by M.S. § 169.68, as it may be amended from time to time.
   (B)   Radios, phonographs, paging systems and the like. No person shall use
or operate or permit the use or operation of any radio receiving set, musical
instrument, phonograph, paging system, machine or other device for the
production or reproduction of sound in a distinct and loudly audible manner as
to unreasonably disturb the peace, quiet and comfort of any person nearby.
Operation of any such set, instrument, phonograph, machine or other device
between the hours of 11:00 p.m. and 7:00 a.m. in such a manner as to be plainly
audible at the property line of the structure or building in which it is
located, in the hallway or apartment adjacent, or at a distance of 50 feet if
the source is located outside a structure or building shall be prima facie
evidence of a violation of this section.
   (C)   Participation in noisy parties or gatherings. No person shall
participate in any party or other gathering of people giving rise to noise,
unreasonably disturbing the peace, quiet or repose of another person. When a
police officer determines that a gathering is creating such a noise
disturbance, the officer may order all persons present, other than the owner or
tenant of the premises where the disturbance is occurring, to disperse
immediately. No person shall refuse to leave after being ordered by a police
officer to do so. Every owner or tenant of such premises who has knowledge of
the disturbance shall make every reasonable effort to see that the disturbance
is stopped. Noise that is audible at the property line of the structure or
building in which it is located, in the hallway or apartment adjacent or at a
distance of 50 feet if the source is located outside a structure or building
shall be prima facie evidence of a violation of this section.
(Ord. passed 4-9-2001) Penalty, see §
94.99
§ 94.03 ENFORCEMENT.
   (A)   The Police Department of the city shall enforce the provisions of this
chapter. The Police Department may inspect private premises other than private
residences and shall make all reasonable efforts to prevent violations of this
chapter.
   (B)   This chapter may be enforced by injunction, action for abatement or
other appropriate civil remedy.
(Ord. passed 4-9-2001)
§ 94.04 NOISE IMPACT STATEMENTS.
   The Council may require any person applying for a change in zoning
classification or a permit or license for any structure, operation, process,
installation or alteration, or project that may be considered a potential noise
source to submit a noise impact statement on a form prescribed by the Council.
It shall evaluate each such statement and take its evaluation into account in
approving or disapproving the license or permit applied for or the zoning
change requested.
(Ord. passed 4-9-2001)
§ 94.99 PENALTY.
   Any violation of this chapter involving the operation of a motor vehicle is
a petty misdemeanor. Every person who violates any other provision of this
chapter is guilty of a misdemeanor. Each act of violation and each day a
violation occurs or continues constitutes a separate offense.
(Ord. passed 4-9-2001)
