GENERAL PROVISIONS
§ 111.01 PURPOSE AND INTENT.
   (A)   Because the city recognizes that the sale of commercial tobacco,
tobacco-related devices, electronic delivery devices and nicotine or lobelia
delivery products to persons under the age of 18 violates both state and
federal laws; and because studies, which the city accepts and adopts, have
shown that youth use of any commercial tobacco product has increased to 26.4%
in Minnesota; and because nearly 90% of people who smoke begin smoking before
they have reached the age of 18 years, and that almost no one starts smoking
after age 25; and because the CDC reports that as of 12-10-2019, there have
been 2,409 cases of e-cigarette, or vaping, product use associated lung injury
nationwide resulting in 52 deaths, three of those deaths occurring in
Minnesota; and because the Surgeon General and the FDA Commissioner has labeled
the recent uptick in youth use of electronic delivery devices as an “epidemic”
and because marketing analysis, public health research and commercial tobacco
industry documents reveal that tobacco companies have used menthol, mint,
fruit, candy and alcohol flavors as a way to target youth and young adults and
that the presence of the flavors can make it more difficult to quit; and
because studies show that youth and young adults are especially susceptible to
commercial tobacco product availability, advertising and price promotions at
tobacco retail environments; and because commercial tobacco use has been shown
to be the cause of many serious health problems which subsequently place a
financial burden on all levels of government, this chapter is intended to
regulate the sale of commercial tobacco, tobacco-related devices, electronic
delivery devices and nicotine or lobelia delivery products for the purpose of
enforcing and furthering existing laws, to protect youth and young adults
against the serious health effects associated with use and initiation, and to
further the official public policy of the state to prevent young people from
starting to smoke, as stated in M.S. § 144.391, as it may be amended from time
to time.
   (B)   In making these findings, the City Council accepts the conclusions and
recommendations of: the U.S. Surgeon General reports, E-cigarette Use Among
Youth and Young Adults (2016), The Health Consequences of Smoking - 50 Years of
Progress (2014) and Preventing Tobacco Use Among Youth and Young Adults (2012);
the Centers for Disease Control and Prevention in their studies, “Vital Signs:
Tobacco Product Use Among Middle and High School Students”
(Ord. 18.0, passed 12-16-2019)
§ 111.02 DEFINITIONS.
   Except as may otherwise be provided or clearly implied by context, all terms
are given their commonly accepted definitions. For the purpose of this chapter,
the following definitions shall apply unless the context clearly requires a
different meaning.
   CHILD-RESISTANT PACKAGING. Packaging that meets the definition set forth in
16 C.F.R. § 1700.15(b), as in effect on 1-1-2015, and was tested in accordance
with the method described in 16 C.F.R. § 1700.20, as in effect on 1-1-2015.
   CIGAR. Any roll of tobacco that is wrapped in tobacco leaf or in any other
substance containing tobacco, with or without a tip or mouthpiece, which is not
a cigarette as defined in M.S. § 297F.01, subd. 3, as may be amended from time
to time.
   COMPLIANCE CHECKS. The system the city uses to investigate and ensure that
those authorized to sell licensed products are following and complying with the
requirements of this chapter. COMPLIANCE CHECKS involve the use of persons
under the age of 21 who purchase or attempt to purchase licensed products.
COMPLIANCE CHECKS may also be conducted by the city or other units of
government for educational, research and training purposes or for investigating
or enforcing federal, state or local laws and regulations relating to licensed
products.
   ELECTRONIC DELIVERY DEVICE. Any product containing or delivering nicotine,
lobelia or any other substance, whether natural or synthetic, intended for
human consumption through the inhalation of aerosol or vapor from the product.
ELECTRONIC DELIVERY DEVICE includes, but is not limited to, devices
manufactured, marketed or sold as e-cigarettes, e- cigars, e-pipes, vape pens,
mods, tank systems or under any other product name or descriptor. ELECTRONIC
DELIVERY DEVICE includes any component part of a product, whether or not
marketed or sold separately, ELECTRONIC DELIVERY DEVICE does not include any
product that has been approved or certified by the U.S. Food and Drug
Administration for sale as a tobacco-cessation product, as a tobacco-dependence
product or for other medical purposes, and is marketed and sold for such an
approved purpose.
   FLAVORED PRODUCT. Any licensed product that contains a taste or smell, other
than the taste or smell of tobacco, that is distinguishable by an ordinary
consumer either prior to or during the consumption of the product, including,
but not limited to, any taste or smell relating to chocolate, cocoa,, vanilla,
honey, fruit or any candy, dessert, alcoholic beverage, herb or spice. A public
statement or claim, whether express or implied, made or disseminated by the
manufacturer of a licensed product, or by any person authorized or permitted by
the manufacturer to make or disseminate public statements concerning the
products, that a product has or produces a taste or smell other than a taste or
smell of tobacco, mint, menthol or wintergreen will constitute presumptive
evidence that the product is a FLAVORED PRODUCT.
   IMITATION TOBACCO PRODUCT. Any edible non-tobacco product designed to
resemble a tobacco product, or any non-edible tobacco product designed to
resemble a tobacco product and intended to be used by children as a toy.
IMITATION TOBACCO PRODUCT includes, but is not limited to, candy or chocolate
cigarettes, bubble gum cigars, shredded bubble gum resembling chewing tobacco
and shredded beef jerky in containers resembling tobacco snuff tins. IMITATION
TOBACCO PRODUCT does not include electronic delivery devices or nicotine or
lobelia delivery products.
   INDOOR AREA. All space between a floor and a ceiling that is bounded by
walls, doorways, or windows, whether open or closed, covering more than 50% of
the combined surface area of the vertical planes constituting the perimeter of
the area. A wall includes any retractable divider, garage door or other
physical barrier, whether temporary or permanent. A standard window screen
(0.011 gauge with an 18 by 16 mesh count) is not considered a WALL.
   LICENSED PRODUCTS. The term that collectively refers to any tobacco,
tobacco-related device, electronic delivery device or nicotine or lobelia
delivery product.
   LOOSIES. Any single or individually-packaged cigars or cigarettes offered
for sale, regardless of whether they have been removed from their original
retail packaging, and any other licensed product that has been removed from its
original retail packaging and offered for sale. LOOSIES does not include
individual cigars with a retail price, after any discounts are applied and
before any sales taxes are imposed, of at least $4 per cigar.
   MOVEABLE PLACE OF BUSINESS. Any form of business that is operated out of a
kiosk, truck, van, automobile or other type of vehicle or transportable shelter
and not a fixed address store front or other permanent type of structure
authorized for sales transactions.
   NICOTINE OR LOBELIA DELIVERY PRODUCT. Any product containing or delivering
nicotine or lobelia intended for human consumption, or any part of such a
product, that is not a tobacco or an electronic delivery device as defined in
this section. NICOTINE OR LOBELIA DELIVERY PRODUCT does not include any product
that has been approved or otherwise certified for legal sale by the U.S. Food
and Drug Administration as a tobacco-cessation product, a tobacco-dependence
product or for other medical purposes, and is being marketed and sold solely
for that approved purpose.
   PHARMACY. A place of business at which prescription drugs are prepared,
compounded or dispensed by or under the supervision of a pharmacist and from
which related clinical pharmacy services are delivered.
   RETAIL ESTABLISHMENT. Anyplace of business where licensed products are
available for sale to the general public. The phrase includes, but is not
limited to, grocery stores, tobacco products shops, convenience stores,
gasoline service stations, bars and restaurants.
   SALE. Any transfer of goods for money, trade, barter or other consideration.
   SELF-SERVICE DISPLAY. The open display of licensed products in any manner
where any person has access to the licensed products without the assistance or
intervention of the licensee or the licensee’s employee.
   SMOKING. Inhaling, exhaling, burning or carrying any lighted or heated
cigar, cigarette or pipe, or any other lighted or heated product containing,
made, or derived from nicotine, tobacco, marijuana, or other plant, whether
natural or synthetic, that is intended for inhalation. SMOKING also includes
carrying or using an activated electronic delivery device.
   TOBACCO. Any product containing, made or derived from tobacco that is
intended for human consumption, whether chewed, smoked, absorbed, dissolved,
inhaled, snorted, sniffed or ingested by any other means, or any component,
part or accessory of a tobacco product including, but not limited to:
cigarettes; cigars; cheroots; stogies; perique; granulated, plug cut, crimp
cut, ready rubbed and other smoking tobacco; snuff; snuff flour; cavendish;
plug and twist tobacco; fine cut and other chewing tobaccos; shorts; refuse
scraps, clippings, cuttings and sweepings of tobacco; and other kinds and forms
of tobacco. TOBACCO does not include any product that has been approved by the
U.S. Food and Drug Administration for sale as a tobacco-cessation product, as a
tobacco-dependence product or for other medical purposes, and is being marketed
and sold solely for such an approved purpose.
   TOBACCO-RELATED DEVICE. Any rolling papers, wraps, pipes or other device
intentionally designed or intended to be used with tobacco products. TOBACCO-
RELATED DEVICE includes components of tobacco-related devices or tobacco
products, which may be marketed or sold separately. TOBACCO-RELATED DEVICES may
or may not contain tobacco.
   VENDING MACHINE. Any mechanical, electric or electronic, or other type of
device that dispenses licensed products upon the insertion of money, tokens or
other form of payment directly into the machine by the person seeking to
purchase the licensed product.
   YOUTH-ORIENTED FACILITY. Any facility with residents, customers, visitors or
inhabitants of which 25% or more are regularly under the age of 21 or that
primarily sells, rents or offers services or products that are consumed or used
primarily by persons under the age of 21. YOUTH-ORIENTED FACILITY includes, but
is not limited to, schools, playgrounds, recreation centers and parks.
(Ord. 18.0, passed 12-16-2019)
§ 111.03 PROHIBITED ACTS.
   (A)   (1)   In general. No person shall sell or offer to sell any licensed
product:
         (a)   By means of any type of vending machine;
         (b)   By means of loosies, as defined;
         (c)   Containing opium, morphine, jimson weed, bella donna, strychnos,
cocaine, marijuana or other deleterious, hallucinogenic, toxic or controlled
substances except nicotine and other substances found naturally in tobacco or
added as part of an otherwise lawful manufacturing process. It is not the
intention of this provision to ban the sale of lawfully manufactured cigarettes
or other products subject to this chapter; and/or
         (d)   By any other means, to any other person, on in any other manner
or form prohibited by federal, state or other local law, ordinance provision or
other regulation.
      (2)   Legal age. No person shall sell any licensed product to any person
under the age of 21.
         (a)   Age verification. Licensees must verify by means of government-
issued photographic identification that the purchaser is at least 21 years of
age. Verification is not required for a person over the age of 30. That the
person appeared to be 30 years of age or older does not constitute a defense to
a violation of this division (A)(2).
         (b)   Signage. Notice of the legal sales age and age verification
requirement must be posted prominently and in plain view at all times at each
location where licensed products are offered for sale. The required signage,
which will be provided to the licensee by the city, must be posted in a manner
that is clearly visible to anyone who is or is considering making a purchase.
      (3)   Self-service sales. No person shall allow the sale of licensed
products by any self-service displays where the customer may have access to
those items without haying to request the item from the licensee or the
licensee’s employee and where there is not a physical exchange of the licensed
product from the licensee or the licensee’s employee to the customer. All
licensed products must be stored behind the sales counter, in another area not
freely accessible to customers. or in a case or other storage unit not left
open and accessible to the general public. Any retailer selling licensed
products at the time this chapter is adopted must comply with this section
within 90 days of the effective date of this chapter.
      (4)   Flavored products. No person shall sell or offer for sale any
flavored products.
      (5)   Imitation tobacco products. No person shall sell, offer to sell or
otherwise distribute any imitation tobacco products within the city.
      (6)   Liquid packaging. No person shall sell or offer to sell any liquid,
whether or not the liquid contains nicotine, which is intended for human
consumption and use in an electronic delivery device, in packaging that is not
child-resistant. Upon request by the city, a licensee must provide a copy of
the certificate of compliance or full laboratory testing report for the
packaging used.
   (B)   It is an administrative violation of this chapter for any person 21
years of age or older to purchase or otherwise obtain any licensed product on
behalf of a person under the age of 21. It is also a violation for any person
21 years of age and older to coerce or attempt to coerce a person under the age
of 21 to purchase or attempt to purchase any licensed product.
(Ord. 18.0, passed 12-16-2019) Penalty, see §
111.99
§ 111.04 RESPONSIBILITY.
   All licensees are responsible for the actions of their employees in regard
to the sale, offer to sell and furnishing of licensed products on the licensed
premises. The sale, offer to sell or furnishing of any licensed product by an
employee shall be considered an act of the licensee. Nothing in this section
shall be construed as prohibiting the city from also subjecting the employee to
any civil penalties that the city deems to be appropriate under this chapter,
state or federal law or other applicable law or regulation.
(Ord. 18.0, passed 12-16-2019)
§ 111.05 COMPLIANCE CHECKS AND INSPECTIONS.
   All licensed premises must be open to inspection by law enforcement or other
authorized city officials during regular business hours. From time to time, but
at least twice per year, the city will conduct compliance checks. In accordance
with state law, the city will conduct at least one compliance check that
involves the participation of a person between the ages of 15 and 17 and at
least one compliance check that involves the participation of a person between
the ages of 18 and 20 to enter the licensed premises to attempt to purchase
licensed products. Prior written consent from a parent or guardian is required
for any person under the age of 18 to participate in a compliance check.
Persons used for the purpose of compliance checks will be supervised by law
enforcement or other designated personnel.
(Ord. 18.0, passed 12-16-2019)
§ 111.06 EXCEPTIONS AND DEFENSES.
   (A)   Religious, spiritual or cultural ceremonies or practices. Nothing in
this chapter prevents the provision of tobacco or tobacco-related devices to
any person as part of an indigenous practice or a lawfully recognized
religious, spiritual or cultural ceremony or practice.
   (B)   Reasonable reliance. It is an affirmative defense to a violation of
this chapter for a person to have reasonably relied on proof of age as
described by state law.
(Ord. 18.0, passed 12-16-2019)
LICENSING; CONDITIONS
§ 111.20 LICENSE REQUIRED.
   No person shall sell or offer to sell any licensed product without first
having obtained a license to do so from the city.
(Ord. 18.0, passed 12-16-2019) Penalty, see §
111.99
§ 111.21 APPLICATION.
   An application for a license to sell licensed products must be made on a
form provided by the city. The application must contain the foil name of the
applicant, the applicant’s residential and business addresses and telephone
numbers, the name of the business for which the license is sought and any
additional information the city deems necessary. Upon receipt of a completed
application, the City Clerk will forward the application to the City Council
for action at its next regularly scheduled meeting. If the City Clerk
determines that an application is incomplete, it will be returned to the
applicant with notice of the information necessary to make the application
complete.
(Ord. 18.0, passed 12-16-2019)
§ 111.22 ACTION.
   (A)   The City Council may approve or deny the application for a license, or
it may delay action for a reasonable period of time to complete any
investigation of the application or the applicant deemed necessary.
   (B)   If the City Council approves the application, the City Clerk will
issue the license to the applicant.
   (C)   If the City Council denies the application, notice of the denial will
be given to the applicant along with notice of the applicant’s right to appeal
the decision.
(Ord. 18.0, passed 12-16-2019)
§ 111.23 TERM.
   All licenses issued are valid for one calendar year from the date of issue.
(Ord. 18.0, passed 12-16-2019)
§ 111.24 REVOCATION OR SUSPENSION.
   Any license issued may be suspended or revoked following the procedures set
forth in §
111.99 of this chapter.
(Ord. 18.0, passed 12-16-2019)
§ 111.25 TRANSFERS.
   All licenses issued are valid only on the premises for which the license was
issued and only for the person to whom the license was issued. The transfer of
any license to another location or person is prohibited.
(Ord. 18.0, passed 12-16-2019) Penalty, see §
111.99
§ 111.26 MOVEABLE PLACE OF BUSINESS.
   No license will be issued to a moveable place of business. Only fixed-
location businesses are eligible to be licensed.
(Ord. 18.0, passed 12-16-2019)
§ 111.27 DISPLAY.
   All licenses must be posted and displayed at all times in plain view of the
general public on the licensed premises.
(Ord. 18.0, passed 12-16-2019) Penalty, see §
111.99
§ 111.28 RENEWALS.
   The renewal of a license issued under this chapter will be handled in the
same manner as the original application. The request for a renewal must be made
at least 30 days, but no more than 60 days, before the expiration of the
current license.
(Ord. 18.0, passed 12-16-2019)
§ 111.29 ISSUANCE AS PRIVILEGE AND NOT RIGHT.
   The issuance of a license is a privilege and does not entitle the license
holder to an automatic renewal of the license.
(Ord. 18.0, passed 12-16-2019)
§ 111.30 MINIMUM AGE OF CLERK.
   Individuals employed by a licensed retail establishment under this chapter
must be at least 16 years of age to sell licensed products.
(Ord. 18.0, passed 12-16-2019)
§ 111.31 MAXIMUM NUMBER OF LICENSES.
   (A)   The maximum number of licenses issued by the city at any time is
limited to three.
   (B)   When the maximum number of licenses has been issued, the city may
place persons seeking licensure on a waiting list and allow them to apply on a
first-come, first-served basis, as licenses are not renewed or are revoked.
   (C)   A new applicant who has purchased a business location holding a valid
city license will be entitled to first priority; provided, the new applicant
meets all other application requirements in accordance with this chapter.
(Ord. 18.0, passed 12-16-2019)
§ 111.32 RESTRICTED LOCATIONS; PROXIMITY TO YOUTH-ORIENTED FACILITIES.
   (A)   Proximity to youth-oriented facilities.
      (1)   No license will be granted to any person for a retail establishment
location that is within 1,000 feet of a youth-oriented facility, as measured by
the shortest line from the property line of the space to be occupied by the
proposed licensee to the nearest property line of a youth-oriented facility.
      (2)   This restriction does not apply to an existing license holder who
has been licensed to sell licensed products in that same location for at least
one year before the date this division (A) was enacted into law.
   (B)   Pharmacies ineligible for licensure. No existing license will be
eligible for renewal to any pharmacy, including any retail establishment that
operates or contains an on-site pharmacy, and no pharmacy or any retail
establishment that operates an on-site pharmacy will be granted a new license.
(Ord. 18.0, passed 12-16-2019)
§ 111.33 SAMPLING PROHIBITED.
   (A)   Smoking prohibited. Smoking, including smoking for the purpose of the
sampling of licensed products, is prohibited within the indoor area of any
retail establishment licensed under this chapter.
   (B)   Samples prohibited. No person shall distribute samples of any licensed
product free of charge or at a nominal cost.
(Ord. 18.0, passed 12-16-2019) Penalty, see §
111.99
§ 111.34 INSTRUCTIONAL PROGRAM.
   (A)   Licensees must ensure that all employees complete a training program
on the legal requirements related to the sale of licensed products and the
possible consequences of license violations.
   (B)   Any training program must be pre-approved by the city.
   (C)   Licensees must maintain documentation demonstrating their compliance
and must provide this documentation to the city at the time of renewal, or
whenever requested to do so during the license term.
(Ord. 18.0, passed 12-16-2019)
§ 111.35 FEES.
   No license will be issued under this chapter until the appropriate license
fees are paid in full. The fees will be established by the city’s fee schedule
and may be amended from time to time.
(Ord. 18.0, passed 12-16-2019)
§ 111.36 LICENSE DENIAL.
   (A)   Grounds for denying the issuance or renewal of a license include, but
are not limited to, the following:
      (1)   The applicant is under 21 years of age;
      (2)   The applicant has been convicted within the past five years of any
violation of a federal, state or local law, ordinance provision or other
regulation relating to licensed products;
      (3)   The applicant has had a license to sell licensed products suspended
or revoked within the preceding 12 months of the date of application;
      (4)   The applicant fails to provide any of the information required on
the licensing application or provides false or misleading information; and/or
      (5)   The applicant is prohibited by federal, state or other local law,
ordinance or other regulation from holding a license.
   (B)   Except as may otherwise be provided by law, the existence of any
particular ground for denial does not compel the city to deny the license.
   (C)   If a license is mistakenly issued or renewed to a person, it will be
revoked upon the discovery that the person was ineligible for the license under
this chapter. The city will provide the license holder with notice of the
revocation, along with information on the right to appeal.
(Ord. 18.0, passed 12-16-2019)
§ 111.99 PENALTY.
   (A)   Violations.
      (1)   Notice. A person violating this chapter may be issued, either
personally or by mail, a citation from the city that sets forth the alleged
violation and that informs the alleged violator of his, her or their right to a
hearing on the matter and how and where a hearing may be requested, including a
contact address and phone number.
      (2)   Hearings.
         (a)   Upon issuance of a citation, a person accused of violating this
chapter may request in writing a hearing on the matter. Hearing requests must
be made within ten business days of the issuance of the citation and delivered
to the City Clerk or other designated city officer. Failure to properly request
a hearing within ten business days of the issuance of the citation will
terminate the person’s right to a hearing.
         (b)   The City Clerk or other designated city officer will set the
time and place for the hearing. Written notice of the hearing time and place
will be mailed or delivered to the accused violator at least ten business days
prior to the hearing.
      (3)   Hearing Officer. The City Council will designate a Hearing Officer.
The Hearing Officer will be an impartial employee of the city or an impartial
person retained by the city to conduct the hearing.
      (4)   Decision. A decision will be issued by the Hearing Officer within
ten business days of the hearing. If the Hearing Officer determines that a
violation of this chapter did occur, that decision, along with the Hearing
Officer’s reasons for finding a violation and the penalty to be imposed, will
be recorded in writing, a copy of which will be provided to the city and the
accused violator by in-person delivery or mail as soon as practicable. If the
Hearing Officer finds that no violation occurred or finds grounds for not
imposing any penalty, those findings will be recorded and a copy will be
provided to the city and the acquitted accused violator by in-person delivery
or mail as soon as practicable. The decision of the Hearing Officer is final,
subject to an appeal as described in division (A)(6) below.
      (5)   Costs. If the citation is upheld by the Hearing Officer, the city’s
actual expenses in holding the hearing up to a maximum amount as set by City
Council from time to time must be paid by the person requesting the hearing.
      (6)   Appeals. Appeals of any decision made by the Hearing Officer must
be filed in the county’s District Court within ten business days of the date of
the decision.
      (7)   Continued violation. Each violation, and every day in which a
violation occurs or continues, shall constitute a separate offense.
   (B)   Administrative penalties.
      (1)   Licensees. Any licensee cited and found to have violated this
chapter, or whose employee violated this chapter, will be required to complete
retailer education that the city determines to be appropriate for a first
violation and may be subject to an administrative fine as set by City Council
from time to time for failure to complete education; will be charged an amount
as set by City Council from time to time for a second offense at the same
licensed premises within a 24-month period; and an amount as set by City
Council from time to time for a third or subsequent offense at the same
location within a 24-month period. Upon the third violation, the license will
be suspended for a period of not less than 30 consecutive days. Upon a fourth
violation, the license will be revoked.
      (2)   Other individuals. Individuals, other than persons under the age of
21 regulated by division (B)(3) below, who are found to be in violation of this
chapter will be charged an administrative fine as set by City Council from time
to time.
      (3)   Persons under the age of 21. Persons under the age of 21 who
violate this chapter may only be subject to non-criminal, non-monetary civil
penalties such as tobacco-related education classes, diversion programs,
community services or another penalty that the city determines to be
appropriate. The City Council will consult with court personnel, educators,
parents, children and other interested parties to determine an appropriate
penalty for persons under the age of 21 in the city. The penalty may be
established by ordinance and amended from time to time.
      (4)   Statutory penalties. If the administrative penalty authorized to be
imposed by M.S. § 461.12, as it may be amended from time to time, differ from
that established in this section, then the higher penalty will prevail.
   (C)   Misdemeanor prosecution. Nothing in this section prohibits the city
from seeking prosecution as a misdemeanor for any alleged violation of this
chapter by a person 21 years of age or older.
(Ord. 18.0, passed 12-16-2019)
CHAPTER 112: AMUSEMENTS AND LEISURE
Section
   
112.01   Bingo and other gambling
   
112.02   Public dances
 
   
112.99   Penalty
§ 112.01 BINGO AND OTHER GAMBLING.
   (A)   Purpose. The purpose of this section is to regulate and control the
conduct of gambling and bingo.
   (B)   Provisions of state law adopted. The provisions of M.S. Chapter 349,
as it may be amended from time to time, relating to the definition of terms,
licensing and restrictions of gambling and bingo are adopted and made a part of
this section as if set out in full.
   (C)   Definitions.
      (1)   For the purpose of this section, the following definitions shall
apply unless the context clearly requires a different meaning.
         BINGO. A game where each player has a card or board for which a
consideration has been paid containing five horizontal rows of spaces, with
each row except, the center one containing five figures. The central row has
form figures with the word “free” marked in the center. A player wins a game by
completing any combination of five spaces in a row following the announcement
of the numbers to all the players.
         GAMBLING. Includes paddle wheels, tipboards, raffles, pull tab and any
other similar devise authorized by appropriate state law.
      (2)   Any expansion of the above definitions shall be as authorized or
explained by state statutes or applicable court decisions only.
   (D)   License requirement. No person shall directly or indirectly operate a
gambling device or bingo occasion without a license to do so as provided in
this section.
   (E)   Persons eligible for a license. A license shall be issued only to
fraternal, religious and veterans organizations, or any corporation, trust or
association organized for exclusively scientific, literary, charitable,
educational or artistic purposes, or any club which is organized and operated
exclusively for pleasure or recreation. The organization must be non-profit in
nature and have been in existence for at least three years and shall have at
least 30 active members.
   (F)   License fees.
      (1)   Fees. The annual fee for a paddle wheel, for a tipboard, for a
raffle, for a pull-tab and for bingo shall be as set by City Council from time
to time. If all five licenses are issued, the annual fee shall be as set by
City Council from time to time.
      (2)   Application procedure. Application for a license shall be made upon
a form prescribed by the Council. No person shall make a false representation
in an application. The Council shall act upon the application within 180 days
from the date of application, but shall not issue a license until at least 30
days after the date of application.
   (G)   Profits. Profits from the operation of gambling and bingo shall be
used for proper purposes only.
   (H)   Conduct of gambling and bingo.
      (1)   Manager. All operation of gambling devices and bingo shall be under
the supervision of a single manager to be designated by the organization. The
manager shall be responsible for gross receipts and profits from gambling and
bingo and for their operation. The manager shall be responsible for using
profits for a proper purpose.
      (2)   Bond. The manager shall provide a fidelity bond in the sum as set
by City Council from time to time in favor of the organization conditioned on
the faithful performance of his, her or their duties.
      (3)   Qualifications of manager. The manager shall be an active member of
the organization, be of legal age and shall qualify under state law. The
licensee shall state the name of the manager on the license application. The
manager must be present at all times when bingo or gambling, except for raffles
are being operated on the premises involved. If a licensee changes its manager
of gambling or bingo during its license period, it must notify the city in
writing immediately.
   (I)   Compensation. No compensation shall be paid to any person in
connection with the operation of gambling or bingo by a licensed organization.
No person who is not an active member of an organization, or the spouse or
surviving spouse of an active member, may participate in the organization’s
operation of gambling or bingo.
   (J)   Reporting requirements.
      (1)   Gross receipts. Each organization licensed to operate gambling or
bingo shall keep records of its gross receipts, expenses and profits for each
single gathering or occasion at which gambling or bingo is operated. All
deductions from gross receipts for each single gathering or occasion shall be
documented with receipts or other records indicating the amount, a description
of the purchased item or service or other reason for the deduction and the
recipient. The distribution of profits shall be itemized as to payee, purpose,
amount and date of payment.
      (2)   Separation of funds. Gross receipts from the operation of gambling
or bingo shall be segregated from other revenues of the organization and placed
in a separate account. The person who accounts for gross receipts, expenses and
profits from the operation of gambling or bingo shall not be the same person
who accounts for other revenues of the organization.
      (3)   Monthly reports. Each organization licensed to operate gambling or
bingo shall report monthly to its membership, and to the City Clerk, its gross
receipts, expenses and profits from gambling or bingo, and the distribution of
profits. The license shall preserve the records for at least three years.
   (K)   Eligible premises.
      (1)   Gambling or bingo shall be operated by a licensed organization only
upon premises which it owns or leases; except that, tickets for raffles may be
sold off the premises. Leases shall be in writing and shall be for a term of at
least 12 months. No lease shall provide that rental payments be based on a
percentage of receipts.
      (2)   A copy of the lease shall be filed with the City Clerk.
   (L)   Gambling prizes. Total prizes from the operation of paddle wheels,
tipboards and pull-tabs awarded in any single day in which they are operated
shall not exceed an amount as set by City Council from time to time. Total
prizes resulting from any single spin of a paddle wheel or from any single
tipboard or from a single pull-tab shall not exceed an amount as set by City
Council from time to time. Total prizes from the operation of a raffle shall
not exceed an amount as set by City Council from time to time. Total prizes
awarded in any calendar year by any organization shall not exceed an amount as
set by City Council from time to time.
   (M)   Bingo prizes. Prizes for a single bingo game shall not exceed an
amount as set by City Council from time to time, except for a “cover-all” game.
“Cover-all” prizes may not exceed an amount as set by City Council from time to
time; provided that, the total value of the prizes for a bingo occasion shall
not exceed an amount as set by City Council from time to time. The aggregate
value of prizes for a bingo occasion shall not exceed an amount as set by City
Council from time to time; except that, in the case of a bingo occasion during
which a “cover-all” game is played for a maximum prize of an amount as set by
City Council from time to time, the aggregate value of prizes for the bingo
occasion shall not exceed an amount as set by City Council from time to time.
(Ord. passed 5-5-1982) Penalty, see §
112.99
§ 112.02 PUBLIC DANCES.
   (A)   Definitions. For the purpose of this section, the following
definitions shall apply unless the context clearly requires a different
meaning.
      ADMISSION FEE. A fee charged for admission to a public dance; a fee
charged for a ticket, bullion or other indication of payment that includes
among other things admission to a dance; a payment made to a club, corporation,
business or other entity that is made for the primary purpose of gaining entry
to the public dance.
      PERSON. Any individual, business, organization, association, club or any
other entity that conducts a public dance. In the event of an organization,
corporation, club or other entity, the person holding the dance shall be deemed
to be the officers, directors, trustees or agents of the organization that are
conducting the dance.
      PUBLIC DANCE. Any dance where the public may participate by paying an
admission fee.
   (B)   Permit required. No person shall give, hold or conduct a public dance
unless the person conducting the dance shall first have procured a permit to
conduct the public dance from the city.
   (C)   Law officer required. At each such dance, an officer of the law shall
be present at all times during the conducting of the dance. The officer in
attendance shall be designated by the Chief of Police of the city.
   (D)   Issuance of permit. The city will issue a permit to any person wishing
to hold a public dance upon the following terms and conditions.
      (1)   A separate permit must be obtained for each dance. The person
conducting the dance must pay the fee for the permit as the City Council
determines from time to time.
      (2)   The person requesting the permit must pay a fee to the city to
reimburse the city for the cost of employing police officer at the dance.
      (3)   The person conducting the dance shall comply with all requests by
the Chief of Police or the officer in attendance during the conducting of the
dance.
      (4)   The application for permit shall specify addresses of the persons
to whom the permit is to be issued, the date and hours during which the dance
is to be held and the specific location where the dance will be.
      (5)   The permit shall be posted in a conspicuous place during the dance.
      (6)   The city reserves the right to refuse to issue a permit to any
person previously convicted of any crime related to the operation of any dance.
   (E)   Alcoholic beverages. At any dance where beer, wine or intoxicating
liquors are served and where people under the age of 21 are allowed to attend,
every person under the age of 21 shall be identified by the person to whom the
dance permit is issued or to an employee or agent designated by the permit
holder before any person under the age of 21 shall have his, her or their hand
stamped with a red mark showing the person going into the dance is under the
age of 21. The persons under the age of 21 shall not consume or attempt to
consume intoxicating or 3.2% malt liquors on the site.
   (F)   Private dances. Dances held by clubs or organizations for their
members or invited guests only are not considered public dances pursuant to
this section. Dances held at a school or church for the benefit of students or
members of the church only and their invited guests shall not be considered
public dances.
(Ord. passed 9-25-1989) Penalty, see §
112.99
§ 112.99 PENALTY.
   (A)   Any person violating any provision of this chapter for which no
specific penalty is prescribed shall be subject to §
10.99 of this code of ordinances.
   (B)   (1)   Criminal penalty. Violation of any provision of §
112.01 of this chapter shall be a misdemeanor. A person convicted of violating
any provision of §
112.01 of this chapter shall be subject to a fine of not more than an amount as
set by City Council from time to time or imprisonment for a term not to exceed
90 days or both, plus in either case the costs of prosecution.
      (2)   Suspension and revocation. Any license may be suspended or revoked
for any violation of §
112.01 of this chapter or appropriate state statutes. A license shall not be
suspended or revoked until the procedural requirements of division (B)(3) below
have been complied with; except that, when the Council has evidence that
probable cause exists as to an ordinance violation, the city may temporarily
suspend upon service of notice of the hearing provided for in division (B)(3)
below. The temporary suspension shall not extend for more than two weeks.
      (3)   Procedure. A license shall not be revoked under division (B)(2)
above until notice and an opportunity for a hearing have first been given to
the licensee. The notice shall be personally served and shall state the
ordinance provision or state statute reasonably believed to be violated. The
notice shall also state that the licensee may demand a hearing on the matter,
in which case the license will not be revoked until after the hearing is held.
A demand for a hearing by a licensee shall be in writing and delivered to the
City Clerk within ten days after the notice has been served on the licensee. If
the licensee requests a hearing, one shall be held on the matter by the Council
at least one, but not more than four, weeks after the date on which the request
is made. If, as a result of the hearing, the Council finds that an ordinance or
statute violation exists, then the Council may suspend or revoke the license.
   (C)   Any violations of §
112.02 of this chapter by any person, firm, corporation, organization, club or
entity shall be considered a misdemeanor.
(Ord. passed 5-5-1982; Ord. passed 9-25-1989)

FIREARMS
§ 130.01 DEFINITION.
   For the purpose of this subchapter, the term FIREARM shall mean any weapon
from which is propelled any dart, arrow, missile, projectile or bullet by means
of explosive or gas, or by energy stored in a string or spring, notwithstanding
the fact that the dart, arrow, missile or projectile remains attached to the
weapon by wire.
(Ord. passed 9-22-2014)
§ 130.02 PROHIBITED ACTIVITY.
   No person shall fire, discharge or transport any firearm within the city
limits. A person may transport a firearm if it is in a closed case and not
ready for immediate firing or discharge.
(Ord. passed 9-22-2014) Penalty, see §
130.99
§ 130.03 EXCEPTIONS.
   This subchapter does not apply to:
   (A)   Law enforcement or military personnel acting within the performance of
their duty;
   (B)   Any person lawfully defending a person or property;
   (C)   The transportation by a person who has obtained a permit to possess or
transport a pistol under the provision of M.S. § 624.714, as it may be amended
from time to time;
   (D)   A person discharging a firearm in an outdoor shooting or archery range
which has been approved for that purpose by the Chief of Police;
   (E)   Except a person lawfully hunting on public or private lands within the
city that have been approved for use of firearms by the Chief of Police;
   (F)   A person whose firearm is using blank ammunition in conjunction with a
sporting event or upon a ceremonial occasion; and/or
   (G)   A person firing or discharging a firearm which propels a single
projectile which is a spherical steel shot of 0.177 caliber or less, commonly
referred to as a “BB gun”.
(Ord. passed 9-22-2014)
GAME HUNTING
§ 130.15 PROHIBITED ACTIVITY.
   No person may use a firearm, bow or other dangerous weapon in hunting game
within the city, except as permitted herein.
(Ord. passed 9-22-2014) Penalty, see §
130.99
§ 130.16 EXCEPTIONS.
   A person may hunt deer using a bow and pointed-tip arrow under all of the
following circumstances:
   (A)   The shooting occurs on property the person owns or on another person’s
property if the shooter has in his or her possession written permission of the
property owner to engage in the activity;
   (B)   The arrow does not travel beyond the boundaries of that property;
   (C)   Unless otherwise specified by written permission of the property
owner, any shooting must occur at least 100 feet from any building and from any
land not owned by that landowner;
   (D)   The shooting does not endanger anyone else;
   (E)   The person has in his or her possession a valid state deer archery
license;
   (F)   The person has completed a bow hunter educational program and an
archery proficiency test, and is able to show proof of this proficiency as
granted and administered by a certified archery instructor;
   (G)   A person archery hunting for deer must make use of a tree stand at
least five feet from ground level unless a person engaged in hunting is
physically unable; and
   (H)   Compliance with all requirements of the deer hunting policy for the
city then in effect.
(Ord. passed 9-22-2014)
§ 130.99 PENALTY.
   (A)   Any person violating any provision of this chapter for which no
specific penalty is prescribed shall be subject to §
10.99 of this code of ordinances.
   (B)   Notwithstanding anything in city ordinances to the contrary, a person
who violates any provisions of §§
130.01 through
130.03 of this chapter shall be guilty of a misdemeanor punishable according to
misdemeanor limits, as established from time to time by the state.
   (C)   Notwithstanding anything in city ordinances to the contrary, a person
who violates any provisions of §§
130.15 and
130.16 of this chapter shall be guilty of a misdemeanor punishable according to
misdemeanor limits as established from time to time by the state. The minimum
penalty for a person’s first offense under §
130.15 of this chapter is a fine as set by City Council from time to time.
(Ord. 3961, passed 3-15-2010; Ord. passed 9-22-2014)

CHAPTER 150: BUILDING REGULATIONS; CONSTRUCTION
Section
   
150.01   Life Safety Code adopted
 
   
150.99   Penalty
§ 150.01 LIFE SAFETY CODE ADOPTED.
   (A)   Adoption of Code. The Life Safety Code, copyright 1985, as prepared by
the National Fire Protection Association, Inc., is hereby adopted by reference
as an official code of the city. All provisions of this Code shall apply to the
buildings in the city that are now in existence or are constructed hereafter.
   (B)   Exceptions to the Code.
      (1)   The City Council may grant temporary exceptions to the requirements
of the Life Safety Code under certain conditions. In order to obtain an
exception to the provisions of this Code, the building owner or occupant must
make a written request to the city for the exception itemizing the relief
requested.
      (2)   Upon receipt of the request, the city shall cause the responsible
city official to investigate the building and exception requested and make a
written report to the City Council. The report shall outline the code
provisions that are involved describing in detail what is required by the Code,
what the existing condition is, what the cost of complying is estimated to be
and the official’s estimate of what consequences there may be for the safety to
occupants or neighbors of the building if the relief or some part thereof is
granted. The person preparing the report may also make a recommendation to the
City Council regarding the request.
      (3)   Before granting any variance to the provisions of the Life Safety
Code, the City Council shall make the following findings:
         (a)   The relief granted will not endanger the life of any person;
         (b)   The variance requested will not violate any law, statute,
ordinance or regulation not contained in the Life Safety Code;
         (c)   The benefits received from the required change are too expensive
to be justified in the building involved at the time requested; or
         (d)   The installation of the required changes will be economically
feasible if done over a period of time.
   (C)   Enforcement and disclosure.
      (1)   The City Fire Marshal or the City Zoning Administrator shall
enforce this section through the proper legal channels. The owner or occupant
of any building shall grant access to the inspector at reasonable times upon
showing of proper identification for the purpose of making inspections
necessary to enforce this section.
      (2)   The inspector shall note any defects on the premises being
inspected in a written report. One copy of the report shall be kept as the city
record and a complete copy of the report shall be given or mailed to the
building owner. Any defects noted shall be cured within a reasonable time or
such time as is determined by the inspector for purposes of this section. For
purposes of this section, a REASONABLE TIME shall not exceed 30 days.
      (3)   The city inspection records shall be public information and any
person may have access to the records during normal business hours.
      (4)   If defects noted are not cured as required by this section, the
city may record a copy of the inspection report in the County Recorder’s
office.
      (5)   Any defects noted on the inspection report shall be disclosed in
writing to any tenant or prospective purchaser of the premises involved. This
disclosure is required only if the defects noted are not cured within the
required time allowed.
   (D)   Public use. A copy of the Life Safety Code of 1985 shall be kept at
the City Clerk’s office at all times and shall be made available for public use
in the City Clerk’s office only.
(Ord. passed - -) Penalty, see §
150.99
§ 150.99 PENALTY.
   (A)   For the purpose of this chapter, the following definitions shall apply
unless the context clearly requires a different meaning.
   (B)   (1)   Any person, corporation, partnership or other organization who
violates or fails to comply with any of the provisions of §
150.01 of this chapter shall be guilty of a misdemeanor.
      (2)   Each day that the violation is permitted to exist shall constitute
a separate offense.
(Ord. passed - -)
CHAPTER 151: STREETS, SIDEWALKS AND PUBLIC PROPERTY
Section
   
151.01   Public sidewalks
 
   
151.99   Penalty
§ 151.01 PUBLIC SIDEWALKS.
   (A)   All sidewalks on the sides of streets in the city shall be kept and
maintained in a safe and good condition for use by the public and in a good
state of repair by and at the expense of the owners of the premises abutting on
that side of the street where the sidewalk is located and in front of which
premises the sidewalk is constructed. The owner of the premises shall be
responsible to check the sidewalk to make certain that the sidewalk complies
with this section.
   (B)   (1)   Because they may endanger life, limb and property, the following
conditions in sidewalks are hereby declared to be nuisances:
         (a)   Any crack, fissure, raise, hump, unevenness or condition therein
of the width, height, depth or form (height or depth of three-fourths inch or
more) that pedestrians lawfully using the walk might catch their shoes, feet,
canes, crutches or other proper implements thereon or therein or which might
cause pedestrians using the walk to trip, stumble or fall or which endangers
the use of the walks for travel with wheelchairs, preambulators or similar
conveyances;
         (b)   Sags or depressions therein which can catch or hold water, dirt
or other debris;
         (c)   Accumulations of earth, sod, vegetation or other materials on or
along the edges of the sidewalks which prevent the free drainage of water over
the edges, from the surface of the walk; and
         (d)   All snow accumulations over one inch deep and ice not removed
from public sidewalks within 12 hours after the ice and snow have ceased to be
deposited thereon.
      (2)   Whenever the nuisance exists in the city, the city shall:
         (a)   Whenever the nuisance is brought to the attention of the city,
the City Clerk shall order the owner of the premises along which the sidewalk
is constructed to correct the condition causing the nuisance and to put the
walk in proper and safe condition for use by the public, at his or her own
expense, within the time specified in the order, but not to exceed 30 days;
         (b)   The order shall be in writing and shall briefly describe and
designate the nuisance and shall state that unless the nuisance is abated, the
cause thereof corrected and removed, and the sidewalk put in a safe and proper
condition within the specified time, that the city will cause the same to be
done at the expense of the owner of the premises and will cause the expense
thereof to be assessed against the premises. If the nuisance consists of an
accumulation of snow or ice, the corrective action shall be taken by the city
immediately after the specified time has elapse and the cost of the same shall
be added to the real estate tax rolls as set forth in division (B)(3) below
without any hearing being required prior to removing the snow and ice and
assessing the cost thereof; and
         (c)   The order to repair shall be personally served upon the owner
and if the premises are occupied, then the order shall also be personally
served on the occupant or occupants thereof in the same manner as a summons.
      (3)   In the event the owner or occupant of the premises does not repair
the premises as required in division (B)(2) above, the following procedure
shall be followed.
         (a)   The City Clerk shall notify the City Council who shall then set
a date, time and place to have a hearing concerning the repairs to be made.
         (b)   Prior to the hearing, notice of the same shall be published in
the city newspaper and shall be served upon the occupant or owner of the
premises at least ten days prior to the hearing. The notice shall describe the
defects to be cured, the estimated amount of the costs and expenses and the
date, time and location of the hearing.
         (c)   At the hearing and after hearing all interested parties, the
Council may make the order as is appropriate. In order to find that the repairs
described shall be made, the Council must make the following findings:
            1.   A defect in the sidewalk as described in division (B)(1) above
exists;
            2.   The notice required to be given in division (B)(2) above has,
in fact, been served on the owner or occupant of the premises;
            3.   The owner or occupant of the premises has not made the
required repairs at the time of this hearing; and
            4.   The estimated cost of repairing the same is reasonable and
proper for the repairs to be made.
      (4)   If, after completing the findings above, the Council determines
that the repairs indicated are necessary, it shall order the City Clerk to
cause the same to be completed and to add 10% of the cost of making the
improvements to the bill to reimburse the city for the expenses of clerical,
advertising, service and hearing expenses and shall further order the City
Clerk to certify the cost of the same to the County Auditor. The Auditor shall
extend the same on the tax roles of the county against the premises involved
and the costs and expenses shall be collected and paid to the city by the
Auditor as and for the reimbursement to the city for the costs and expenses
incurred in this matter.
   (C)   It shall be unlawful for any person to block or place obstacles on any
public sidewalk, as herein defined, or to order the same, except as may be
necessary to warn of an unsafe condition, to allow for repairs or as authorized
by the City Council or state law.
   (D)   Motorized vehicles, bicycles, skateboards and other wheeled vehicles,
except wheelchairs or similar devices when used for the transportation of
handicapped or ill persons, are prohibited from use of public sidewalks, except
where specifically marked for their use.
(Ord. passed 7-25-1988) Penalty, see §
151.99
§ 151.99 PENALTY.
   (A)   Any person violating any provision of this chapter for which no
specific penalty is prescribed shall be subject to §
10.99 of this code of ordinances.
   (B)   Violations of §
151.01(B)(1)(d) and (C) and (D) of this chapter shall be a petty misdemeanor.
(Ord. passed 7-25-1988)

TREES, TRAILS AND PARKS
§ 152.01 AUTHORITY AND PURPOSE.
   (A)   This subchapter is enacted under the authority of Chapter I of the
Rushford City Charter and M.S. §§ 412.111 and 412.221, subd. 8, as they may be
amended from time to time.
   (B)   The purpose of this subchapter is twofold: to enhance and protect the
city’s greenscape; and to promote and protect public health, safety and general
welfare. It outlines the role and responsibilities of the city and of private
landowners by providing for the regulation of the planting, maintenance and
removal of trees on public and private property within the city.
(Ord. passed 5-12-2008; Ord. passed 5-13-2013)
§ 152.02 DEFINITIONS.
   For the purpose of this subchapter, the following definitions shall apply
unless the context clearly requires a different meaning.
   BOULEVARD. Area between curb and the private property line that is publicly
owned.
   CITY. The City of Rushford, Minnesota.
   PUBLIC PROPERTY. Any area or building owned by the city, including, but not
limited to: boulevards; parks; playgrounds; dikes; parkways; streets; sidewalks
and alleys; and public parking lots.
   PUBLIC RIGHT-OF-WAY. Portion of properly reserved for public use and
accepted for the use by the city to provide circulation and travel to abutting
properties, including, but not limited to: streets; boulevards; alleys;
sidewalks; provisions for public utilities; and cut and fill slopes.
   PUBLIC UTILITY. Any public or private facility or system for producing,
transmitting or distributing communications, electricity, gas, oil products,
water, waste or storm water, which directly or indirectly serves the public or
any part thereof within the corporate limits of the city.
   RUSHFORD FORESTRY STANDARDS AND SPECIFICATIONS. Standards and specifications
needed to carry out work on the bluffs within the city.
   TREE. Any tree, shrub, hedge or woody vegetation.
      (1)   HAZARD TREE. Any public or private tree or part thereof which:
         (a)   Has an infectious or destructive disease, insect problem or
other pestilence which endangers the growth, health, life or well being of
trees in the city, or which threatens to or is capable of causing a spread of a
disease, pestilence or insect infestation;
         (b)   Is dead, dying, broken or decayed;
         (c)   Obstructs street lights, traffic signs or the view of any street
intersection;
         (d)   Obstructs the free passage of pedestrians or vehicles;
         (e)   Is causing the surface of a public street, curb or sidewalk to
be up-heaved or otherwise disturbed;
         (f)   Injures or poses an imminent threat of injury to the sewer
system, electric power lines, gas lines, water lines or other public
improvement; or
         (g)   Constitutes an imminent danger to the health, safety or well-
being of the general public.
      (2)   LANDMARK TREE. Any tree which meets one or more of the following
criteria: species rarity, old age, historical significance, abnormality or
scenic or aesthetic significance as determined by the Tree Board.
      (3)   LARGE TREE. Any plant material that will normally grow to a height
of over 50 feet.
      (4)   MEDIUM TREE. Any plant material that will normally grow to a height
of more than 30 feet, but not over a height of 50 feet.
      (5)   PRIVATE TREE. All trees growing on private property within the city
limits.
      (6)   PUBLIC TREE. All trees growing on any public property or public
right-of-way owned by the city.
      (7)   SMALL TREE. Any plant material that will normally grow to a height
of 30 feet or less.
   TOPPING. The severe cutting back of limbs to stubs larger than three inches
in diameter within the tree’s crown to such a degree so as to remove the normal
canopy and disfigure the tree. Sometimes referred to as TOP. This is an
undesirable practice, causing shock to the tree, leaving it susceptible to
disease and affecting the overall health by disturbing the crown to root ratio.
No public trees shall be TOPPED.
(Ord. passed 5-12-2008; Ord. passed 5-13-2013)
§ 152.03 TREE BOARD; CITY RESPONSIBILITIES.
   (A)   The city will designate, annually, its Administrator or other
qualified city employee to serve as City Forester.
   (B)   The city will allocate resources for the planting and care of public
trees and other vegetation. This includes assigning and managing city staff to
complete necessary tasks. If needed, the city shall appoint or contract with a
licensed arborist to perform the duties imposed by this subchapter. The
arborist will provide proof of liability insurance and workers compensation
insurance.
   (C)   (1)   Board established. There is hereby created and established a
City Tree Board. The Tree Board shall serve in an advisory capacity to the City
Council concerning issues related to the well-being of the urban forest
resources within the municipal boundaries of the city.
      (2)   Board composition. The Mayor shall appoint five persons to serve as
Commissioners of the Tree Board on approval by the City Council. One member
shall be also serving on the City Council. The City Forester will be an ex
officio member of the Tree Board. The remaining Commissioners shall be selected
at large from within the City Commissioners shall serve without compensation.
      (3)   Board officers. The Board shall choose its own officers and keep
minutes of its proceedings. Commissioners from the City Council shall not hold
any office of the Board. A majority of the members shall constitute a quorum
for the transaction of any business.
      (4)   Term of office. The term of the Tree Board shall be three years. In
the event that a vacancy shall occur during the term of any member, his or her
successor shall be appointed for the unexpired portion of the term. Board
members may not serve more than two consecutive terms.
      (5)   Board responsibilities. The affairs of the Board shall include, but
not limited to, promoting well-managed growth and development of the urban
forest within the city. Duties shall be to:
         (a)   Serve as an advocate of the city’s urban forest in its work with
the City Forester to achieve the purposes set forth herein. It will meet
monthly, or otherwise as needed;
         (b)   Recommend the adoption and alteration of all rules, regulations
and ordinances which it shall deem in the public interest to enhance and
beautify the urban forest as well as to carry out the purposes of this
subchapter. For example:
            1.   Establish and enforce standards and specifications needed to
carry out tree care work in the city;
            2.   Develop and maintain a list of both desirable and unsuitable
trees for planting along streets in three size classes: small; medium; and
large; and
            3.   Maintain a list of licensed arborists for any person wanting
to cut, trim, prune, remove, spray or otherwise treat any public tree.
         (c)   Encourage improvement of the urban forest through:
            1.   Policy development, such as:
               a.   The review and approval of capital projects where trees or
other vegetation will be planted or removed on public property; and
               b.   To identify, publicize and preserve landmark trees on both
public and private property.
            2.   Planning through an annual work plan and budget for Council
review and approval. The work plan will:
               a.   Encourage landscaping, planting and maintenance of trees
and vegetation on private property by providing information on the value of
landscaping and on the proper planting and care of trees and other vegetation;
               b.   Promote appreciation of trees and the urban forest for
example, through annual Arbor Day observances and other activities in
conjunction with the school district and community organizations; and
               c.   Assist City departments to enhance the urban forest in the
city and recommend obtaining professional assistance when needed.
         (d)   Hear appeals and act as a review board for matters pertaining to
this subchapter; and
         (e)   The Board shall develop and review plans and advise the Council
on both immediate and long-range planning on the acquisition and development of
parks and recreational facilities which may include advising the Council on
natural resource, wildlife management and natural preservation issues. The
Board shall also make and furnish studies, reports and recommendations as the
Council may request.
(Ord. passed 5-12-2008; Ord. passed 5-13-2013)
§ 152.04 PLANTING, MAINTENANCE AND REMOVAL.
   (A)   Permits required.
      (1)   No person may plant, remove, alter, destroy, cut or disturb any
public tree within the city without permission of the City Forester. In the
case of a requested planting, a permit application shall be submitted and the
City Forester shall consider whether it is an appropriate species for the
proposed location, taking into account appearance, care and maintenance
requirements, spacing, nearby utilities and distance from curbs, sidewalks,
corners, fire hydrants or driveways.
      (2)   Permits shall not be required for work performed by city employees.
   (B)   Tree planting specifications.
      (1)   Tree species. The species should be selected from the list of
“Recommended Trees for SE Minnesota”, as prepared by the University of
Minnesota Extension Service Office.
      (2)   Spacing. The spacing of public trees will be in accordance with the
size classes listed in this subchapter. No tree may be planted closer together
than the following: small trees, 15 feet; medium trees, 30 feet; and large
trees, 40 feet.
      (3)   Utilities. No public trees, other than those species listed herein
as small trees, may be planted under or within ten lateral feet of any overhead
transmission or primary utility wire, or over or within five lateral feet of
any underground water line, sewer line, transmission line or other utility.
      (4)   Distance from curb and sidewalk. The distance trees may be planted
from curbs or curb lines and sidewalks will be in accordance with the tree
species, size and classes listed herein. No trees may be planted closer to any
curb or sidewalk than three feet.
      (5)   Distance from corners, fire hydrants and driveways. No public tree
shall be planted closer than 30 feet to any street corner, measured from the
point of the nearest intersecting curbs or curb lines. No public tree shall be
planted closer than ten feet to any fire hydrant, nor five feet from any
driveway or walkway.
   (C)   Tree maintenance. The city shall have the right, but not the
obligation to plant, prune, maintain and remove public trees as may be
necessary to ensure the public safety or to preserve or enhance the symmetry
and beauty of the public property.
   (D)   Prevention of visual obstructions. Every public or private tree
overhanging any street or right-of-way within the city shall be trimmed so that
the branches shall not obstruct the light from any street lamp or obstruct the
view of any street intersection or street sign and so that there shall be a
clear space of at least 12 feet above the street or eight feet above the
sidewalk.
(Ord. passed 5-12-2008; Ord. passed 5-13-2013)
§ 152.05 PRIVATE HAZARD TREES.
   (A)   Control of private hazard trees. The city maintains the right to take
action on private trees that meet the definition of “hazard trees” to protect
the public. Every effort would be made to cooperate with the citizen before
action was taken.
   (B)   Required process for orders relating to hazard trees.
      (1)   All orders to remove, treat or trim trees or to eradicate or
otherwise control the hazardous condition given pursuant to this section shall
be in writing and authorized by the City Forester. Orders shall be served in
person or by first class mail upon the owner of the property where the trees
are located or, in the case of a public tree, the owner of the property
adjacent to the public right-of-way where the tree is located. The orders shall
afford the owner of the property not less than 20 days from the date of the
mailing of the notice to comply with the order. The order shall also specify
the proper share of the property owner’s financial responsibility for the cost
of eliminating the hazardous condition.
      (2)   If the required action is not taken by the property owner within
the specified time, the City Forester may cause the tree concerned to be
trimmed, removed or treated or the hazardous condition to be eradicated or
otherwise controlled. The costs of the actions shall be apportioned as provided
in §
152.06 of this chapter.
(Ord. passed 5-12-2008; Ord. passed 5-13-2013)
§ 152.06 PRIVATE PROPERTY OWNER’S RESPONSIBILITIES.
   (A)   Protection and maintenance of public trees in boulevard. Any owner of
private property abutting a boulevard shall have the following responsibilities
for public trees located in the boulevard adjacent to the owner’s property:
      (1)   Periodic watering and fertilization of trees, located in the
boulevard, when necessary to maintain good health and vigor; and
      (2)   Protection of trees against damage caused by lawn mowers, weed
trimmers, snow blowers and similar equipment.
   (B)   Apportionment of costs relating to hazard trees.
      (1)   Public trees. In the event that the City Forester determines that a
tree located on the boulevard, as defined in §
152.02 of this chapter, is a hazard, the tree treatment, trimming or removal
will be paid for with the city paying 100% of the cost of the treatment,
trimming or removal. In the event the City Forester determines that a public
tree not located on a “boulevard”, as defined herein, is a hazard, the city
will pay 100% of the cost of treatment, trimming or removal to eliminate the
hazardous condition. The cost involved will include stump removal if determined
necessary by the city.
      (2)   Private trees.
         (a)   In the event the City Forester determines that a private tree is
a hazard to city-owned utilities, the city will assume responsibility for the
cost of removal, treatment, trimming or otherwise eradicating or controlling
the hazardous condition.
         (b)   In the event the City Forester or an approved contractor working
on behalf of the Electric Commission, determines that a private tree is a
hazard to city-owned electric utilities, the disposition of the tree(s) will be
referred to the Electric Utility Commission’s Policy and Guideline for Tree
Trimming/Removal to determine proper procedures for maintaining minimum contact
clearance as well as to determine the property owner’s responsibility, if any.
         (c)   In cases not involving any city owned utility, where the City
Forester has determined that a private tree is a hazard, the owner will be 100%
responsible for removal, treatment, trimming or otherwise eradicating or
controlling the hazardous private tree.
      (3)   Special assessments. If a private hazard tree is not removed or
treated as ordered by the owner of the tree, or if the owner does not
voluntarily pay to the city the cost of removal or treatment by the city, all
costs incurred by the city for trimming, tree removal, treatment or eradicating
or controlling the hazardous condition may be recovered by the city by special
assessment upon the property owner.
(Ord. passed 5-12-2008; Ord. passed 5-13-2013)
§ 152.07 TREE PROTECTION.
   (A)   Abuse or mutilation of public trees. No person shall damage,
transplant, top, remove or mutilate any public tree. Damage may include but is
not limited to the following: posting signs with nails, staples, screws or
other devices; hanging wires of any kind; vehicle damage; weed whip; lawn mower
damage; and poison or toxin applications.
   (B)   Protection of public trees near construction activities. Any public
tree in the immediate vicinity of any excavation, demolition or construction
site of any building, structure, street or utilities work which has potential
for injury, shall be protected from injury by the person responsible for
carrying out the construction activities.
   (C)   Topping. It shall be unlawful for any person, firm or city department
to top any public tree. Trees severely damaged by storms or other causes, or
certain trees under utility wires or other obstructions where other pruning
practices are impractical may be exempted from this subchapter at the
determination of the City Forester.
(Ord. passed 5-12-2008; Ord. passed 5-13-2013) Penalty, see §
152.99
§ 152.08 VIOLATIONS, CLAIMS AND APPEALS.
   (A)   Violations. Any person who violates any provision of this subchapter
or who fails to comply with any notice issued pursuant to provisions of this
subchapter shall be guilty of a misdemeanor. If, as the result of the violation
of any provision of this subchapter, the injury, mutilation or death of a
public tree is caused, the cost of repair or replacement of the tree may be
ordered to be borne by the party in violation. The replacement value of trees
shall be determined in accordance with the latest revision of the International
Society of Arboriculture/Council of Landscape Appraiser’s system.
   (B)   Assessment of claim. In the event that a hazardous condition is not
removed by the date specified in the notice, the City Forester is authorized to
cause the removal of the hazardous condition. The reasonable cost of the
removal shall be filed as a lien against the property on which the hazardous
tree was located.
   (C)   Appeals. Any person who disagrees with any decision of the Forester
may appeal the decision to the Tree Board. The procedure to be followed shall
be as follows.
      (1)   A written notice stating the act of the Forester that is objected
to shall be filed with the Tree Board. The specific section of this subchapter,
if any, that is in question shall be specified. If other standards or
regulations are objected to, such shall be specified in the notice.
      (2)   The Tree Board shall set a date, not later than 30 days after
receipt of the objection to hold a hearing on the objection. Notice of the
hearing shall be sent by first class mail to the objector and published once in
the official city paper.
      (3)   At the hearing, the objector shall set forth their reasons for
objecting and the Forester shall respond. Each of the objector and the Forester
may present the evidence and witnesses as they deem relevant to the issues
involved.
      (4)   The Chair of the Tree Board shall conduct the hearing. A written
decision of the Board containing findings of fact and grounds for the decision
shall be sent to the objector and the Forester within 15 days after the hearing
is completed.
      (5)   If either the objector or the Forester is not satisfied with the
decision of the Tree Board, they may appeal to the City Council who shall
review the matter at the next regular Council meeting after receiving the
notice of appeal. An appeal of the Tree Board decision must be made in writing
within 15 days after the decision has been sent to the parties. A copy of the
appeal must be served on or mailed to the other parties to this appeal. The
Council shall make a decision containing findings of fact and grounds for the
decision within 30 days after the hearing on the appeal has been completed. A
copy of the Council decision shall be mailed to or served on the parties as
soon as it has been made.
      (6)   The decision of the City Council may be appealed to the District
Court of the county. A notice of the appeal must be served on the parties to
this matter within 30 days after the date of the City Council decision has been
mailed to or served on the parties.
(Ord. passed 5-12-2008; Ord. passed 5-13-2013)
ELECTRICAL LINE TREE TRIMMING
§ 152.20 GUIDELINES GENERALLY.
   (A)   In accordance with the mission of the city’s Electrical Utility
Commission to maintain a high quality, safe and reliable electrical
distribution system, the following policy guidelines are established for proper
tree trimming and electrical line maintenance.
   (B)   Any tree that has grown into power lines demands immediate attention.
The city will, arrange for a qualified technician to prune or remove the tree,
eliminating any electrical hazard.
   (C)   Under no circumstances should home owners attempt to prune trees close
to power lines. Trees should never be sheared or topped. Acceptable tree
trimming techniques include the “V-shaped” and “side-trim” methods. The “V-
shaped” method is used on trees growing directly beneath power lines. Entire
branches are removed from the center of the tree while side branches are
allowed to grow up and away from the power lines.
   (D)   Side-trim technique is used where trees are growing alongside power
lines. Only these branches interfering with the lines are removed. Tipping,
removing the ends of side branches, will cause excessive sprouting and may make
it necessary for tree trimming crews to revisit the offending tree frequently.
   (E)   Branches are properly removed at their point of origin or back to a
side branch of sufficient size to assume dominance.
   (F)   Badly deformed trees should be removed. Trees that are very old,
structurally weak or require frequent trimming should be removed.
   (G)   (1)   Dead trees that are a hazard to the primary high voltage lines
will be removed by a qualified technician.
      (2)   Dead or dangerous vegetation that threatens the primary line will
be removed. All vines will be cut and treated on poles and guide wires.
(Ord. passed - -) Penalty, see §
152.99
§ 152.21 PRIMARY HIGH VOLTAGE LINES.
   (A)   Primary high voltage lines will have a minimum clear-cut area under
the line of eight feet (four feet on either side).
   (B)   The exception being where an established privacy or boundary hedge is
present. This will only be allowed when it is not a threat to reliability. If
it becomes a threat, the city has the right to trim or cut what is needed to
restore our reliability. Shrubs shall be permitted outside the eight-foot
boundary. Small trees under 15 feet may be planted 15 feet from the clear cut
zone. Medium trees up to 40 feet may be planted 35 feet from the clear cut zone
and large trees over 40 feet may be planted 45 feet from the clear cut zone.
   (C)   Trees which encroach into the contact clearance area will be trimmed
so that there is at least 12 feet of clearance on either side of the closest
energized phase wire.
