GENERAL PROVISIONS
§ 154.001 AUTHORITY.
   This chapter is adopted pursuant to the authority granted by M.S. Chapter
462, as it may be amended from time to time.
(Ord. passed 8-11-1997, § 1.10; Ord. passed 11- -1997; Ord. passed 2- -2000;
Ord. passed 6- -2001; Ord. passed 7- -2006; Ord. passed 8- -2010)
§ 154.002 CITATION.
   This chapter shall be known and may be cited as the “Rushford Zoning
Ordinance”.
(Ord. passed 8-11-1997, § 1.20; Ord. passed 11- -1997; Ord. passed 2- -2000;
Ord. passed 6- -2001; Ord. passed 7- -2006; Ord. passed 8- -2010)
§ 154.003 PURPOSE.
   It is the purpose of this chapter: to regulate the use of all structures,
lands and waters; to regulate lot coverage, population distribution and
density, and the size and location of all structures in accordance with the
city’s Comprehensive Plan so as to lessen the congestion in streets; to secure
safety from fire, flood, panic and other dangers; to promote health and general
welfare; to provide adequate light and air; to preserve the appearance,
character and unique features of the community; to prevent the overcrowding of
land; to avoid undue concentration of population; to facilitate the adequate
provision of transportation, water, sewer services, schools, parks and other
public requirements; to preserve and improve the character of the area or
neighborhood; to conserve the value of buildings and to encourage the most
appropriate use of land throughout the city.
(Ord. passed 8-11-1997, § 1.30; Ord. passed 11- -1997; Ord. passed 2- -2000;
Ord. passed 6- -2001; Ord. passed 7- -2006; Ord. passed 8- -2010)
§ 154.004 INTERPRETATION OF STANDARDS.
   In their interpretation and application, the provisions of this chapter
shall be held to be minimum requirements, shall be construed in favor of the
city and shall not be deemed as a limitation or repeal of any power granted by
state statutes. Where this chapter imposes a greater restriction than is
imposed or required by other provisions of law or by other rules, regulations
or ordinances, the provision of this chapter shall control.
(Ord. passed 8-11-1997, § 1.40; Ord. passed 11- -1997; Ord. passed 2- -2000;
Ord. passed 6- -2001; Ord. passed 7- -2006; Ord. passed 8- -2010)
§ 154.005 SEVERABILITY.
   If any section, clause, provision or portion of this chapter is adjudged
unconstitutional or invalid by a court of competent jurisdiction, the judgment
shall not affect the remainder of this chapter.
(Ord. passed 8-11-1997, § 1.50; Ord. passed 11- -1997; Ord. passed 2- -2000;
Ord. passed 6- -2001; Ord. passed 7- -2006; Ord. passed 8- -2010)
§ 154.006 DEFINITIONS.
   (A)   Interpretation. For the purpose of this chapter, certain terms and
words are hereby defined. Words used in the present tense shall include the
future, the singular number shall include the plural and the plural number
includes the singular; the word “shall” is mandatory, the word “may” is
permissive; the word “person” includes a firm, association, organization,
partnership, trust, company or corporation as well as an individual; the words
“used” or “occupied” include the words “intended, designed or arranged to be
used or occupied”.
(Ord. passed 8-11-1997, § 2.10; Ord. passed 11- -1997; Ord. passed 2- -2000;
Ord. passed 5- -2000; Ord. passed 6- -2001; Ord. passed 7- -2006; Ord. passed
8- -2010)
   (B)   Definitions. For the purpose of this chapter, the following
definitions shall apply unless the context clearly requires a different
meaning.
      ACCESSORY APARTMENT. A separate unit of housing located within the
confines of another dwelling unit or commercial building. The ACCESSORY
APARTMENT may or may not include kitchen facilities or be connected internally
with the balance of the building. If the ACCESSORY APARTMENT is not connected
internally with the balance of the building, then there shall be bathroom
facilities included in the unit.
      ACCESSORY USE OR STRUCTURE. A subordinate building use or structure, the
use of which is subordinate to that of the main building.
      ADULT. As used in this chapter refers to the persons who have attained
the age of 18 years. Also refers to a business or establishment that is limited
to persons 18 years or older, such as adult video/bookstore, theater or
cabaret.
      ADULT VIDEO/BOOKSTORE. An establishment having as a substantial or
significant portion of its stock in trade, video tapes, movies, books,
magazines and other periodicals which are distinguished or characterized by
their emphasis on matter depicting or describing or relating to specified
sexual activities or specific anatomical areas or an establishment with a
segment or section devoted to the sale or display of the material, or such an
establishment which excludes minors by reason of age.
      ADULT CABARET. A café or restaurant where patrons are entertained by
performers featuring go-go dancers, exotic dancers, strippers or similar
entertainers which excludes minors by reason of age.
      ADULT MINI-MOTION PICTURE THEATER. An enclosed building with a capacity
for less than 50 persons used for presenting motion pictures, slides or
photographic reproductions which:
         (a)   Is distinguished or characterized by an emphasis on matters
depicting, describing or relating to specified sexual activities or specified
anatomical areas, for observance by patrons therein; or
         (b)   Excludes minors by reason of age.
      ADULT MOTION PICTURE THEATER. An enclosed building with a capacity of 50
or more persons used predominantly for presenting motion pictures, slides or
photographic reproductions which:
         (a)   Is distinguished or characterized by an emphasis on matters
depicting, describing or relating to specified sexual activities or specified
anatomical areas, for observation by patrons therein; or
         (b)   Excludes minors by reason of age.
      AGRICULTURE. The production, keeping or maintenance, for sale, lease or
personal use, of plants and animals including, but not limited to, farming,
dairying, pasturage, apiculture, horticulture, floriculture, viticulture and
animal and poultry husbandry and the necessary accessory uses for packing,
treating or storing the produce.
      ALLEY. A service way, other than a street, which affords only a secondary
means of public access to abutting property, and is not intended for general
traffic circulation.
      APARTMENT. A room or suite of rooms which is designed for, intended for
or occupied by one family and equipped with cooking facilities.
      APARTMENT HOUSE. A building or portion thereof designed for or occupied
by three or more families.
      APPROVED PLAN. A plan which has been granted final approval by the
appropriate approving authority.
      AUTOMOBILE SALES. The sale or rental of new and used automobiles and
other motor vehicles in operating condition and the repair and servicing of the
vehicles.
      AWNING SIGN. A sign on or attached to a temporary retractable or fixed
shelter composed of non-rigid material, except for the supporting framework and
braced entirely from the exterior wall of the building.
      BASEMENT. A story having one-half or more of its floor-to-ceiling height
above the average level of the adjoining ground and with a floor-to-ceiling
height of not less than six and one-half feet. A BASEMENT shall be counted as a
story if used or intended to be used for dwelling or business purposes. (See
CELLAR for difference.)
      BED AND BREAKFAST LODGING. A facility other than a hotel or motel where,
for compensation, lodging and meals may be offered for temporary occupancy.
      BLOCK. A tract of land bounded by streets or a combination of streets and
public land, cemeteries, railroad right-of-way, waterways or any other barrier
to the continuity to development within the political jurisdictional limits of
the city.
      BLUFF. The area between the United States Geological Survey contour
interval of 740 and 1,140 feet with a topographic feature such as a hill, cliff
or embankment with an average slope of 25% or greater with a vertical rise or
drop of 25 feet or greater.
      BLUFF IMPACT ZONE. All land located between the toe and the top of the
bluff and the land located within 20 feet measured horizontally beyond the
lowest point of the toe of the bluff.
      BLUFF, TOE OF. The point on a bluff where there is, as visually observed,
a clearly identifiable break in the slope, from gentler to steeper slope above.
If no break in the slope is apparent or if there is disagreement in the
breaking point, the TOE OF THE BLUFF shall be the lowest end of a 50-foot
segment, measured on the ground, with an average slope of 20% or greater.
      BLUFF, TOP OF. The point on a bluff where there is, as visually observed,
a clearly identifiable break in the slope, from steeper to gentler slope above.
If no break in the slope is apparent or if there is disagreement in the
breaking point, the TOP OF THE BLUFF shall be the highest end of a 50-foot
segment, measured on the ground, with an average slope of 20% or greater.
      BLUFF SLOPE. The average slope between the United States Geological
Survey contour intervals of 740 and 1,140 mean sea level.
      BOARD. The Rushford Board of Appeals and Adjustments, as described in §
154.025 of this chapter.
      BOARDING HOUSE. A building other than a hotel where, for compensation,
lodging and meals are provided for long-term residents.
      BOULEVARD. The portion of street right-of-way between the curb line and
lot line.
      BUILDING. Any structure having a roof supported by columns or walls and
intended for the shelter, housing or enclosure of any individual, animal,
process, equipment, goods or materials of any kind or nature. When a structure
is divided into separate parts, each part may be deemed a separate BUILDING.
      BUILDING, HEIGHT OF. The vertical distance measured from the curb level
to the highest point of the roof surface if a flat roof, to the deck line of
mansard roofs and the mean height level between eaves and ridge for gable, hip
and gambrel roofs. For a building on a sloped or uneven site, the vertical
distance of a building shall be measured from the average of the finished grade
of the four outer corners of the building to the highest point of the roof.
      BUILDING LINE. An imaginary line parallel to the front lot line over
which no portion of any building may extend and which is a distance from the
front lot line equal to the depth of the front yard required for the district
in which the lot is located.
      BUILDING, MAIN. A building in which is conducted the principal use of the
lot upon which it is situated.
      BULK. The magnitude or volume of a building.
      CARPORT. A roofed structure providing space for the parking of motor
vehicles and enclosed on not more than two sides. For the purpose of this
chapter, a CARPORT shall be considered as a garage.
      CELLAR. A story having more than one-half of its height below the curb
level or below the highest level of the adjoining ground or with a floor-to-
ceiling height of less than six and one-half feet. A CELLAR shall not be
counted as a story for purposes of height measurement.
      CLINIC. An establishment occupied by one or more members of a healing
profession and used for practice of the profession. A CLINIC shall not include
any in-patient care other than incidental.
      COMMISSION. The Planning Commission of the City of Rushford, Minnesota.
      COMPREHENSIVE PLAN. A document or series of documents adopted by the City
Council setting forth policies for the future of the city.
      CONDITIONAL/INTERIM USE. A temporary use permitted in a particular zoning
district only upon showing that the use in a specified location will comply
with all the conditions and standards for the location or operation of the use
and such additional temporary conditions as required by the Planning
Commission.
      CORNER SIDE YARD. A space extending from the front yard to the rear yard
between the principal building and the side lot line measured perpendicular
from the side lot line to the closest point of the principal building on a
comer lot or parcel of land abutting upon two or more streets at their
intersection, or upon two parts of the same street forming an interior angle of
less than 135 degrees.
      COUNCIL. The City Council of Rushford, Minnesota.
      CURB LEVEL. The level of the established curb in front of the building
measured at the center of the front. Where no curb has been established, the
City Engineer shall establish the CURB LEVEL or its equivalent for the purposes
of this chapter.
      DECK. A wooden or metal structure attached to the primary building, not
fully enclosed or heated.
      DECKLINE. The intersection of two roof surfaces of a mansard roof forming
the highest horizontal line of the steeper roof slope.
      DEVELOPMENT. The act of building structures and installing site
improvements.
      DIKE. A human-made raised earthen embankment constructed for flood
protection, designed to control the flow of water. Also commonly referred to as
a LEVEE.
      DIKE EASEMENT. A grant of a property right by the property owner to
another party for dike purposes, including maintenance and repair.
      DISTRICT. A part, zone or geographic area within the municipality within
which certain zoning or development regulations apply.
      DRIVEWAY. A private roadway providing access for vehicles to a parking
space, garage, dwelling or other structure.
      DWELLING. A permanent building or portion thereof used exclusively for
human residence occupancy, including one-family, two-family and multiple
dwellings, but not including hotels of lodging or boarding houses, bed and
breakfast lodging.
      DWELLING, ATTACHED. A one-family dwelling attached to two or more one-
family dwellings at one or more sides by a common vertical wall or walls.
      DWELLING, DETACHED. A dwelling which is not attached to any other
dwelling by any means on the same lot.
      DWELLING, MULTIPLE-FAMILY. A building or portion thereof designed for or
occupied by three or more dwelling units.
      DWELLING, SINGLE-FAMILY. A detached building designed for or occupied
exclusively by one family.
      DWELLING, TWO-FAMILY. A building designed for or occupied exclusively by
two families living independently of each other. Sometimes called a DUPLEX.
      DWELLING UNIT. One or more rooms in a residential building or residential
portion of a building which are arranged, designed, used or intended for use by
one family, and which includes cooking space, sleeping area and lawful sanitary
facilities reserved for the occupants thereof.
      EASEMENT. A grant of a property right by the property owner to another
party.
      ERECTED. Attached, altered, built, constructed, reconstructed, enlarged
or moved, and shall include the painting of wall signs, but does not include
copy changes on any sign.
      FACTORY-BUILT HOUSING. A factory-built structure designed for long-term
residential use. For the purposes of these regulations, FACTORY-BUILT HOUSING
consists of three types: modular homes; mobile homes; and manufactured homes.
      FACTORY-BUILT STRUCTURE. Any structure which is, wholly or in substantial
part, made, fabricated, formed or assembled in manufacturing facilities for
installation or assembly and installation on a building site.
      FAMILY. One or more persons occupying premises and living as a single
housekeeping unit, as distinguished from a group occupying a boarding house, a
lodging house, nursing home, rest home, club or hotel, as herein defined.
      FARM. An area comprised of ten or more acres which is used for the
growing of and storing of the usual agricultural products such as vegetables,
fruits and grains, and their storage on the area, as well as for the raising
thereon of livestock including, but not limited to, poultry and farm animals
such as horses, cattle, sheep and swine.
      FENCE. An artificially constructed barrier of any material or combination
of materials erected to enclose or screen areas of land.
      FLOOR AREA. When prescribed as the basis of measurement for off-street
parking spaces and loading berths for any use, shall mean the sum of the gross
horizontal areas of the several floors of the building, or portion thereof,
devoted to the use, including accessory storage areas located within selling or
working space such as counters, racks or closets, and any basement or cellar
floor area devoted to retailing activities, to the production or processing of
goods or to business or professional offices. It shall not include: floor area
devoted primarily to storage purposes (except as otherwise noted herein); floor
area devoted to off-street parking or loading facilities, including aisles,
ramps and maneuvering space; or basement or cellar floor area other than area
devoted to retailing activities, to the production or processing of goods or to
business or professional offices.
      FLOOR AREA RATIO (FAR). The floor area ratio is the floor area of the
building or buildings on a lot divided by the land area. For the purpose of
determining the FLOOR AREA RATIO, the “floor area” of a building is the sum of
the gross horizontal areas of all floors of the building measured from the
exterior faces of the exterior walls or from the center line of walls
separating two buildings. The floor area of a building shall include basement
floor area, elevator shafts and stairwells at each floor penthouse, attic space
having headroom of seven feet (2.1 m) or more, interior balconies and
mezzanines, enclosed porches and floor area devoted to accessory uses. Space
devoted to mechanical equipment, parking or loading shall not be included in
the floor area. The floor area for enclosed space having a floor to ceiling
height in excess of 20 feet (6 m) shall be computed on the basis that each 15
feet (4.6 m) of height shall be equal to one floor for the purpose of
computation. Space intended for recreational use in apartment buildings may be
excluded from these calculations if the Planning Commission finds that the
recreational uses are suitable for the size and use of the proposed structure.
      FRONTAGE. All the property on one side of a street or place between two
intersecting streets or places (crossing or terminating) measured along the
line of the street or place, or if the street or place be dead ended, then all
of the property abutting on one side between an intersecting street or place at
the dead end of the street or place.
      GARAGE. An accessory building primarily for storage or repair of self-
propelled vehicles.
      HEIGHT. The vertical distance measured from the curb level to the highest
point of the roof surface if a flat roof, to the deck line of mansard roofs,
and the mean height level between eaves and ridge for gable, hip and gambrel
roofs. For a building on a sloped or uneven site, the vertical distance of a
building shall be measured from the average of the finished grade of the four
outer corners of the building to the highest point of the roof.
      HOME OCCUPATION. Any gainful occupation or profession engaged in by the
legal resident of a dwelling, at or from the dwelling or from an accessory
building.
      HORIZONTAL PLANE. A plane tangent to a level surface.
      HOTEL. A building occupied by individuals as the temporary lodging place
for compensation.
      INDUSTRY. Those fields of economic activity including forestry, fishing,
hunting and trapping, mining, construction, manufacturing, transportation,
communication, electric, gas and sanitary services and wholesale trade.
      INSTITUTION. An organization established for study, research, public
benefit or social welfare.
      JUNK YARD. An establishment or place of business for storing, handling,
keeping, buying or selling of old or scrap, rope, rags, batteries, paper,
plastic, trash, rubber or outside storage of two or more unlicensed motor
vehicles or other vehicles or machinery other than the storage of materials
which is incidental or accessory to any primary use on the same lot.
      LOADING or LOADING BERTH. An off-street space provided for the loading or
unloading of cargo from a motor vehicle.
      LOT. A parcel of land at least sufficient in size to meet minimum zoning
requirements for use and area and to provide the yards and other open spaces as
are herein required. A LOT shall have frontage on a public street and may
consist of:
         (a)   A single lot of record;
         (b)   A portion of a lot of record;
         (c)   A combination of complete lots of record, or complete lots of
record and portions of lots of record, or of portions of lots of record; and
         (d)   A parcel of land described by metes and bounds; provided that,
in no case of division or combination shall any residual lot or parcel be
created which does not meet the requirements of this chapter.
      LOT, CORNER. A lot situated at the junction of two or more intersecting
streets.
      LOT, DEPTH. The mean horizontal distance between the front and the rear
lot lines.
      LOT, DOUBLE FRONTAGE. An interior lot having frontage on two parallel or
approximately parallel streets or places.
      LOT, INTERIOR. A lot other than a corner lot.
      LOT, KEY. The first lot to the rear of a comer lot, the front line of
which is a continuation of the side line of the comer lot, exclusive of the
width of any alley and fronting on the street which intersects or intercepts
the street on which the comer lot fronts.
      LOT LINE. A line of record bounding a lot which divides one lot from
another lot or from a public street or other public space.
      LOT LINE, FRONT. The line separating the lot from a street.
      LOT LINE, REAR. The lot line opposite and most distant from the front lot
line.
      LOT LINE, SIDE. Any lot line other than a front or rear lot line. A SIDE
LOT LINE separating a lot from a street is called a SIDE STREET LOT LINE. A
SIDE LOT LINE separating a lot from another lot or lots is called an INTERIOR
SIDE LOT LINE.
      LOT OF RECORD. A lot which is a part of a subdivision, the map of which
has been recorded in the office of the County Recorder.
      LOT, ZONING. A single tract of land located within a single block which
(at the time of filing for a zoning permit) is designated by its owner or
developer as a tract to be used, developed or built upon as a unit, under
single ownership or control. Therefore, a ZONING LOT OR LOTS may or may not
coincide with a lot of record.
      MANUFACTURED HOME. A structure, transportable in one or more sections,
which in the traveling mode, is eight body feet or more in width or 40 body
feet or more in length, or when erected on site, is 320 or more square feet and
which is built on a permanent chassis and designed to be used as a dwelling
with or without a permanent foundation when connected to the required
utilities, and includes the plumbing, heating, air conditioning and electrical
systems contained therein; except that, the term includes any structure which
meets all the requirements and with respect to which the manufacturer
voluntarily files a certification required by the Secretary of State and
complies with the standards established under this chapter.
      MASSAGE. Any method of treating the external parts of a human body by
rubbing, stroking, kneading, tapping or vibrating with the hand or any
instrument for any form of consideration or gratuity.
      MASSAGE ESTABLISHMENT.
         (a)   Any establishment having a fixed place of business, which
excludes minors by reason of age, where massages are administered for any form
of consideration or gratuity, including, but not limited to, massage parlors,
health clubs, sauna baths and steam baths. This definition shall not be
construed to include:
            1.   Persons licensed by the state under the provisions of M.S.
Chapters 148, 148B, 150A, 152, 153, 157 or 158, and M.S. §§ 604.20 to 604.205,
as they may be amended from time to time, when performing massage therapy or
massage services as a part of the profession or trade for which licensed;
            2.   Persons performing massage therapy or massage services under
the direction of a person licensed as described in division (a)1. above;
            3.   Persons performing massage therapy or massage services upon a
person pursuant to the written instruction or order of a licensed physician;
            4.   Nurses aides, technicians and attendants at any hospital or
health care facility licensed pursuant to M.S. Chapter 145A, as it may be
amended from time to time, in the course of their employment and under the
supervision of the administrator thereof or of a person licensed as described
in division (a)1. above; and/or
            5.   An athletic coach or trainer in any accredited public or
private secondary school, junior college or university or employed by a
professional or semi-professional athletic team or organization, in the course
of his or her employment as the coach or trainer.
         (b)   This definition shall not be construed to include: a non-profit
organization operating a community center, swimming pool, tennis court or other
educational, cultural, recreational and athletic facilities for the welfare of
the residents of the area.
      MOBILE HOME. Any vehicle without motive power used or so manufactured or
constructed as to permit its being used as a conveyance upon the public streets
and highways and so designed, constructed or reconstructed as will permit the
vehicle to be used as a place for human habitation by one or more persons; but
shall also include any such vehicle with motive power not registered as a motor
vehicle in Minnesota. A MOBILE HOME is factory-built housing, built on a
chassis. A MOBILE HOME shall not be construed to be a travel trailer or other
form of recreational vehicle. A MOBILE HOME shall be construed to remain a
mobile home, subject to all regulations applying thereto, whether or not
wheels, axles, hitch or other appurtenances of mobility are removed and
regardless of the nature of the foundation provided. However, certain MOBILE
HOMES may be classified as “manufactured homes”. Nothing in this chapter shall
be construed as permitting a mobile home in other than an approved mobile home
park unless the MOBILE HOME is classified as a “manufactured home”. A MOBILE
HOME shall be a minimum of ten feet in width and 32 feet in length.
      MOBILE HOME PARK. A parcel of land licensed by the state under single
ownership which has been planned and improved for the placement of mobile homes
for non-transient use containing not less than three lots.
      MOBILE HOME STAND. A part of an individual mobile home lot which has been
reserved for placement of a mobile home and any appurtenances thereto.
      MODULAR HOME. Factory-built housing certified as meeting the State
Building Code as applicable to modular housing. Once certified by the state,
MODULAR HOMES shall be subject to the same standards as site-built homes.
      MOTEL. A permanent building or group of buildings so arranged or designed
primarily for temporary occupancy, so laid out as to provide space for parking
vehicles used by the traveling public.
      NATURAL SLOPES. That slope established by USGS topographic map and
existing prior to grading of any kind.
      NON-CONFORMING USE. A use lawfully in existence on the effective date of
this chapter and not conforming to the regulations for the district in which it
is situated; except that, the use is not non-conforming if it would be
authorized under special use permit where located.
      NURSING HOME. A building other than a hotel or hospital where, for
compensation, meals and lodging and physical care are provided for three or
more persons. This definition shall include rest homes and similar
establishments.
      OFFICE. A room or building where administrative or clerical work or
business is conducted (e.g., engineer, doctor, dentist, attorney, real estate
broker, insurance broker, architect, accounting, editorial or other similar
work is conducted).
      OPEN SPACE. Space on a lot not covered by buildings adjacent to a lot
which provides a necessary and desirable function in relation to the adjoining
residential use.
      OPEN SPACE RATIO (OS) METHOD OF COMPUTATION. The open space area is the
lot area less the total ground area covered by buildings and parking and
driveway areas, plus one-half of all exterior space which is open on its sides
to weather, but not open to the sky (carports, exterior balconies), plus all
roof areas which have been suitably improved for use by occupants, calculated
in a direct ratio to the lot area.
      ORDINANCE. A municipally adopted law or regulation.
      OVERHANG. The part of a roof or wall which extends beyond the facade of a
lower wall.
      PARKING BAY. A bay shall include an aisle and the parking spaces adjacent
to the aisle.
      PERMITTED USE. A use which by right is specifically authorized in a
particular zoning district.
      PLACE. An open unoccupied space other than a street or alley permanently
reserved as the principal means of access to abutting property.
      PORCH, OPEN. A roofed projection which has no more than 50% of each
outside wall area enclosed by a building or siding material other than meshed
screens.
      PRESCHOOL or DAY CARE CENTER. The facilities of any dwelling institution
or organization which, for profit or non-profit, are used in the temporary care
of six children or more at any one time.
      PRINCIPAL BUILDING. A non-accessory building in which the principal use
of the lot on which it is located is conducted.
      PRIVATE CLUB OR LODGE. An association of persons who are bona fide
members paying annual dues, which owns, hires or leases a building or portion
thereof, except a fraternity or sorority, the use of the premises being
restricted to members and their guests. It is permissible to serve food and
meals to members and their guests on the premises, provided adequate dining
room space and kitchen facilities are available and are operated in compliance
with state and local municipal laws.
      PRIVATE STREET. Privately owned street, driveway or access road which
serves more than one residence.
      PUBLIC RIGHT-OF-WAY. A public strip of land acquired by reservation,
dedication, forced dedication, prescription or condemnation and intended to be
occupied or is occupied by a road, crosswalk, electric transmission lines, oil
or gas pipeline, water line, sanitary sewer or storm sewer and other similar
uses.
      RETAINING WALL. A structure constructed to hold back or support an
earthen bank.
      RIDGELINE. The uppermost boundary of the conservancy district which is
represented by the contour line of 1,140 feet as shown on a United State
Geological Survey Topographic Map.
      SELECTIVE CUTTING. The removal of single scattered trees; provided, a
continuous tree cover is maintained.
      SERVIENT ESTATE. Land burdened by an easement, other than the dominant
estate. A dominant estate is one that has rights such as an easement in another
piece of land.
      SETBACK. The minimum required horizontal distance between every
structure, including the building overhang or furthest projecting portion of
the structure and lot line of the lot on which it is located.
      SEXUAL ACTIVITY ESTABLISHMENT. An establishment which excludes minors by
reason of age, used for the display of live presentations distinguished or
characterized by an emphasis on matter depicting or describing or involving
specified sexual activities or specified anatomical areas. The provisions of
this definition shall not apply to a theater, concert hall, art center, museum
or similar establishment which is primarily devoted to presentations
distinguished or characterized by an emphasis on matter depicting or describing
or relating to specified sexual activities or specified anatomical areas.
      SHOPPING CENTER/STRIP MALL. A planned retail and service area under
single ownership, management or control, characterized by a concentrated
grouping of stores and compatible uses with various facilities to be used in
common, such as ingress and egress roads, parking accommodations and the like.
      SIGN. Any identification, description, illustration or device which is
visible from any public place or is located on private property and exposed to
the public and which directs attention to a product, service, place, activity,
person, institution, business or solicitation, including any permanently
installed or situated merchandise; or any emblem, painting, banner, pennant,
placard or temporary sign designed to advertise, identify or convey information
with the exception of window displays and national flags. For the purpose of
this chapter, SIGNS shall also include all sign structures.
      SIGN, ABANDONED. A sign which no longer correctly directs or exhorts any
person or advertises a business, lessor, owner, product or activity which is no
longer available.
      SIGN AREA. The area enclosed by one continuous line, connecting the
extreme points or edges of a sign. The area shall be determined using the
largest area or silhouette visible at any one time from any one point. This
area does not include the main supporting sign structure.
      SIGN, BANNER. A temporary sign composed of lightweight material not
enclosed in a rigid frame, secured or mounted so as to allow movement of the
sign caused by movement of the wind.
      SIGN, GROUND. Any sign which is supported by one or more structures,
uprights, poles or braces in or upon the ground and independent of support from
any building.
      SIGN, OFF-PREMISES. Any sign that advertises goods, products, services or
facilities or directs persons to a different location from where the sign is
located.
      SIGN, ON-PREMISES. A sign which carries only advertisements strictly
incidental to a lawful use of the premises on which it is located, including
signs indicating the business transacted, services rendered, goods sold or
produced on the premises, name of the business, name of the person or firm or
corporation occupying the premises.
      SIGN, PORTABLE. Any sign not permanently attached to a building,
structure or the ground, capable of being moved at periodic intervals.
      SIGN, PROJECTING. A sign, other than a wall sign, which projects from and
is supported by a wall of a building or structure.
      SIGN, PUBLIC SERVICE. Any sign intended primarily to promote items of
general interest to the community such as time, temperature and data,
atmospheric conditions, news or traffic control and the like.
      SIGN, ROOF. A sign erected upon or above a roof or parapet of a building
or structure.
      SIGN, SWINGING. A sign installed on an arm or spar that is not, in
addition, permanently fastened to an adjacent wall or upright pole.
      SIGN, WALL. Any sign attached to, painted upon, or erected against the
wall of a building or structure, with the exposed face of the sign in a plane
parallel to the plane of the wall.
      SOLAR ACCESS EASEMENT. An easement, the purpose of which is to provide
continued access to incident sunlight necessary to operate a solar collector.
      SOLAR COLLECTOR. A device or structural feature of a building that
collects solar energy and that is part of a system for the collection, storage
and distribution of solar energy. For purposes of this chapter, a greenhouse is
a SOLAR COLLECTOR.
      SPECIAL USE. A use of land, water or building which is allowable only
after the issuance of a permit by the Board of Adjustment and Appeals.
      SPECIFIED ANATOMICAL AREAS.
         (a)   Less than completely and opaquely covered:
            1.   Human genitals, pubic region;
            2.   Buttocks; and
            3.   Female breast below a point immediately above the top of the
areola.
         (b)   Human male genitals in a discernible turgid state even if
completely and opaquely covered.
      SPECIFIED SEXUAL ACTIVITIES.
         (a)   Human genitals in a state of sexual stimulation or arousal;
         (b)   Acts of human masturbation, sexual intercourse or sodomy; and
         (c)   Fondling or other erotic touching of human genitals, pubic
region, buttocks or female breasts.
      STEEP SLOPES. Land where agricultural activity or development is either
not recommended or poorly suited due to slope steepness and the site’s soil
characteristics, as mapped and described in available soil surveys or other
technical reports, unless appropriate design and construction techniques and
farming practices are used. Where specific information is not available, STEEP
SLOPES are lands having average slopes over 12%, as measured over a horizontal
distance of 50 feet, that are not bluffs.
      STORY. The portion of a building, other than a cellar, included between
the surface of any floor and the surface of the floor next above it or, if
there be no floor above it, then the space between the floor and the ceiling
next above it.
      STORY, HALF. A story under a gable, hip or gambrel roof, the wall plates
of which on at least two opposite exterior walls are not more than two feet
above the floor of the story.
      STREET. A public or private thoroughfare which affords the primary means
of access to abutting property.
      STRUCTURAL ALTERATION. Any change in the supporting members of a building
such as bearing walls, columns, beams or girders or any substantial change in
the dimension or configurations of the roof or in the exterior walls.
      STRUCTURE. Any thing constructed or erected, the use of which requires
more or less permanent location on the ground or attached to something having a
permanent location on the ground.
      SURFACE WATER. Water on the earth’s surface exposed to the atmosphere as
rivers, lakes and streams.
      USE. The primary purpose or activity for which land or a building or
structure thereon is designed, arranged or intended or for which it is or may
be occupied or maintained.
      UTILITY BUILDING. An accessory building not to exceed 144 square feet in
floor area normally used in a residential zone.
      VARIANCE. A relaxation of the terms of the zoning code where the variance
will not be contrary to a public interest and where, owing to conditions
peculiar to the property and not the result of the actions of the applicant, a
literal enforcement of the zoning code would result in unnecessary and undue
hardship.
      WATER RUN OFF. The portion of the rainfall, melted snow or irrigation
water that flows across ground surfaces and eventually is returned to surface
water.
      YARD.
         (a)   An open space on the same lot with a building occupied, which is
unobstructed by any portion of a structure from 24 inches above the ground
upward, except as otherwise provided herein.
         (b)   The measurement of a YARD shall be construed as the minimum
horizontal distance between the lot line and the building or structure.
      YARD, FRONT. A yard extending across the full width of the lot measured
between the front line of the lot and the nearest point of a building.
      YARD, REAR. A yard extending across the full width of the lot and
measured between the rear line of the lot and the nearest point of the
principal building.
      YARD, SIDE. A yard between the nearest point of the building and the side
line of the lot and extending from the front yard to the rear yard.
      ZONING ADMINISTRATOR. The local official responsible for administration
and implementation of the zoning regulations of the city or his or her
authorized representative.
(Ord. passed 8-11-1997, § 2.20; Ord. passed 11- -1997; Ord. passed 2- -2000;
Ord. passed 5-22-2000; Ord. passed 6- -2001; Ord. passed 7- -2006; Ord. passed
8- -2010)
ADMINISTRATION AND ENFORCEMENT
§ 154.020 COMPLIANCE.
   No structure, land or water shall hereafter be used and no structure or part
thereof shall hereafter be located, erected, moved, reconstructed, extended,
enlarged, converted or structurally altered without a zoning permit and/or
certificate of zoning compliance and without full compliance with the
provisions of this chapter and all other applicable local, county and state
regulations.
(Ord. passed 8-11-1997, § 20.10; Ord. passed 11- -1997; Ord. passed 2- -2000;
Ord. passed 6- -2001; Ord. passed 7- -2006; Ord. passed 8- -2010) Penalty, see
§
154.999
§ 154.021 ZONING ADMINISTRATOR.
   The Administrator of the Zoning Ordinance shall enforce the provisions of
this chapter and shall have the following powers and duties in connection
therewith:
   (A)   To issue all permits and certificates required by this chapter;
   (B)   To cause any building, structure, land or any thing located on or
attached hereto to be inspected and examined and to order in writing the
remedying of any condition found to exist therein in violation of any provision
of this chapter; and
   (C)   To act as advisor to the Board of Adjustment and to carry out and
enforce any decisions or determinations of the Board.
(Ord. passed 8-11-1997, § 20.20; Ord. passed 11- -1997; Ord. passed 2- -2000;
Ord. passed 6- -2001; Ord. passed 7- -2006; Ord. passed 8- -2010)
§ 154.022 VIOLATIONS.
   (A)   It shall be unlawful to construct any structure or building in
violation of any of the provisions of this chapter.
   (B)   In case of any violation, the City Council, the Zoning Administrator,
the Board of Appeals and Adjustment, the City Planning Commission or any
property owner who would specifically be damaged by the violation may institute
appropriate action or proceedings to enjoin a violation of this chapter.
(Ord. passed 8-11-1997, § 20.30; Ord. passed 11- -1997; Ord. passed 2- -2000;
Ord. passed 6- -2001; Ord. passed 7- -2006; Ord. passed 8- -2010) Penalty, see
§
154.999
§ 154.023 ZONING PERMITS; FEES.
   (A)   No structure shall hereafter be erected, reconstructed, structurally
altered, enlarged, added to or removed, nor shall an excavation for any such
structure be commenced until and unless a zoning permit therefor shall have
been issued by the Zoning Administrator.
   (B)   An application for a zoning permit shall be filed with the Zoning
Administrator and shall be accompanied by a drawing or plat, in duplicate,
drawn to scale and showing the legal description of the lot and the proposed
building and their dimensions; the exact location of the proposed building on
the lot and the use for which the building and any existing building is
designed or intended; location, dimension and present use of any existing
building on the same lot; street location, other public or private drives and
generalized parking areas; existing topography and illustration of existing
drainage considerations (proposed grades not necessary at this point); existing
tree masses and other geologic or environmentally important characteristics;
proposed private and public ownership boundaries including proposed private
lots, and such other information as the Zoning Administrator shall require for
the proper enforcement of this chapter. All zoning permits issued under this
chapter shall expire and be null and void on the date which shall be 12 months
after issuance, unless work shall have been commenced under the permit prior to
the date. No zoning permit shall be issued to anyone who is in violation of any
part of this chapter.
(Ord. passed 8-11-1997, § 20.50; Ord. passed 11- -1997; Ord. passed 2- -2000;
Ord. passed 6- -2001; Ord. passed 7- -2006; Ord. passed 8- -2010)
§ 154.024 CERTIFICATES OF ZONING COMPLIANCE.
   (A)   No land shall be occupied or used, and no building hereafter erected
or structurally altered shall be occupied or used in whole or in part for any
purpose whatsoever, until a certificate is issued by the Zoning Administrator
stating that the building and use comply with the provisions of this chapter.
No change of use shall be made in any building or part thereof, now or
hereafter erected or structurally altered, without a permit being issued
therefor by the Zoning Administrator. No permit shall be issued to make a
change unless the changes are in conformity with the provisions of this
chapter.
   (B)   Nothing in this part shall prevent the continuance of a non-conforming
use as hereinbefore authorized unless a discontinuance is necessary for the
safety of life or property.
   (C)   Certificates of occupancy and compliance shall be applied for
coincidentally with the application for a zoning permit and shall be issued
within 30 days after the lawful erection or alteration of the building is
completed. A record of all certificates shall be kept on file in the office of
the Zoning Administrator, and copies shall be furnished on request to any
persons having a proprietary or tenancy interest in the building affected.
   (D)   No permits for excavation for, or the erection or alteration of any
building shall be issued before the application has been made for certificate
of compliance; and no building or premises shall be occupied until that
certificate and permit is issued.
   (E)   A temporary certificate of zoning compliance may be issued by the
administrative official for a period not exceeding six months during
alterations or partial occupancy of a building pending its completion or
completion of site improvements; provided that, the temporary certificate may
require such conditions and safeguards as will protect the safety of the
occupants and the public.
(Ord. passed 8-11-1997, § 20.60; Ord. passed 11- -1997; Ord. passed 2- -2000;
Ord. passed 6- -2001; Ord. passed 7- -2006; Ord. passed 8- -2010)
§ 154.025 BOARD OF APPEALS AND ADJUSTMENTS.
   (A)   Board created. There is hereby created a Board of Appeals and
Adjustments consisting of one member of the Planning Commission and four
citizens, all appointed by the Council. The term of the member of the Planning
Commission shall be coterminous with his or her term on the commission. Each
other member shall serve for a term of four years; except that, terms of the
initial members shall be so arranged that the term of one member expires each
year. Each member shall serve until his or her successor is appointed. Members
shall serve without compensation. Staff services for the Board shall be
furnished by the Zoning Administrator. The Zoning Administrator and Fire Chief
will serve as ex officio members of the Board of Appeals and Adjustments.
   (B)   Procedure. Three members of the Board constitute a quorum and a
majority of all members is necessary for an decision of the Board. The Board
shall elect one of its members as Chairperson and appoint a Secretary who may,
but need not be one of its members. Subject to the provisions of this chapter,
the Board may adopt rules necessary to the conduct of its affairs. The
Chairperson or, in his or her absence, the Acting Chairman may administer oaths
to witnesses. All meetings shall be open to the public. The Board shall keep
minutes of its proceedings, showing the vote of each member upon each question,
or if absent or failing to vote, indicating the fact. The Board shall keep
records of its examinations and other official actions.
   (C)   Powers and duties of Board. The Board shall have the power and duty of
hearing and deciding, subject to appeal to the City Council as herein provided,
appeals or requests in the following cases:
      (1)   Appeals where it is alleged that there is an error in any order,
requirement, decision or determination made by an administrative officer in the
enforcement of this chapter; and
      (2)   Requests for variances from the literal provisions of this chapter
in instances where their strict enforcement would cause undue hardship because
of circumstances unique to the individual property under consideration.
   (D)   Appeal.
      (1)   An appeal for administrative review as specified in division (C)
above may be taken to the Board by any person, firm or corporation or any city
officer, department or agency affected by a decision of the Zoning
Administrator. Such an appeal shall be taken by filing a notice of appeal
specifying the grounds thereof with the Zoning Administrator, and the Board
within 30 days after the decision. If the appeal is not taken by the owner of
the property which is the subject matter of the decision appealed from, the
notice shall not be so filed until after it has been served upon the owner
either in person or by mail.
      (2)   The Zoning Administrator shall forthwith transmit to the Board all
papers constituting the record upon which the action appealed from was taken.
An appeal for an administrative review or a variance stays all proceedings,
including criminal proceedings, in furtherance of the action appealed from
unless the Zoning Administrator certifies to the Board that by reason of facts
state in the certificate a stay would, in his or her opinion, cause imminent
peril to life or property. In that case, the proceedings shall not be stayed
otherwise than by a restraining order granted by a court of competent
jurisdiction. An application for a variance may be filed by the owner of the
affected property at any time.
   (E)   Variances.
      (1)   No variance shall be granted to allow a use not permissible under
the terms of this chapter in the district involved, except the temporary use of
a one-family dwelling as a two-family dwelling for not to exceed one year.
      (2)   In granting a variance, the Board or the Council on appeal, may
prescribe appropriate conditions in conformity with this chapter.
      (3)   When the conditions are made part of the terms under which the
variance is granted, violation of the conditions is a violation of this
chapter.
      (4)   A variance shall not be granted by the Board or by the Council on
appeal, unless it conforms to the following standards:
         (a)   Special conditions and circumstances exist which are peculiar to
the land, structure or building involved and do not result from the actions of
the petitioner;
         (b)   Literal interpretation of the provisions of this chapter would
deprive the petitioner of rights commonly enjoyed by other properties in the
same district under the terms of this chapter;
         (c)   Granting the variance requested will not confer on the applicant
any special privilege that is denied by this chapter to other lands, structures
or buildings in the same district; and
         (d)   The proposed variance will not impair an adequate supply of
light and air to adjacent property or unreasonably diminish or impair
established property values within the surrounding area, or in any other
respect impair the public health, safety or welfare of the residents of the
city.
   (F)   Decision by the Board.
      (1)   Upon filing with the Board, a request for variance or an appeal
from an administrative order or determination, the Board shall set a date for
public hearing thereon, which shall be no later than 60 days from the date of
filing and shall hear the persons as wish to be heard, either in person or by
agent or attorney.
      (2)   Notice of any such hearing shall be mailed not less than ten days
before the date of hearing to the person who filed the appeal or request, and
in the case of a request for a variance, to each owner of property situated
wholly or partly within 350 feet of the property to which the variance relates,
insofar as the names and addresses of the owners can be determined by the Clerk
from records available to the public.
      (3)   Within a reasonable time after the hearing, the Board shall make
its order deciding the matter and serve a copy of the order upon the appellant
or the petitioner by mail, but the Board shall not make its order until the
planning agency has had a reasonable time, not to exceed 60 days, to review and
report to the Board upon the appeal or petition.
      (4)   The appellant or petitioner may within 45 days from the date of the
order file with the City Clerk an appeal to the Council from the decision of
the Board.
   (G)   Hearing and decision by the Council.
      (1)   The Council shall, at its next regular meeting after the filing of
an appeal to it from a decision of the Board set a date for hearing thereon,
which shall be not later than 60 days after the meeting.
      (2)   After hearing the oral or written views of all interested persons,
the Council shall make its decision at the same meeting or at a specified
future meeting thereof.
   (H)   Form of action taken and record thereof. The Board, and the Council on
appeal, shall provide for a record of its proceedings, which shall include the
minutes of its meeting, its findings and the action taken on each matter heard
by it, including its final order. The City Clerk shall maintain a permanent
record of the disposition of all appeals to the Council from decisions of the
Board.
(Ord. passed 8-11-1997, § 20.70; Ord. passed 11- -1997; Ord. passed 2- -2000;
Ord. passed 6- -2001; Ord. passed 7- -2006; Ord. passed 8- -2010)
§ 154.026 SCHEDULE OF FEES.
   (A)   No permit or certificate shall be issued unless or until the zoning
permit fees have been paid in full.
   (B)   The schedule of fees, as adopted by the City Council, is attached to
the ordinance codified herein as “Exhibit A”.
(Ord. passed 8-11-1997, § 20.80; Ord. passed 11- -1997; Ord. passed 2- -2000;
Ord. passed 6- -2001; Ord. passed 7- -2006; Ord. passed 8- -2010)
§ 154.027 AMENDMENTS AND CHANGES.
   (A)   Procedure.
      (1)   It is the intent of this chapter that all questions of
interpretation and enforcement shall be first presented to the administrative
official, and that the questions shall be presented to the Board of Appeals and
Adjustment only on appeal from the decision of the Zoning Administrator, and
that recourse from the decisions of the Board of Appeals and Adjustment shall
be in the courts as provided by law and particularly by statute.
      (2)   The procedure to be followed shall be as follows.
         (a)   Whenever a person desires that any amendment or change, be made
in this chapter, including the text and/or map, as to any property in the city,
there shall be presented to the Board of Appeals and Adjustments a petition
requesting the change or amendment and clearly describing the property and its
boundaries as to which the change or amendment is desired, duly signed by the
owner or owners of all real estate included within the boundaries of the tract
as described in the petition. It shall be the duty of the Board of Appeals and
Adjustments to vote upon the petition within 60 days after the filing of the
petition.
         (b)   A public hearing shall be held by the Board of Appeals and
Adjustments. Notice of time and place of the hearing shall be published not
less than four, nor more than 20, days preceding the hearing and at least once
in a newspaper of general circulation in the city, but the hearing shall not
take place less than seven days after date of publication. The Zoning
Administrator shall notify all adjacent or opposite property owners separated
by public right-of-way, as listed by the applicant in the original application,
of the time, date and subject matter of the hearing.
         (c)   In cases where a proposed amendment, supplement or change is
disapproved by the Board of Appeals and Adjustments, or a protest be presented
duly signed by the owners of 20% or more of the area of the lots included in
the proposed change or by the property owners of 20% or more of the property
which is located within 200 feet of the exterior boundaries of the property for
which the change or amendment was requested, the change or amendment shall not
become effective, except by the favorable vote of at least three-fourths of all
the members of the Council. It shall be the duty of the City Council to vote
upon the petition within 60 days after the receipt of the Board of Appeals and
Adjustments recommendation. Whenever any petition for an amendment, supplement
or change of the zoning or regulations herein contained or subsequently
established shall have been denied by the City Council, then no new petition
covering the same property or the same property and additional property shall
be filed or considered by the City Council until one year shall have elapsed
from the date of the filing of the first petition.
      (3)   Every petition requesting an amendment shall be accompanied by a
fee of an amount as set by City Council from time to time, plus an amount as
set by City Council from time to time per acre or fraction thereof contained in
the property subject to the petition, to cover publication and processing
costs; except that, a petition for rezoning to a Planned Unit Development
District shall instead be subject to fees as stipulated in §§
154.210 through
154.214 of this chapter.
(Ord. passed 8-11-1997, § 21.10; Ord. passed 11- -1997; Ord. passed 2- -2000;
Ord. passed 6- -2001; Ord. passed 7- -2006; Ord. passed 8- -2010)
   (B)   Conditional/interim uses.
      (1)   Any landowner or his or her designated agent may file an
application to use the land for one or more of the conditional/interim uses
provided for in this chapter in the zoning district in which the land is
located.
      (2)   Any application shall be subject to the following requirements.
         (a)   An application for a conditional/interim use shall be filed with
the Zoning Administrator on a form prescribed by the Zoning Administrator. The
application shall be accompanied by the plans and/or data prescribed by the
Board of Appeals and Adjustments, a fee of an amount as set by City Council
from time to time to cover publication and processing costs and shall include a
statement in writing by the applicant and adequate evidence showing that the
proposed conditional/interim use will conform to the standards set forth in the
following divisions (B)(2)(b) through (B)(2)(e) below.
         (b)   Upon receipt in proper form of the application fee and
statement, the Board of Appeals and Adjustments shall hold at least one public
hearing on the proposed conditional/interim use. Notice of the hearing will be
published as set forth in division (A)(2) above. Supplemental or additional
notices may be published or distributed as the Board of Appeals and Adjustments
may, by rule, prescribe from time to time.
         (c)   For each application for conditional/interim use, the Board of
Appeals and Adjustments shall determine its findings and render a decision upon
the issuance of a conditional/interim use permit, including the stipulations of
additional conditions and guarantees that the conditions will be complied with
when they are deemed necessary for the protection of the public interest.
Whenever an application is denied, it cannot be renewed for one year
thereafter.
         (d)   No conditional/interim use shall be recommended by the Board of
Appeals and Adjustments unless the Board shall find:
            1.   The establishment, maintenance or operation of the
conditional/interim use will not be detrimental to or endanger the public
health, safety, morals, convenience, comfort or general welfare;
            2.   The conditional/interim use will not be injurious to the use
and enjoyment of other property in the immediate vicinity for the purposes
already permitted, nor substantially diminish and impair property values within
the neighborhood;
            3.   The establishment of the use will not impede the normal
orderly development and improvement of the surrounding property for uses
permitted in the district;
            4.   Adequate utilities, access roads, drainage and/or necessary
facilities have been or are being provided;
            5.   Measures have been or will be taken to provide ingress and
egress so designed as to minimize traffic congestion in the public streets; and
         (e)   Prior to the granting of any conditional/interim use, the Board
of Appeals and Adjustments shall stipulate the conditions and restrictions upon
the establishment, location, construction, maintenance and operation of the
conditional/interim use as is deemed necessary for the protection of the public
interest and to secure compliance with the standards and requirements specified
in division (B)(2)(d) above. In all cases in which conditional/interim uses are
granted, the Board of Appeals and Adjustments shall require the evidence and
guarantees as it may deem necessary as proof that the conditions stipulated in
connection therewith are being and will be complied with.
(Ord. passed 8-11-1997, § 21.20; Ord. passed 11- -1997; Ord. passed 2- -2000;
Ord. passed 6- -2001; Ord. passed 7- -2006; Ord. passed 8- -2010)
DISTRICT ESTABLISHMENTS
§ 154.040 DISTRICTS ESTABLISHED.
   To carry out the purpose of this chapter, the city is hereby divided into
different zoning district classifications as follows:
A     Agriculture District
BF    Bluff Protection Overlay District
C-A   Arterial Commercial District
C-CBD Central Business District Commercial District
FP    Floodplain Management District
I-1   Light Industrial District
I-2   Heavy Industrial District
PUD   Planned Unit Development District
R-1   Single-Family Residential District
R-2   Single-Family Residential District
R-3   Multi-Family Residential District
R-MH  Manufactured Home District
 
(Ord. passed 8-11-1997, § 3.10; Ord. passed 11- -1997; Ord. passed 2- -2000;
Ord. passed 6- -2001; Ord. passed 7- -2006; Ord. passed 8- -2010)
§ 154.041 ZONING MAP.
   (A)   (1)   The boundaries of these districts are indicated upon the
Official Zoning Map of the city, which map is made a part of this chapter by
reference hereto.
      (2)   The official zoning map and all the notations, references and other
matters shown thereon shall be as much a part of this chapter as if the
notations, references and other matters set forth by the map were all fully
described herein.
      (3)   The Official Zoning Map shall be on file in the office of the City
Clerk and shall bear the signature of the Mayor, attested to by the City Clerk.
   (B)   If, in accordance with the provisions of this chapter, changes are
made in the district boundaries or other matter portrayed on the official
zoning map, the ordinance number and date of change shall be recorded by the
City Clerk on the official zoning map.

(Ord. passed 8-11-1997, § 3.20; Ord. passed 11- -1997; Ord. passed 2- -2000;
Ord. passed 6- -2001; Ord. passed 7- -2006; Ord. passed 8- -2010)
§ 154.042 INTERPRETATION OF DISTRICT BOUNDARIES.
   Where uncertainty exists as to the boundaries of districts as shown on the
official zoning map on file in the office of the City Clerk, the following
rules shall apply.
   (A)   Boundaries indicated as approximately following the center lines of
streets, highways or alleys shall be construed to follow the center lines.
   (B)   Boundaries indicated as approximately following platted lot lines
shall be construed as following the lot lines.
   (C)   Boundaries indicated as approximately following city limits shall be
construed as following the city limits.
   (D)   Boundaries indicated as approximately following the center lines of
streams, rivers, canals, lakes or other bodies of water shall be construed to
following the center lines.
   (E)   Boundaries indicated as parallel to or extensions of features
indicated in divisions (A) through (D) above shall be so construed. Distances
not specifically indicated on the Official Zoning Map shall be determined by
the scale of the map.
   (F)   Where physical or cultural features existing on the ground are at
variance with those shown on the Official Zoning Map or in other circumstances
not covered by divisions (A) through (E) above, the Zoning Administrator shall
interpret the district boundaries.
   (G)   The district boundaries are either streets or alleys unless otherwise
shown, and where the districts designated on the map made a part of this
chapter are bounded approximately by street or alley lines, the street or alley
shall be construed to be the boundary of the district.
   (H)   Where the district boundaries are not otherwise indicated and where
the property has been or may hereafter be divided into blocks and lots, the
district boundaries shall be construed to be the lot lines, and where the
districts designated on the map made a part of this chapter are bounded
approximately by lot lines, the lot lines shall be construed to be the boundary
of the districts unless the boundaries are otherwise indicated on the map.
   (I)   In subdivided property, the district boundary lines on the map made a
part of this chapter shall be determined by use of the scale contained on the
map.
(Ord. passed 8-11-1997, § 3.30; Ord. passed 11- -1997; Ord. passed 2- -2000;
Ord. passed 6- -2001; Ord. passed 7- -2006; Ord. passed 8- -2010)
§ 154.043 ANNEXED TERRITORY.
   All property which is annexed to the city subsequent to the effective date
hereof shall be placed in the R-1 Single-Family Residential District unless the
annexation ordinance temporarily places the land in another district. Within
one year following annexation, the Planning Commission shall evaluate and
recommend a permanent district classification to the City Council.
(Ord. passed 8-11-1997, § 3.40; Ord. passed 11- -1997; Ord. passed 2- -2000;
Ord. passed 6- -2001; Ord. passed 7- -2006; Ord. passed 8- -2010)
A - AGRICULTURAL DISTRICT
§ 154.055 INTENT.
   This district is intended to provide areas in which agriculture and related
uses are encouraged and prime agricultural land will be preserved. Usual urban
residential densities are not allowed until streets, utilities and other
services and facilities can properly and economically be extended to serve the
areas, to promote the orderly development and fiscal integrity of the city.
(Ord. passed 8-11-1997, § 5.10; Ord. passed 11- -1997; Ord. passed 2- -2000;
Ord. passed 6- -2001; Ord. passed 7- -2006; Ord. passed 8- -2010)
§ 154.056 PERMITTED USES.

   (A)   Single-family detached dwellings;
   (B)   Agriculture;
   (C)   Golf courses;
   (D)   Open spaces;
   (E)   Parks; and
   (F)   Accessory uses incidental to and on the same zoning lot as the
principal use as follows:
      (1)   Agriculture buildings and structures;
      (2)   Club houses and other structures on the grounds of golf course;
      (3)   Garages and carports;
      (4)   Private greenhouses; and
      (5)   Private guest houses.
(Ord. passed 8-11-1997, § 5.20; Ord. passed 11- -1997; Ord. passed 2- -2000;
Ord. passed 6- -2001; Ord. passed 7- -2006; Ord. passed 8- -2010)

stores;
   (H)   Filtration plants;

   (K)   Public or private educational facilities;
   (L)   Public utility and services uses, including, but not limited to,
electric substations, gas regulator stations, radio and television transmitting
towers;
   (M)   Railroad rights-of-way, but not including railroad yards and shops;

   (A)   Height. The maximum height of buildings shall not exceed 35 feet or
two and one-half stories, excluding farm buildings.
   (B)   Yard setbacks.
      (1)   Front yard. A front yard not less than 50 feet shall be provided.
      (2)   Side yard. A side yard of not less than 25 feet shall be provided
for interior and corner lots.
      (3)   Rear yard. A rear yard of not less than 35 feet shall be provided.
      (4)   Corner side yard. There shall be provided a side yard of not less
than 50 feet in every instance where the side lot line is adjacent to a public
street.
   (C)   Minimum lot area. The minimum lot area shall be ten acres.

R-3 - MULTI-FAMILY RESIDENTIAL DISTRICT

   The R-3 Multi-Family Residential District is intended to provide space for
development of relatively high density residential uses, predominantly
apartment or single-family attached dwelling units. Developments in this
district are characterized by shared parking, open space and other facilities,
located near major arterial streets and shopping facilities in neighborhoods
that are or soon will be served by a full range of public facilities.
(Ord. passed 8-11-1997, § 8.10; Ord. passed 11- -1997; Ord. passed 2- -2000;

§ 154.108 BULK REGULATIONS.
   (A)   Height. The maximum height of buildings shall not exceed 45 feet or
three stories.
   (B)   Yard setbacks.

provided.
      (2)   Side yard. A side yard of not less than six feet shall be provided
for structures two and one-half stories or less in height; over two and one-
half stories, a side yard of not less than ten feet shall be provided.
      (3)   Rear yard. A rear yard of not less than 25 feet shall be provided.
      (4)   Corner side yard. There shall be provided a side yard of not less
than 25 feet in every instance whether the side lot line is adjacent to a
public street.
   (C)   Minimum lot area. The minimum lot area shall be 6,000 square feet.
   (D)   Minimum lot area per family. There shall be provided at least 1,800
square feet of lot area per dwelling unit for multiple-family dwellings, 3,000
square feet of lot area per family for two-family dwellings, 6,000 square feet
of lot area per family for single-family dwellings and all other permitted or
conditional/interim uses.
   (E)   Minimum lot width. The average minimum lot width shall be not less
than 50 feet.
(Ord. passed 8-11-1997, § 8.40; Ord. passed 11- -1997; Ord. passed 2- -2000;
Ord. passed 6- -2001; Ord. passed 7- -2006; Ord. passed 8- -2010)

154.320 through

R-MH - MANUFACTURED HOME DISTRICT
§ 154.120 ESTABLISHMENT.
   (A)   There is hereby created and established within the city a district to
be known as the R-MH Manufactured Home District. The R-MH Manufactured Home
District shall provide for mobile home parks in areas of the community where
the use is compatible with existing and indicated future development.
   (B)   This district shall be served by streets to provide adequate access to
each lot. Before any petition for the establishment of a Manufactured Home
District is submitted to the Planning Commission, it shall be accompanied by a
plan for development that conforms to the requirements of this chapter and
state law and regulations.
   (C)   The developer shall show the Planning Commission evidence that the
proposed plan meets all requirements of the state before the plan may be
approved.
(Ord. passed 8-11-1997, § 9.10; Ord. passed 11- -1997; Ord. passed 2- -2000;
Ord. passed 6- -2001; Ord. passed 7- -2006; Ord. passed 8- -2010)
§ 154.121 PERMITTED USES.
   The uses of land permitted in the R-MH Manufactured Home District are mobile
home parks and complementary uses such as administration buildings, storm
shelters and services offered for the use of the residents of the mobile home
parks.
(Ord. passed 8-11-1997, § 9.20; Ord. passed 11- -1997; Ord. passed 2- -2000;
Ord. passed 6- -2001; Ord. passed 7- -2006; Ord. passed 8- -2010)
§ 154.122 GROSS DEVELOPMENT AREA DENSITY.
   (A)   The area proposed for a mobile home park shall have at least five
acres of gross development area.
   (B)   The area may be developed in two or more stages; provided that, each
stage conforms in all respects with the overall mobile home park development
plan.
   (C)   (1)   The maximum gross development area shall be limited to 20 acres.
      (2)   The maximum density allowed for the gross development areas shall
be six mobile home units per acre.
(Ord. passed 8-11-1997, § 9.30; Ord. passed 11- -1997; Ord. passed 2- -2000;
Ord. passed 6- -2001; Ord. passed 7- -2006; Ord. passed 8- -2010)
§ 154.123 STANDARDS AND REQUIREMENTS.
   (A)   A mobile home shall not be occupied for dwelling purposes unless it is
properly connected to municipal water, sewer and electrical utilities.
   (B)   No mobile home shall be connected to water, sewer or electrical
services after the date of this chapter unless the mobile home compiles with
the standards and requirements prescribed by the United States Department of
Housing and Urban Development if manufactured after 6-14-1976 or standards
required by American National Standards Institute (ANSI Al 19.1) or the
provisions of the National Fire Protection Association (NFPA 501B) if
manufactured before 6-14-1976.
   (C)   A certificate, if issued by the manufacturer of the mobile home, shall
be permanently affixed on a readily visible location on the exterior of the
mobile home as prima facie evidence of compliance.
(Ord. passed 8-11-1997, § 9.40; Ord. passed 11- -1997; Ord. passed 2- -2000;
Ord. passed 6- -2001; Ord. passed 7- -2006; Ord. passed 8- -2010)
§ 154.124 LOT AREA.
   Every lot upon which a mobile home unit is located shall have adequate
access to a public street and shall conform to the following minimum lot area
and spacing requirements; for residential use, the lot area shall be a minimum
of 5,000 square feet with a minimum lot width of 50 feet.
(Ord. passed 8-11-1997, § 9.50; Ord. passed 11- -1997; Ord. passed 2- -2000;
Ord. passed 6- -2001; Ord. passed 7- -2006; Ord. passed 8- -2010)
§ 154.125 SETBACKS AND SEPARATION.
   Every lot shall have a front setback of not less than 25 feet in depth,
measured from the edge of the surface private street or 20 feet from the public
street right-of-way. There must be at least a ten-foot side yard setback and a
minimum of 20 feet rear yard setback. Decks attached to a mobile home may
intrude up to five feet into a setback area. Storage buildings may intrude into
the rear yard, but shall be located at least three feet from the side and rear
lot lines. No storage building shall be located in the required front yard or
in the side yard adjacent to the home.
(Ord. passed 8-11-1997, § 9.60; Ord. passed 11- -1997; Ord. passed 2- -2000;
Ord. passed 6- -2001; Ord. passed 7- -2006; Ord. passed 8- -2010)
§ 154.126 SOIL AND GROUND COVER.
   Exposed ground surfaces in all parts of every mobile home park shall be
paved, covered with stone screening or other solid material or protected with a
vegetative growth capable of preventing soil erosion and objectionable dust.
(Ord. passed 8-11-1997, § 9.70; Ord. passed 11- -1997; Ord. passed 2- -2000;
Ord. passed 6- -2001; Ord. passed 7- -2006; Ord. passed 8- -2010)
§ 154.127 SIGNS.
   (A)   One permanent, low-illuminated identification sign may be permitted at
any entrance to a mobile home park.
   (B)   The sign shall be of ornamental metal, stone, masonry or other
permanent materials, and shall include only the name of the mobile home park.
   (C)   The sign shall not exceed 16 square feet in surface area, and the
maximum height above street grade shall be as follows: if located on the lot
line, it shall not exceed two feet in height; however, the sign may be located
in required yard and for every three feet it is set back from the lot line, the
sign may be one foot greater in height, up to a maximum of six feet.
(Ord. passed 8-11-1997, § 9.80; Ord. passed 11- -1997; Ord. passed 2- -2000;
Ord. passed 6- -2001; Ord. passed 7- -2006; Ord. passed 8- -2010)
§ 154.128 CONDITIONAL/INTERIM USES.
   The following uses may be permitted in the R-MH Manufactured Home District
on a conditional use basis, following approval in the manner prescribed in §
154.027(B) of this chapter: recreational camping area (RCA), as currently
licensed through the state’s Department of Health.
(Ord. passed 8-11-1997, § 9.10; Ord. passed 11- -1997; Ord. passed 2- -2000;
Ord. passed 6- -2001; Ord. passed 7- -2006; Ord. passed 8- -2010; Ord. passed
11-22-2010)
C-A - ARTERIAL COMMERCIAL DISTRICT
§ 154.140 INTENT.
   The C-A Arterial Commercial District is intended to provide space for
commercial areas located outside the central business district. These districts
accommodate a wide range of facilities with a city- wide impact and include, as
well, much of the commercial frontage along the major transportation arteries.
(Ord. passed 8-11-1997, § 10.10; Ord. passed 11- -1997; Ord. passed 2- -2000;
Ord. passed 6- -2001; Ord. passed 7- -2006; Ord. passed 8- -2010)
§ 154.141 PERMITTED USES.
   The following uses are permitted in the C-A District:
   (A)   Adult establishments, adult bookstores, adult cabarets, adult mini
motion picture theaters, adult motion picture theaters and sexual activity
establishments.
      (1)   Location. These uses shall not be located within 500 feet of each
other, nor shall they be located within 500 feet of any public or parochial
school, licensed day care facility, church, public park or residential zoning
district.
      (2)   Restrictions. All building openings, entries, windows and the like
shall be covered or screened in such a manner as to prevent a view into the
interior from any public or semi-public area; and for new construction, and
whenever else it is considered feasible by the Zoning Administrator, the
building shall be oriented so as to minimize any possibility of viewing the
interior from public or semipublic areas. Advertisements, displays or other
promotional materials shall not be shown or exhibited so as to be visible to
the public from pedestrian sidewalks, walkways or from other public or semi-
public areas. Any such advertisements, displays or other promotional materials
shall be considered as signs and shall comply with §§
154.340  through
154.352 of this chapter.
   (B)   Amusement establishments, including bowling alleys, dance halls, pool
halls, swimming pools and skating rinks;
   (C)   Antique shops;
   (D)   Art shops and picture framing establishments;
   (E)   Automobile service stations, parts and accessory stores or washing
establishments;
   (F)   Automobile, trailer, motorcycle, boat and farm implement
establishments for display, hire, rental and sales (including sales lots),
including as incidental to the major uses all repair work in connection with
their own or customers’ vehicles. However, this division (E) shall not be
construed to include automobile, tractor or machinery wrecking and rebuilding
and used parts yards;
   (G)   Baby and children’s shops;
   (H)   Bakeries or bakery outlet, retail sales only;
   (I)   Banks, savings and loan associations or similar companies;
   (J)   Barber shops or beauty shops;
   (K)   Bed and breakfast lodging;
   (L)   Bicycle shops, sales and repairs;
   (M)   Blueprinting and photostatting establishments;
   (N)   Bookstores;
   (O)   Building materials sales;
   (P)   Business and professional offices;
   (Q)   Business machine sales and service;
   (R)   Business or secretarial schools or colleges, commercial trade schools
or other commercially operated schools for adults;
   (S)   Camera stores;
   (T)   Carpenter and cabinet making shops for retail custom work;
   (U)   Carpet and rug stores, retail sales only;
   (V)   Catalog sales stores;
   (W)   Catering establishments;
   (X)   China and glassware stores;
   (Y)   Churches, temples or synagogues;
   (Z)   Cigar, tobacco and pipe shops;
   (AA)   Clinics;
   (BB)   Clothes cleaning establishments and laundry pick-up stations;
   (CC)   Clothing stores;
   (DD)   Clubs or lodges, non-profit or fraternal;
   (EE)   Coin or philatelic stores;
   (FF)   Collection office of public utility;
   (GG)   Community or neighborhood centers or meeting halls;
   (HH)   Confectionery stores;
   (II)   Dance studios;
   (JJ)   Delicatessens;
   (KK)   Department stores;
   (LL)   Drug stores;
   (MM)   Dry goods stores;
   (NN)   Dwelling units when constructed in conjunction with or as part of a
structure occupied by a use permitted or conditionally permissible by this
chapter;
   (OO)   Employment agencies;
   (PP)   Florist shops;
   (QQ)   Food stores, including supermarkets, dairy stores, produce markets
and meat and/or fish markets;
   (RR)   Funeral homes, mortuaries and crematories;
   (SS)   Furniture and home furnishing stores;
   (TT)   Garden supply, tool and seed stores;
   (UU)   Gift or hobby shops;
   (VV)   Hardware stores;
   (WW)   Health studios, gyms or recreational establishments;
   (XX)   Hobby shops;
   (YY)   Hospitals;
