   (ZZ)   Hotels;
   (AAA)   Household appliances, sales and/or repair;
   (BBB)   Jewelry stores and watch repair shops;
   (CCC)   Labor union offices;
   (DDD)   Lawn mower repair shops;
   (EEE)   Leather goods stores;
   (FFF)   Locker plant, for storage and retail shops only;
   (GGG)   Locksmith and gunsmith shops;
   (HHH)   Magazine and newspaper stores;
   (III)   Motels, motor hotels and tourist courts;
   (JJJ)   Music stores or studios;
   (KKK)   Nursery schools or day care centers;
   (LLL)   Office supply stores;
   (MMM)   Optician sales, retail;
   (NNN)   Orthopedic and medical appliance stores;
   (OOO)   Paint, glass and wallpaper stores;
   (PPP)   Parking lots, other than accessory, and subject to the provisions of
§§
154.320 through
154.325 of this chapter;
   (QQQ)   Pawn shops;
   (RRR)   Pet stores;
   (SSS)   Photographic studios, including the developing of film and pictures
when conducted as part of the principal business on the premises;
   (TTT)   Plumbing and heating shops;
   (UUU)   Post offices;
   (VVV)   Printing and publishing;
   (WWW)   Public auction room;
   (XXX)   Public neighborhood recreational or cultural centers;
   (YYY)   Radio and television sales and repair;
   (ZZZ)   Restaurants, taverns and cocktail lounges including live
entertainment or dancing;
   (AAAA)   Shoe or clothing repair shops;
   (BBBB)   Sign painting shops;
   (CCCC)   Signs, in conformance with §§
154.340  through
154.352 of this chapter;
   (DDDD)   Small animal hospitals, and or small animal veterinary clinics;
provided, any outdoor exercising runway shall be at least 200 feet from any
residential boundary. Small animals are defined as pets and companion animals
(i.e., dogs, cats, birds, reptiles);
   (EEEE)   Sporting goods stores;
   (FFFF)   Tailor and dressmaking shops;
   (GGGG)   Taxidermists;
   (HHHH)   Telegraph offices;
   (IIII)   Telephone company offices and answering services;
   (JJJJ)   Television and radio studios;
   (KKKK)   Theaters, indoor;
   (LLLL)   Toy stores;
   (MMMM)   Travel agency;
   (NNNN)   Upholstering shops;
   (OOOO)   Variety stores and convenience stores including fuel sales; and
   (PPPP)   Accessory uses, incidental to and on the same zoning lot as a
permitted principal use.
(Ord. passed 8-11-1997, § 10.20; Ord. passed 11- -1997; Ord. passed 2- -2000;
Ord. passed 6- -2001; Ord. passed 7- -2006; Ord. passed 8- -2010)
§ 154.142 CONDITIONAL/INTERIM USES.
   The following uses may be permitted in the C-A District on a conditional
basis, following approval in the manner prescribed in §
154.027(B) of this chapter:
   (A)   Drive-in movie theaters;
   (B)   Dwelling units established as the sole principal use of a lot, as
permitted by and subject to the regulations of the least restrictive
residential district adjoining a C-A zoned lot, or in the event of no adjoining
residential district, then the residential uses permitted and subject to the
regulations of the R-3 District;
   (C)   Public utility service uses;
   (D)   Other commercial uses determined by the Board of Appeals and
Adjustment to be of the same general character as the uses permitted in this
district; and
   (E)   Accessory uses, incidental to and on the same zoning lot as the
principal use.
(Ord. passed 8-11-1997, § 10.30; Ord. passed 11- -1997; Ord. passed 2- -2000;
Ord. passed 6- -2001; Ord. passed 7- -2006; Ord. passed 8- -2010)
§ 154.143 BULK REGULATIONS.
   (A)   Height. A maximum height of buildings shall not exceed 35 feet.
   (B)   Yard setbacks.
      (1)   Front yard. A front yard not less than 25 feet shall be provided.
      (2)   Side yard. No side yard shall be required, except as required in
divisions (B)(4) or (B)(5) of this section. However, if a side yard is
provided, it shall be not less than five feet.
      (3)   Rear yard. A rear yard not less than 25 feet shall be provided,
except as required in division (B)(5) below.
      (4)   Corner side yard. There shall be provided a side yard of not less
than 25 feet in depth in every instance where the side lot line is adjacent to
a public street.
      (5)   Transitional yards. Where the side or rear lot line of a lot in the
C-A District coincides with a side or rear lot line in an adjacent residential
district, a yard shall be provided not less in width than 1.25 times the height
of the wall of the building on the C-A zoned lot. The side yard in the C-
A District shall not be less than 15 feet in width. The rear yard in the C-
A District shall not be less than 25 feet in width. Where a side or rear lot
line in the C-A District coincides with a side or rear lot line in an adjacent
residential district, the yard shall contain a wall, screening fence or
densely-planted compact hedge not less than five feet, nor more than ten feet,
in height, which wall, fence or hedge shall be well maintained.
   (C)   Lot area. There shah be provided not less than 2,000 square feet of
lot area for each commercial establishment. There shall also be provided not
less than 1,800 square feet of lot area for each dwelling unit constructed
under the provisions of §
154.141 of this chapter.
   (D)   Floor area ratio. The floor area ratio shall not exceed 1.0.
(Ord. passed 8-11-1997, § 10.40; Ord. passed 11- -1997; Ord. passed 2- -2000;
Ord. passed 6- -2001; Ord. passed 7- -2006; Ord. passed 8- -2010)
§ 154.144 OFF-STREET PARKING AND LOADING REQUIREMENTS.
   Spaces for off-street parking and loading shall be provided in accordance
with the provisions of §§
154.320 through
154.325 of this chapter.
(Ord. passed 8-11-1997, § 10.50; Ord. passed 11- -1997; Ord. passed 2- -2000;
Ord. passed 6- -2001; Ord. passed 7- -2006; Ord. passed 8- -2010)
C-CBD - CENTRAL BUSINESS DISTRICT COMMERCIAL DISTRICT
§ 154.155 INTENT.
   The C-CBD Central Business District Commercial District is defined as the
major shopping area and center of commerce containing in addition to retail
uses, governmental offices, service uses, professional, cultural, recreational
and entertainment establishments and uses, residence, appropriate industrial
activities and transportation facilities designed to provide space for a wide
variety of retail stores, offices and related activities which serve the entire
city or may even have regional impact. This district is characterized by high
intensity and large bulk development. It is intended that this district not be
mapped outside the downtown area.
(Ord. passed 8-11-1997, § 11.10; Ord. passed 11- -1997; Ord. passed 2- -2000;
Ord. passed 6- -2001; Ord. passed 7- -2006; Ord. passed 8- -2010)
§ 154.156 PERMITTED USES.
   The following uses are permitted in the C-CBD District:
   (A)   Uses permitted in the C-A District;
   (B)   Multiple-family dwellings;
   (C)   Accessory uses incidental to and on the same zoning lot as a permitted
principal use; and
   (D)   Accessory apartments.
(Ord. passed 8-11-1997, § 11.20; Ord. passed 11- -1997; Ord. passed 2- -2000;
Ord. passed 5-22-2000; Ord. passed 6- -2001; Ord. passed 7- -2006; Ord. passed
8- -2010)
§ 154.157 CONDITIONAL/INTERIM USES.
   The following uses may be permitted in the C-CBD District on a conditional/
interim use basis, following approval in the manner prescribed in §
154.027(B) of this chapter:
   (A)   Drive-in establishments excluding drive-in theaters;
   (B)   Warehousing; and
   (C)   Accessory uses incidental to and on the same zoning lot as the
principal use.
(Ord. passed 8-11-1997, § 11.30; Ord. passed 11- -1997; Ord. passed 2- -2000;
Ord. passed 6- -2001; Ord. passed 7- -2006; Ord. passed 8- -2010)
§ 154.158 BULK REGULATIONS.
   (A)   Height. The maximum height of buildings shall not exceed 100 feet.
   (B)   Yard setbacks.
      (1)   Front yard. None required.
      (2)   Side yard. None required, except as required in division (B)(5)
below. However, if a side yard is provided, it shall be not less than five
feet.
      (3)   Rear yard. None required.
      (4)   Corner side yard. None required.
      (5)   Transitional yards.
         (a)   Where the side or rear lot line of a lot in the C-CBD District
coincides with a side or rear lot line in an adjacent residential district, a
yard shall be provided not less in width than 1.35 times the height of the wall
of the building on the C-CBD zoned lot. The side yard in the C-CBD District
shall not be less than ten feet in width.
         (b)   Where a side or rear lot line in the C-CBD District coincides
with a side or rear lot line in an adjacent residential district, the yard
provided shall contain a wall, screening fence or densely-planted compact hedge
not less than five feet, nor more than ten feet, in height, which wall, fence
or hedge shall be well maintained.
   (C)   Lot area. No minimum. Also, no minimum lot area per dwelling unit for
multiple-family residential uses.
   (D)   Floor area ratio. The floor area ratio shall not exceed 6.0 for non-
residential uses.
(Ord. passed 8-11-1997, § 11.40; Ord. passed 11- -1997; Ord. passed 2- -2000;
Ord. passed 6- -2001; Ord. passed 7- -2006; Ord. passed 8- -2010)
§ 154.159 OFF-STREET PARKING AND LOADING REQUIREMENTS.
   Spaces for off-street parking and loading shall be provided in accordance
with the provisions of §§
154.320 through
154.325 of this chapter.
(Ord. passed 8-11-1997, § 11.50; Ord. passed 11- -1997; Ord. passed 2- -2000;
Ord. passed 6- -2001; Ord. passed 7- -2006; Ord. passed 8- -2010)
I-1 - LIGHT INDUSTRIAL DISTRICT
§ 154.170 INTENT.
   The I-1 Light Industrial District is designed to provide an environment
suitable for industrial activities that do not create appreciable nuisances or
hazards, or that require a pleasant, hazard- and nuisance-free environment. It
is intended that the permitted uses be compatible and not detrimental to
adjacent properties.
(Ord. passed 8-11-1997, § 12.10; Ord. passed 11- -1997; Ord. passed 2- -2000;
Ord. passed 6- -2001; Ord. passed 7- -2006; Ord. passed 8- -2010)
§ 154.171 PERMITTED USES.
   The following uses are permitted in the I-1 Light Industrial District:
   (A)   Agricultural equipment manufacture, rental or sales;
   (B)   Alcoholic beverages manufacture, bottling or wholesaling;
   (C)   Automobile assembly, body and fender works, painting, upholstery,
repair or sales;
   (D)   Automobile service stations, for the retail sale or dispensing of
fuel, lubricants, tires, batteries, accessories and supplies including
installation and minor services customarily incidental thereto; facilities for
chassis and gear lubrication are permitted only if enclosed in a building;
   (E)   Bottling plants;
   (F)   Building material sales;
   (G)   Car washes;
   (H)   Cartage and express facilities;
   (I)   Cold storage plant;
   (J)   Contractors’, architects’ and engineers’ offices, shops and yards;
such as building, cement, electrical, heating, ventilating and air
conditioning, masonry, painting, plumbing, refrigeration and roofing;
   (K)   Distribution plant;
   (L)   Dog kennel and facility for boarding, breeding or training;
   (M)   Dry cleaning and laundry establishments with no limitation on number
of employees;
   (N)   Dwelling units for watchmen and their families, located on the
premises where they are employed in such capacity;
   (O)   Electrical appliance and equipment manufacturing;
   (P)   Electrical supplies sales, warehousing and distribution;
   (Q)   Electronic instrument and device manufacturing, repair and service;
   (R)   Farm and industrial machinery sales and repair;
   (S)   Film processing;
   (T)   Fuel sales;
   (U)   Fumigating contractor;
   (V)   Garages for storage, repair and servicing of motor vehicles;
   (W)   Greenhouses, wholesale;
   (X)   Ice manufacture, sales and storage;
   (Y)   Instrument manufacturing - dental, optical, surgical, measuring,
musical;
   (Z)   Laboratories for research, testing and experimental purposes;
   (AA)   Large animal hospital and/or large animal veterinary clinics,
provided any outdoor exercising runway shall be at least 200 feet from any
residential boundary. Large animals are defined as horses and food animals
(i.e., cows, pigs and sheep);
   (BB)   Machine shop;
   (CC)   Mail order houses;
   (DD)   Paint mixing (no boiling);
   (EE)   Parking lots, other than accessory, and subject to the provisions of
§§
154.320 through
154.325 of this chapter;
   (FF)   Photographic equipment manufacture (except film and chemicals);
   (GG)   Plating;
   (HH)   Plumbing contractor’s office and plumbing supplies sales and
wholesaling;
   (II)   Pottery and statuary manufacturing;
   (JJ)   Printing and publishing;
   (KK)   Public utility and service uses including:
      (1)   Bus stations, bus terminals, bus turn-arounds (off-street), bus
garages and bus lots;
      (2)   Fire stations;
      (3)   Police stations;
      (4)   Railroad passenger stations;
      (5)   Railroad rights-of-way;
      (6)   Telephone exchanges, telephone transmission equipment buildings and
microwave relay towers;
      (7)   Utility service substations - electric, gas, telephone and water;
and
      (8)   Waterworks, reservoirs, pumping stations and filtration plants.
   (LL)   Radar installations and towers;
   (MM)   Radio and television studios, stations and towers, transmitting and
receiving;
   (NN)   Signs in compliance with §§
154.340  through
154.352 of this chapter;
   (OO)   Trade schools;
   (PP)   Truck terminals;
   (QQ)   Warehousing and storage facilities;
   (RR)   Weighing stations, operated by the state;
   (SS)   The manufacturing, compounding, processing, packaging or treatment of
cosmetics, pharmaceuticals and food products, except fish and meat products,
cereals, sauerkraut, vinegar, yeast, stock feed, flour and the rendering or
refining of fats and oils;
   (TT)   The manufacture, compounding, assembling or treatment of articles or
merchandise from previously prepared materials such as bone, cloth, cork,
fiber, leather, paper, plastics, metals or stones, tobacco, wax, yams and wood;
and
   (UU)   Accessory uses incidental to and on the same zoning lot as a
permitted principal use.
(Ord. passed 8-11-1997, § 12.20; Ord. passed 11- -1997; Ord. passed 2- -2000;
Ord. passed 6- -2001; Ord. passed 7- -2006; Ord. passed 8- -2010)
§ 154.172 CONDITIONAL/INTERIM USES.
   The following uses may be permitted in the I-1 Light Industrial District on
a conditional basis, following approval in the manner prescribed in §
154.027(B) of this chapter: uses permitted in the C-A Arterial Commercial
District, except residential uses:
   (A)   Airports and commercial heliports, including aircraft landing fields,
runways, flight strips and flying schools, together with hangars, terminal
buildings and other auxiliary facilities;
   (B)   Air, railroad and water freight terminals, railroad switching and
classification yards, repair shops and roundhouses;
   (C)   By-products processing;
   (D)   Heliports, private;
   (E)   Institutions for the care or treatment of alcoholics, drug addict
patients or the mentally ill or retarded;
   (F)   Manufacture, compounding, processing, packaging or treatment of fish
and meat products, cereals, sauerkraut, pickles, vinegar, yeast, stock feed or
flour;
   (G)   Parks and playgrounds;
   (H)   Penal and correctional institutions;
   (I)   Race tracks of all kinds;
   (J)   Recreational buildings or community centers;
   (K)   Stadiums, auditoriums and arenas, open or enclosed;
   (L)   Theaters, automobile drive-ins;
   (M)   Other manufacturing, processing, storage or commercial uses determined
by the Zoning Board of Adjustment to be of the same general character as the
uses permitted in this District and found not to be obnoxious, unhealthful or
offensive by reason of the potential emission or transmission of noise,
vibration, smoke, dust, odors, toxic or noxious matter, glare, heat, fire or
explosives hazards; and
   (N)   Accessory uses incidental to and on the same zoning lot as the
principal use.
(Ord. passed 8-11-1997, § 12.30; Ord. passed 11- -1997; Ord. passed 2- -2000;
Ord. passed 6- -2001; Ord. passed 7- -2006; Ord. passed 8- -2010)
§ 154.173 PROHIBITED USES.
   The following uses are specifically prohibited from the I-1 Light Industrial
District:
   (A)   Dwelling units and lodging rooms, other than watchmen’s quarters as
specified in §
154.171 of this chapter;
   (B)   Acid manufacture or storage except on a limited scale as an accessory
use to a permitted industry;
   (C)   Garbage, waste materials, offal, dead animal or refuse incineration or
storage;
   (D)   Manufacture of fertilizer;
   (E)   Manufacture or storage of gun powder, fireworks or other explosives;
   (F)   Slaughterhouses and stockyards; and
   (G)   Similar uses which create excessive hazards, noise, vibrations, smoke,
dust, odors, heat, glare or other objectionable influence beyond the property
boundaries.
(Ord. passed 8-11-1997, § 12.40; Ord. passed 11- -1997; Ord. passed 2- -2000;
Ord. passed 6- -2001; Ord. passed 7- -2006; Ord. passed 8- -2010)
§ 154.174 BULK REGULATIONS.
   (A)   Height. The maximum height of buildings shall not exceed 75 feet or
six stories.
   (B)   Yard setbacks.
      (1)   Front yard. A front yard not less than 25 feet shall be provided.
      (2)   Side yard.
         (a)   No side yard shall be required, except as required in divisions
(B)(4) and (B)(5) below.
         (b)   However, if a side yard is provided, it shall be not less than
five feet.
      (3)   Rear yard. No rear yard shall be required, except as required in
division (B)(5) below.
      (4)   Corner side yard. There shall be provided a side yard of not less
than 25 feet in depth in every instance where the side lot line is adjacent to
a public street.
      (5)   Transitional yards. Where the side or rear lot line in the I-
1 District coincides with a side or rear lot line in an adjacent residential
district, a yard of not less than 15 feet shall be provided along the side or
rear lot line on the industrial lot.
   (C)   Floor area ratio. The floor area ratio shall not exceed 1.5.
(Ord. passed 8-11-1997, § 12.50; Ord. passed 11- -1997; Ord. passed 2- -2000;
Ord. passed 6- -2001; Ord. passed 7- -2006; Ord. passed 8- -2010)
§ 154.175 OFF-STREET PARKING AND LOADING REQUIREMENTS.
   Spaces for off-street parking and loading shall be provided in accordance
with the provisions of §§
154.320 through
154.325 of this chapter.
(Ord. passed 8-11-1997, § 12.60; Ord. passed 11- -1997; Ord. passed 2- -2000;
Ord. passed 6- -2001; Ord. passed 7- -2006; Ord. passed 8- -2010)
§ 154.176 DEVELOPMENT AND PERFORMANCE STANDARDS.
   (A)   Emissions of noise, vibration, dust, smoke or other particulate
matter, toxic materials, odor or glare shall not exceed the standard set by the
Minnesota Pollution Control Agency.
   (B)   Storage or sale of gas, fuel or oil shall comply with and be approved
by the Chief of the Fire Department.
(Ord. passed 8-11-1997, § 12.70; Ord. passed 11- -1997; Ord. passed 2- -2000;
Ord. passed 6- -2001; Ord. passed 7- -2006; Ord. passed 8- -2010)
I-2 - HEAVY INDUSTRIAL DISTRICT
§ 154.190 INTENT.
   The I-2 Heavy Industrial District is designed to accommodate large,
relatively self-contained and isolated areas intended to be used for industrial
activities whose potential nuisance or hazard generation is moderately high.
(Ord. passed 8-11-1997, § 13.10; Ord. passed 11- -1997; Ord. passed 2- -2000;
Ord. passed 6- -2001; Ord. passed 7- -2006; Ord. passed 8- -2010)
§ 154.191 PERMITTED USES.
   The following uses are permitted in the I-2 Heavy Industrial District:
   (A)   Any use permitted in the I-1 Light Industrial District; and
   (B)   Any other use not in conflict with any provision of city ordinances
regulation of nuisances and not listed in §§
154.192 and
154.193 of this chapter.
(Ord. passed 8-11-1997, § 13.20; Ord. passed 11- -1997; Ord. passed 2- -2000;
Ord. passed 6- -2001; Ord. passed 7- -2006; Ord. passed 8- -2010)
§ 154.192 CONDITIONAL/INTERIM USES.
   The following uses may be permitted in the I-2 Heavy Industrial District on
a conditional basis, following approval in the manner prescribed in §
154.027(B) of this chapter:
   (A)   Any use listed as a conditional/interim use in the 1-1 Light
Industrial District;
   (B)   Acid manufacture or wholesale storage of acids;
   (C)   Activities involving the manufacture, storage or utilization of
materials or products which decompose by detonation;
   (D)   Cement, lime, gypsum or plaster of paris manufacture;
   (E)   Extraction of gravel, sand or other raw materials;
   (F)   Fat rendering or distillation of bones;
   (G)   Fertilizer manufacture;
   (H)   Garbage, waste materials, offal, dead animal or refuse dumping,
incineration or storage;
   (I)   Gas manufacture;
   (J)   Glue manufacture;
   (K)   Grain elevator;
   (L)   Junk yards and auto graveyards;
   (M)   Petroleum refining;
   (N)   Smelting of tin, copper, zinc or iron ores;
   (O)   Stockyards or slaughterhouses; and
   (P)   Tannery.
(Ord. passed 8-11-1997, § 13.30; Ord. passed 11- -1997; Ord. passed 2- -2000;
Ord. passed 6- -2001; Ord. passed 7- -2006; Ord. passed 8- -2010)
§ 154.193 PROHIBITED USES.
   The following uses are specifically prohibited from the I-2 Heavy Industrial
District: dwelling units and lodging rooms other than watchman’s quarters as
specified in §
154.171 of this chapter.
(Ord. passed 8-11-1997, § 13.40; Ord. passed 11- -1997; Ord. passed 2- -2000;
Ord. passed 6- -2001; Ord. passed 7- -2006; Ord. passed 8- -2010)
§ 154.194 BULK REGULATIONS.
   (A)   Height. No height limitation, except where buildings are erected
within 200 feet of a residence or business district, in which case buildings or
structures shall not exceed 75 feet in height.
   (B)   Yard setbacks.
      (1)   Front yard. A front yard not less than 25 feet shall be provided.
      (2)   Side yard. No side yard shall be required, except as required in
divisions (B)(4) and (B)(5) below. However, if a side yard is provided, it
shall be not less than five feet.
      (3)   Rear yard. No rear yard shall be required, except as required in
division (B)(5) below.
      (4)   Corner side yard. There shall be provided a side yard of not less
than 25 feet in depth in every instance where the side lot line is adjacent to
a public street.
      (5)   Transitional yards. Where the side or rear lot line in the I-
2 Heavy Industrial District coincides with a side or rear lot line in an
adjacent residential district, a yard of not less than 15 feet shall be
provided along the side or rear lot line on the industrial lot.
   (C)   Floor area ratio. The floor area ratio shall not exceed 2.0.
(Ord. passed 8-11-1997, § 13.50; Ord. passed 11- -1997; Ord. passed 2- -2000;
Ord. passed 6- -2001; Ord. passed 7- -2006; Ord. passed 8- -2010)
§ 154.195 OFF-STREET PARKING AND LOADING REQUIREMENTS.
   Spaces for off-street parking and loading shall be provided in accordance
with the provisions of §§
154.320 through
154.325 of this chapter.
(Ord. passed 8-11-1997, § 13.60; Ord. passed 11- -1997; Ord. passed 2- -2000;
Ord. passed 6- -2001; Ord. passed 7- -2006; Ord. passed 8- -2010)
§ 154.196 DEVELOPMENT AND PERFORMANCE STANDARDS.
   Emissions of noise, vibration, smoke, dust or other particulate matter,
toxic materials, odor or glare shall not exceed the standards set by the
state’s Pollution Control Agency. Storage or sale of gas, fuel or oil shall
comply with and be approved by the Chief of the Fire Department.
(Ord. passed 8-11-1997, § 13.70; Ord. passed 11- -1997; Ord. passed 2- -2000;
Ord. passed 6- -2001; Ord. passed 7- -2006; Ord. passed 8- -2010)
PUD - PLANNED UNIT DEVELOPMENT DISTRICT
§ 154.210 PURPOSE.
   (A)   The PUD (Planned Unit Development District) is intended to encourage a
more efficient use of land and public services and greater amenity by allowing,
under certain circumstances, a more flexible means of land development or
redevelopment than is otherwise afforded through the strict enforcement of the
zoning requirements of other use districts for lot-by-lot development. Although
PUD developments may appear to deviate in certain aspects from a literal
interpretation of the zoning and subdivision ordinances, the PUD and its
accompanying guidelines are intended to allow freedom of design in order to
promote developments which will be an asset to the city by equaling or
surpassing the quality of developments resulting from the application of more
conventional zoning regulations.
   (B)   PUDs are conceived for use primarily in two situations:
      (1)   Vacant land. Areas of substantial open land where compliance with
this chapter may limit good urban planning, may restrict the full achievement
of the comprehensive plan or may not meet those changes in technology and
demand that would be consistent with the best interest of the city; and
      (2)   Built-up and aged areas. Areas of the city needing rehabilitation
and redevelopment, including areas which may be deficient in public facilities
and services, where it is believed that private investment should be encouraged
to contribute to that redevelopment; and in recognition that the necessary
redevelopment cannot be expected to take place in strict accordance with those
uniform regulations appropriate to more viable and established areas of the
city.
(Ord. passed 8-11-1997, § 14.10; Ord. passed 11- -1997; Ord. passed 2- -2000;
Ord. passed 6- -2001; Ord. passed 7- -2006; Ord. passed 8- -2010)
§ 154.211 PERMITTED USES.
   (A)   Each planned unit development, shall be comprised of one or more
subdistricts which shall be as follows:
      (1)   PUD - R and RH, Residential;
      (2)   PUD - C, Commercial; and
      (3)   PUD - I, Industrial.
   (B)   Permitted uses within each PUD subdistrict are as follows:
      (1)   PUD-R and RH: dwelling units in detached, semi-detached, attached
groups of attached or clustered or multi-storied structures, or any combination
thereof;
      (2)   PUD - C:
         (a)   Any commercial use which by design, use and restriction is
compatible with all adjacent uses; and
         (b)   Mixed commercial and residential use, located within the same
building.
      (3)   PUD - I: any industrial use which by design, use and restriction is
compatible with all adjacent uses; and
      (4)   In each of the above types, the following uses are permitted:
         (a)   Public and private educational and recreational facilities; and
         (b)   Accessory buildings and uses customarily incidental to a
permitted use.
(Ord. passed 8-11-1997, § 14.20; Ord. passed 11- -1997; Ord. passed 2- -2000;
Ord. passed 6- -2001; Ord. passed 7- -2006; Ord. passed 8- -2010)
§ 154.212 BULK REGULATIONS.
   (A)   Minimum lot size. There is no minimum site area for establishment of a
PUD District as long as other requirements and guidelines are met.
   (B)   Floor area ratios and open space ratios. The ratios shall be as
follows:
 
       Maximum Floor Area Ratio Minimum Open Space Ratio
PUD-R  0.25                     0.5
PUD-RH 0.75                     0.5
PUD-C  1.0                      0.5
PUD-I  1.0                      0.5
 
   (C)   Approval or rejection. The above bulk regulations, when met, shall not
automatically constitute approval of the submitted plan. The City Council
reserves the right to approve or reject any plans submitted that are not in
conformance with the city’s Comprehensive Plan, are not properly related to
adequate traffic arteries, are not compatible with surrounding land uses or are
not otherwise in the best interest of the city.
(Ord. passed 8-11-1997, § 14.30; Ord. passed 11- -1997; Ord. passed 2- -2000;
Ord. passed 6- -2001; Ord. passed 7- -2006; Ord. passed 8- -2010)
§ 154.213 GENERAL PROVISIONS.
   (A)   Ownership. A tract of land to be developed as a planned unit
development shall be under the control of:
      (1)   A single owner; or
      (2)   A group of land owners, acting through a corporation, partnership
or joint venture, where each owner agrees in advance to be bound by the
conditions and regulations which will be effective with the district.
   (B)   Plan preparation. The applicant is required to have the necessary
documents and supporting plans prepared by a registered landscape architect,
engineer, architect or certified planner.
   (C)   Number of buildings. More than one building may be placed in one
platted or recorded lot in any PUD.
   (D)   Zoning permit. No zoning permit shall be granted for any building on
land for which a plan for a PUD is in the process of city review or which does
not conform to the approved final plan.
   (E)   Occupancy and use. Occupancy and use of buildings and structures in a
PUD may be permitted when the buildings and structures have been completed to
the satisfaction of the city and the access drives and parking areas are
sufficiently completed to support emergency vehicles at all times and are kept
in a dust free condition. Final surfacing of streets, roadways and parking
areas and landscaping may be deferred over winter months upon written
application to and approval of the city.
   (F)   City and state regulations. Approval of PUD shall not relieve the
owner in any way from complying with the city and state regulations which
dictate circumstances under which subdivision plats must be approved.
   (G)   Covenants, easements and restrictions. The final plan will contain the
proposed covenants, easements and other provisions relating to the bulk,
location and density of the residential units, non-residential uses and public
facilities as are necessary for the welfare of the PUD and are consistent with
the best interest of the city.
   (H)   Streets, utilities, services and public facilities. The uniqueness of
each proposal for a PUD requires the specifications and standards for streets,
utilities and services may be subject to minor modifications from the
specifications and standards established in this chapter and other city
ordinances governing their construction. The City Council may, therefore, waive
or modify the specifications or standards where it is found that they are not
required in the interests of the residents or of the entire city.
   (I)   Open space. A primary function of the PUD provision is to encourage
development which will preserve and enhance the worthwhile, natural terrain
characteristics and not force intense development to utilize all portions of a
given site in order to arrive at the maximum density allowed. In evaluating
each individual proposal the recognition of this objective will be a basic
consideration in granting approval or denial including, but not limited to, the
following:
      (1)   The amount and location of common open space shall be consistent
with the stated purpose and intent of this section; and
      (2)   All common open space shall be labeled as such and as to its intent
or design functions.
   (J)   Architectural style. Architectural style of buildings shall not solely
be a basis for denial or approval of a plan. However, the overall appearance
and compatibility of individual buildings to other site elements or to
surrounding development will be primary considerations in the review stages of
the Planning Commission and the City Council.
   (K)   Review. The Planning Commission shall review each PUD at least once a
year until the development has been completed and shall make a report to the
City Council on the status of the development in each PUD District. If
development is not progressing according to schedule, the owner shall be
required to submit a statement to the city setting forth the reasons for the
lack of progress. If the Council finds that the development has not occurred
according to the established development schedule or is not otherwise
reasonable in the view of the Council, the Council may initiate rezoning to
remove the PUD District. If the City Council finds that insufficient progress
is made toward completion for a 24-month period following notification to the
owner of insufficient progress, the property shall automatically revert to the
zoning district existing on the property immediately prior to PUD zoning
approval. In any event, it shall not be necessary for the council to find the
rezoning to a PUD District was in error.
(Ord. passed 8-11-1997, § 14.40; Ord. passed 11- -1997; Ord. passed 2- -2000;
Ord. passed 6- -2001; Ord. passed 7- -2006; Ord. passed 8- -2010)
§ 154.214 APPROVAL PROCEDURES.
   The following procedures and requirements shall be met for the establishment
of a PUD District.
   (A)   Pre-application conference.
      (1)   Prior to filing an application for preliminary PUD plan approval,
the applicant shall consult with the Planning Commission to determine the
procedures and requirements governing approval of PUDs and shall submit a
concept plan. This plan should show the generalized overall plan for
development of the proposed PUD area. Included as a part of the overall plan
should be the following elements:
         (a)   Location and size of site;
         (b)   Street location, other public or private drives and generalized
parking areas;
         (c)   Approximate building location;
         (d)   Existing topography and illustration of existing drainage
considerations (proposed grades not necessary at this point);
         (e)   Approximate density and number and types of dwelling units;
         (f)   Existing tree masses and other geologic or environmentally
important characteristics;
         (g)   Proposed private and public ownership boundaries including
proposed private lots for sale as well as common ownership areas; and
         (h)   Project costs.
      (2)   The Planning Commission shall have 30 days in which to review and
comment in regard to the above listed items.
   (B)   Preliminary PUD Plan approval.
      (1)   Following review of and comment upon the concept plan, the
applicant shall submit an application for preliminary PUD plan approval to the
city. The application shall be deemed a petition for rezoning to a PUD
classification and shall include:
         (a)   The location, size and legal description of the site;
         (b)   The nature of the developer’s and landowner’s interest in the
land proposed to be developed including an attorney’s opinion as to ownership
or options to purchase land or lands proposed as part of the development
package;
         (c)   The total number of living units and the overall density of the
project defined as the number of living units per acre. Note: existing or
proposed public or semi-public park, church and school lands shall be excluded
from any residential density computations for purposes of determining total
residential holding capacities;
         (d)   The location and boundaries of proposed land uses, if more than
one proposed;
         (e)   Approximate height and location of buildings and other
structures;
         (f)   Land use intensity ratios computed for the proposed plan;
         (g)   Form of organization proposed to own and maintain public or
private open space;
         (h)   Preliminary layout of the proposed systems for the collection
and treatment of sanitary wastes and disposition of storm water;
         (i)   Proposed source and distribution of water facilities;
         (j)   The substance of covenants, grants of easements or other
restrictions proposed to be imposed upon the use of the land, buildings and
structures, including proposed easements or grants for utilities;
         (k)   The provisions for parking of motor vehicles including the total
number and location of public and private parking spaces;
         (l)   The location and width of proposed public and private streets
and thoroughfares;
         (m)   A schedule showing the proposed phasing and time limitations
within which applications for final approval of all phases and construction of
improvements within phases of the PUD are intended to be filed;
         (n)   Generalized preliminary grading plan, but not to include
detailed final grades or elevations;
         (o)   Generalized landscaping plan, but not to include detailed
planting list or planting plan;
         (p)   Generalized exterior building elevations and facades;
         (q)   Proposed private and public ownership boundaries including
proposed private lots for sale as well as common ownership areas;
         (r)   All maps and diagrams shall be of sufficient scale and size to
permit easy interpretation by the Commission, Council and staff;
         (s)   North arrow and graphic scale shall be provided with all maps or
sketches; and
         (t)   Maps illustrating the following information for the project area
and those adjoining properties within 200 feet (60m) of the proposed project
boundaries excluding street or highway right-of- way:
            1.   Existing zoning;
            2.   Existing land use; and
            3.   Existing ownership.
      (2)   A fee in the amount established by the city to cover the city’s
soft costs for engineering and legal services shall accompany the application.
      (3)   The Planning Commission shall hold a public hearing, following the
public notice method prescribed for zoning change requests, and make
recommendation to the City Council upon the application for preliminary
approval within 60 days following receipt of all required plans and
documentation. The City Council shall take final action of the preliminary PUD
plan within a reasonable time following receipt of the Planning Commission’s
recommendations.
      (4)   In granting approval of the preliminary PUD plan, the Planning
Commission and the City Council shall determine whether the PUD District
regulations and standards have been satisfied based on the preliminary plans
and specifications, after making the following findings:
         (a)   Ownership or contractual right to develop the property to be
developed is held by the applicant;
         (b)   Provision for public sewer and water facilities is adequate;
         (c)   The streets to be provided will assure a traffic circulation
pattern which minimizes through traffic, allows for adequate turning and
parking and provides ample space for the movement of public and private
vehicles for the servicing of the proposed land uses without blocking traffic;
         (d)   There will not be undue conflicts between pedestrian and
vehicular traffic;
         (e)   The design of open spaces and housing will assure both easy
access and privacy;
         (f)   Adequate street, parking, walkway and accessory lighting will be
provided;
         (g)   Continuity of open space and recreation areas will be
maintained;
         (h)   Provision has been made for:
            1.   The preservation of existing vegetative, geologic, historic or
other environmentally important characteristics of the site; and
            2.   Where existing landscape; materials, are inadequate to create
an esthetically pleasing atmosphere, additional trees, shrubs and other
appropriate plant materials will be added.
         (i)   In a PUD, adequate deed restrictions and covenants are present,
running in favor of any one or more of the following:
            1.   The city;
            2.   An automatic homeowner’s association; or
            3.   Individual homeowners, for the proper maintenance, care and
preservation of the exterior design, all common structures, facilities, sewer
and water utilities, pedestrian and vehicular access and open spaces by the
original and all subsequent owners of property within the development.
         (j)   In addition to other deed restrictions or covenants, running in
favor of the city, there shall be provision for the free and uninhibited access
to all private or common areas by the fire, police or other public safety
vehicles and personnel for the customary performance of their respective duties
and responsibilities.
      (5)   If the primary PUD plan is approved, the approval shall constitute
a rezoning of the property as designed on the plan and the applicant shall
proceed to final PUD plan approval. If the application for preliminary PUD plan
is denied, the applicant must submit another application and fee to receive
consideration.
   (C)   Final PUD plan approval.
      (1)   (a)   Within one year following City Council approval of the
preliminary PUD plan, an application for final PUD plan approval shall be
submitted to the Planning Commission and the City Council.
         (b)   The final PUD plan shall include either the entire site included
in the preliminary PUD plan or a portion thereof in accordance with the phasing
plan approved.
         (c)   Subsequent final PUD plan submittals shall be submitted in
accordance with the approved phasing plan.
      (2)   A fee in the amount established by the city, in addition to the
following information, drawings and data, shall accompany each application:
         (a)   A plan with locations of all structures including placement,
size and type with topography showing two-foot contour intervals;
         (b)   Elevations or sections through the site which will best indicate
the relationship of the building with the various terrain features and site
elements;
         (c)   Detailed grading and drainage plan at two-foot contour
intervals;
         (d)   Utility plan for all public utilities;
         (e)   Detailed landscape plan;
         (f)   Deed restrictions, covenants, agreements, by-laws, of proposed
homeowner’s association and other documents controlling the use of property,
type of construction or development or the activities of future residents;
         (g)   The procedures for approving and recording of plats shall be
followed if the final plan involves platting or division of land or the
platting of public streets; and
         (h)   Any other information which is necessary, to fully represent the
intentions of the preliminary plan.
      (3)   The Planning Commission shall consider the final PUD plan and make
recommendation to the City Council within 60 days following submission of the
required material. The City Council shall make its consideration and
determination within a reasonable time following receipt of the Planning
Commission’s recommendation. In granting final approval of the application, the
Planning Commission and the City Council shall determine whether the following
PUD District regulations and standards have been satisfied based on the final
PUD plans and specifications which shall include final forms of all documents,
maps, tables and other pertinent information previously submitted and revised
to the date of final submittal:
         (a)   The final PUD plan is in substantial conformity to the
preliminary PUD plan approved;
         (b)   Detailed grading plans illustrate:
            1.   Proper provision has been made in regard to drainage both on
and off the site; and
            2.   Especially that adjoining properties are not adversely
affected by improper channelization or concentration of runoff as a result of
the proposed grading.
         (c)   The applicant has entered a development agreement with the city
to provide the public and private improvements including amenities shown on the
plans.
   (D)   Changes.
      (1)   Changes in the final PUD plan involving the location and alignment
of structures not to exceed ten feet (3 m) in any direction and other minor
revisions in the shape of structures may be authorized by the city for good
cause shown provided the changes are within the maximum allowable floor area
limits, are in harmony with the intent of the concept statement as to uses and
densities of use and the architectural style has been approved in writing by
the PUD’s homeowner association or other ownership body.
      (2)   All other changes shall be made only after public hearings by the
Commission and Council, upon notice as provided in division (B) above and any
changes approved by the Council shall be by resolution as an amendment to the
final PUD plan.
(Ord. passed 8-11-1997, § 14.50; Ord. passed 11- -1997; Ord. passed 2- -2000;
Ord. passed 6- -2001; Ord. passed 7- -2006; Ord. passed 8- -2010)
BP - BLUFF PROTECTION OVERLAY DISTRICT
§ 154.225 PURPOSE.
   This overlay district is created to protect and preserve areas in the
community where bluffs and steep slopes represent an enhancement to the
community and a risk of impairment to the health, safety, tax base and general
welfare of the community if left unprotected. Its intent is to establish
reasonable regulations related to development upon and adjacent to the steep
slope areas in the community.
(Ord. passed 8-11-1997, § 15.10; Ord. passed 11- -1997; Ord. passed 2- -2000;
Ord. passed 6- -2001; Ord. passed 7- -2006; Ord. passed 8- -2010)
§ 154.226 DISTRICT APPLICATION.
   The Bluff Protection (BP) District shall be applied to and superimposed upon
all zoning districts as contained herein as existing or amended by the text and
map of this chapter. The regulations and requirements imposed by the BP
District shall be in addition to those established for districts which jointly
apply. Under the joint application of districts, the more restrictive
requirements shall apply.
(Ord. passed 8-11-1997, § 15.20; Ord. passed 11- -1997; Ord. passed 2- -2000;
Ord. passed 6- -2001; Ord. passed 7- -2006; Ord. passed 8- -2010)
§ 154.227 BOUNDARIES.
   The boundaries of the BP District shall be defined as those lots in the city
which contain bluffs and/or steep slopes. The boundaries shall be graphically
represented on preliminary and final subdivision plats as slope easements.
(Ord. passed 8-11-1997, § 15.30; Ord. passed 11- -1997; Ord. passed 2- -2000;
Ord. passed 6- -2001; Ord. passed 7- -2006; Ord. passed 8- -2010)
§ 154.228 DEVELOPMENT REGULATIONS.
   (A)   Persons desiring to construct a structure, perform grading or earth
moving activities or destroy or remove vegetation from land located within the
BP District, but not within the Bluff Impact Zone, including the required
setback area, shall only do so following review and approval by the Planning
Commission.
   (B)   Persons desiring to construct a structure, perform grading or earth
moving activities or destroy or remove vegetation from land located within a
Bluff Impact Zone, including the required setback area, shall only do so
following issuance of a conditional/interim use permit as prescribed in this
chapter.
   (C)   The plan to be submitted for consideration with regard to the
conditional/interim use permit shall include the following information:
      (1)   Existing site conditions including all structures, shrub masses,
trees and topographic conditions and site boundaries;
      (2)   Proposed structures and site improvements, grading changes, tree
and vegetation removal and landscaping improvements, including trees, shrubs
and ground covers;
      (3)   Height and size of any structures proposed to be constructed;
      (4)   Timetable for anticipated dates of construction;
      (5)   Location and design of any private sewage and other sanitary waste
disposal systems; and
      (6)   Erosion control plan during and following construction.
   (D)   Considerations for approval or denial of the conditional/interim use
permit shall include, but not be limited to the following:
      (1)   Degree to which the intent of this chapter is addressed in the
proposal;
      (2)   Compliance with the bulk and setback requirements of this district;
      (3)   Degree to which tree and vegetation removal and slope alteration
has been minimized in the proposed development;
      (4)   Appropriateness of the proposed plant material for the site for
slope protection purposes;
      (5)   The degree to which dislocated vegetation can be relocated or
replanted on the site; and
      (6)   Minimization of hard surfacing or other impervious materials within
35 feet of the bluff or steep slope.
(Ord. passed 8-11-1997, § 15.40; Ord. passed 11- -1997; Ord. passed 2- -2000;
Ord. passed 6- -2001; Ord. passed 7- -2006; Ord. passed 8- -2010)
§ 154.229 GENERAL REGULATIONS.
   The following requirements shall be imposed on all development in the BP
District.
   (A)   Structures shall not be placed within bluff impact zones.
   (B)   Setback from toe or top of the bluff to any structure in any district
shall be no less than 30 feet.
   (C)   The maximum height of any structure shall be no more than 30 feet.
   (D)   No person may conduct a mining or quarry activity or expand a mining
or quarry activity within 30 feet of the toe or top of a bluff or in BP
District.
   (E)   No tower shall be located within one-quarter mile of the bluff impact
zone, except for water reservoirs owned and constructed by the city.
   (F)   No grading, excavating or filling shall be allowed within the bluff
impact zone.
   (G)   Grading and filling within the BP District, but not within a bluff
impact zone, shall comply with the following conditions.
      (1)   No more than one-third of the surface area of a lot shall be devoid
of vegetative ground cover at any time.
      (2)   Temporary ground cover such as mulch shall be used and permanent
cover such as sod shall be planted as soon as possible.
      (3)   Methods to prevent erosion and trap sediment shall be employed in
accordance with regulations of the state’s Pollution Control Agency and
acceptable engineering practices.
      (4)   Fill shall be stabilized according to accepted engineering
standards.
      (5)   A soil erosion control plan must be submitted to the Zoning
Administrator before grading or construction begins on any lot affected by the
regulations of this chapter.
      (6)   Methods, such as silt fences, hay bales and the like must be used
to minimize soil erosion and to trap sediments before they reach any surface
water features.
      (7)   Fill or excavated material must not be placed in a manner that
creates an unstable slope.
      (8)   Alterations of topography will only be allowed if they are
accessory to permitted or conditional/interim uses and do not adversely affect
adjacent or nearby properties.
   (H)   The removal of natural vegetation shall be restricted to prevent
erosion into public waters, to consume nutrients in the soil and to preserve
steep slope aesthetics.
   (I)   Clearcutting is prohibited, except to remove the minimum amount of
vegetation necessary for placing roads, utilities, structures and parking
areas.
   (J)   Natural vegetation shall be restored insofar as feasible after any
construction project.
   (K)   Selective cutting of trees and underbrush is allowed as long as
sufficient cover is left to make cars and structures inconspicuous when viewed
from a distance.
   (L)   All stairways and lifts on bluffs shall be visually inconspicuous.
Stairways and lifts are the preferred alternatives to major topographic
alterations for achieving access up and down bluffs and steep slopes.
Stairways, lifts and landings shall meet the following design requirements.
      (1)   Stairways and lifts must not exceed four feet in width on
residential lots. Wider stairways may be used for commercial properties, public
open space, recreational properties and planned unit developments.
      (2)   Landings for stairways and lifts on residential lots must not
exceed 32 square feet in area. Landings larger than 32 square feet may be used
for commercial properties, public open space, recreational properties and
planned unit developments.
      (3)   Canopies or roofs are not allowed on stairways, lifts or landings.
      (4)   Stairways, lifts and landings may be either constructed on posts or
pilings, or placed into the ground, provided they are designed in a manner that
ensures control of soil erosion.
      (5)   Stairways, lifts and landings must be located in the most visually
inconspicuous portion of lots, as viewed from adjacent areas, assuming summer,
leaf-on conditions whenever practical.
   (M)   Vegetation alterations necessary for the construction of structures
and sewage treatment systems are exempt from the vegetation alteration
standards.
   (N)   Vegetation alterations necessary for the construction of roads and
parking areas are exempt from the vegetation alteration standards.
   (O)   Limited removal of vegetation is allowed subject to the following
standards: on steep slopes which are not located in Bluff Impact Zones,
selective clearing, cutting, pruning and trimming of trees and shrubs is
allowed to provide a view from the dwelling site and to accommodate the
placement of stairways, lifts, landings, picnic areas, access paths, beach and
watercraft access areas, and permitted water oriented accessory structures or
facilities; provided that:
      (1)   The screening of structures, vehicles or other facilities as viewed
from adjacent areas, assuming summer, leaf on conditions, is not reduced by
more than 25%;
      (2)   Along waterways, existing shading of water surface is preserved;
and
      (3)   The above provisions are not applicable to the removal of trees,
limbs or branches that are dead, diseased or pose safety hazards.
(Ord. passed 8-11-1997, § 15.50; Ord. passed 11- -1997; Ord. passed 2- -2000;
Ord. passed 6- -2001; Ord. passed 7- -2006; Ord. passed 8- -2010)
SUPPLEMENTARY REGULATIONS AND CONDITIONS
§ 154.240 CONFORMANCE REQUIRED.
   Except as hereinafter specified, no land, building or structure shall
hereafter be used, and no building or part thereof or other structure shall be
located, erected, moved, reconstructed, extended, enlarged or altered, except
in conformity with the regulations herein specified for the district in which
it is located.
(Ord. passed 8-11-1997, § 4.10; Ord. passed 11- -1997; Ord. passed 2- -2000;
Ord. passed 6- -2001; Ord. passed 7- -2006; Ord. passed 8- -2010)
§ 154.241 NUMBER OF BUILDINGS PER LOT.
   (A)   Every building shall be located on a lot. Only one principal building
shall be located, erected or moved onto a lot; except that, multiple principal
buildings may be permitted on a lot in the R~3, C-A, C-CBD, I-1, I-2 and PUD
Districts, after a plot plan showing location of proposed buildings and
development of the property shall be approved by the Planning Commission.
   (B)   In review of the plans, the Planning Commission shall consider the
following:
      (1)   Proper provision of parking facilities;
      (2)   Adequate provision of open space;
      (3)   Adequate distance between structures;
      (4)   Adequate provision for handling and storage of trash and rubbish,
and prop screening of handling and storage areas; and
      (5)   Compatibility with development character of surrounding
neighborhood.
(Ord. passed 8-11-1997, § 4.20; Ord. passed 11- -1997; Ord. passed 2- -2000;
Ord. passed 6- -2001; Ord. passed 7- -2006; Ord. passed 8- -2010)
§ 154.242 ACCESSORY BUILDING AND/OR STRUCTURES.
   (A)   No accessory building and/or structure shall be erected in any
required yard other than a rear yard, except as provided herein. Properties
whose rear yard is adjacent to an alley must have a minimum of a 20-foot
setback for garages if they are accessed from the alley. On a corner lot,
accessory buildings and/or structures shall conform to setback regulations for
principal buildings on the street side. Accessory buildings and/or structures
may be erected as a part of the principal building or may be connected thereto
by a breezeway or similar structure; provided, all yard requirements for a
principal building are complied with. Detached accessory buildings and/or
structures located nearer than 60 feet to the front lot line shall be
considered to be attached to the principal building and all yard requirements
for a principal building must be complied with.
   (B)   Accessory buildings and/or structures which are not a part of the main
building shall not occupy more than 30% of the rear yard; however, this
regulation shall not be interpreted to prohibit the construction of a 440
square foot garage in a rear yard. Accessory buildings shall not have exterior
side walls exceeding ten feet in height.
   (C)   No accessory building and/or structure shall be constructed upon a lot
more than six months prior to the commencement of construction of the main
building.
(Ord. passed 8-11-1997, § 4.30; Ord. passed 11- -1997; Ord. passed 2- -2000;
Ord. passed 6- -2001; Ord. passed 7- -2006; Ord. passed 8- -2010)
§ 154.243 DRIVEWAY SETBACK.
   Driveways should adhere to a minimum side yard requirements of five feet.
Driveways must allow for vehicle to be parked off-street by providing a minimum
of a 20-foot-long parking stall on their lot.
(Ord. passed 8-11-1997, § 4.40; Ord. passed 11- -1997; Ord. passed 2- -2000;
Ord. passed 6- -2001; Ord. passed 7- -2006; Ord. passed 8- -2010)
§ 154.244 REQUIRED AREA OR SPACE CANNOT BE REDUCED.
   All lots shall abut a public street and each lot shall have a minimum amount
of street frontage distance as required by the applicable zoning district. No
lot, yard, court, parking area or other space shall be reduced in area or
dimension so as to make the area or dimension less than the minimum required by
this chapter. No part of a yard, court, parking area or other space provided
about or for any building or structure for the purpose of complying with the
provisions of this chapter shall be included as part of a yard, court, parking
area or other space required under this chapter for another building or
structure.
(Ord. passed 8-11-1997, § 4.50; Ord. passed 11- -1997; Ord. passed 2- -2000;
Ord. passed 6- -2001; Ord. passed 7- -2006; Ord. passed 8- -2010)
§ 154.245 ZONING DISTRICT DIVIDING PROPERTY.
   Where one parcel of property is divided into two or more portions by reason
of different zoning district classifications, each portion shall be used
independently of the other in its respective zoning classification, and for the
purpose of applying the regulations of this chapter, each portion shall be
considered as if in separate and different ownership. Alternatively, the entire
parcel may be used as permitted by the regulations applicable to the most
restrictive zoning classification. However, nothing in this section shall be
construed as permitting residential use of any property within any industrial
district classification.
(Ord. passed 8-11-1997, § 4.60; Ord. passed 11- -1997; Ord. passed 2- -2000;
Ord. passed 6- -2001; Ord. passed 7- -2006; Ord. passed 8- -2010)
§ 154.246 BULK REGULATIONS FOR RESIDENTIAL DISTRICTS.
   The following bulk regulations shall be adhered to within the R-1, R-2 and
R-3 Residential Districts.
   (A)   For all zoning permits issued after the effective date of this
chapter, the principal structure shall have a floor area of not less than 768
square feet and the minimum dimension of the main body of the dwelling unit
shall not be less than 24 feet.
   (B)   All structures shall be roofed with a non-metal type of roofing
material, except copper and pre- finished metal roofs that simulate shingles,
shakes or tiles are permitted. No corrugated steel or galvanized metal roofs
are permitted on all principal structures.
   (C)   All principal structures shall be sided with a material other than
flat or corrugated steel or galvanized metal.
(Ord. passed 8-11-1997, § 4.70; Ord. passed 11- -1997; Ord. passed 2- -2000;
Ord. passed 6- -2001; Ord. passed 7- -2006; Ord. passed 8- -2010)
§ 154.247 DEVELOPMENT PLANS.
   (A)   In review of development plans as required by §
154.241  of this chapter, the Planning Commission shall consider compliance
with this chapter as well as compatibility of the proposed development with
development characteristics of the surrounding neighborhood. The following
criteria shall be considered in the approval, conditional approval or
disapproval of the development plan:
      (1)   Adequate provision of open space;
      (2)   Adequate placement of landscaping material to screen adjoining
areas of a less intense use;
      (3)   Adequate land area to ensure an acceptable density; and
      (4)   Adequate provision for traffic circulation.
   (B)   The Planning Commission may impose conditions that are more
restrictive than the general regulations for the zoning district in which the
development plan is being proposed.
(Ord. passed 8-11-1997, § 4.80; Ord. passed 11- -1997; Ord. passed 2- -2000;
Ord. passed 6- -2001; Ord. passed 7- -2006; Ord. passed 8- -2010)
§ 154.248 DIKE SETBACK.
   The dike is the manmade structure constructed by the U.S. Army Corp of
Engineers for flood control protection. There shall be a minimum building
setback requirement of 20 feet from the dike. This distance is to be measured
from whichever is closest the top of the dike or the toe of the dike. The “toe
of the dike” is the point on the dike where there is, as visually observed, a
clearly identifiable break in the slope from gentler to steeper slope above. If
no break in the slope is apparent or if there is disagreement in the breaking
point, the toe of the dike shall be the lowest end of a 50-foot segment
measured on the ground, with an average slope of 20% or greater. The “top of
the dike” is the point of the dike where there is, as visually observed, a
clearly identifiable break in the slope, from steeper to gentler slope above.
If no break in the slope is apparent or if there is disagreement in the
breaking point, the top of the dike shall be the highest end of a 50-foot
segment, measured on the ground, with an average slope of 20% or greater.
(Ord. passed 8-11-1997, § 4.90; Ord. passed 11- -1997; Ord. passed 2- -2000;
Ord. passed 6- -2001; Ord. passed 7- -2006; Ord. passed 8- -2010)
§ 154.249 HEIGHT EXCEPTIONS.
   (A)   The height limitations stipulated in this chapter shall not apply to
the following:
      (1)   Farm buildings, architectural features and the like. Barns, silos
or other farm buildings or structures on farms; church spires, belfries,
cupolas and domes, monuments, water towers, fire and hose towers, windmills,
chimneys, smokestacks, flagpoles, radio towers, masts and aerials; parapet
walls extending not more than four feet above the limiting height of the
building; and
      (2)   Places of public assembly. Places of public assembly in churches,
schools and other permitted public and semi-public buildings; provided that,
these are located on the first floor of the building; and, provided that, for
each three feet by which the height of the building exceeds the maximum height
otherwise permitted in the district, its side and rear yards shall be increased
in width or depth by an additional foot over the side and rear yards required
for the highest building otherwise permitted in the district.
   (B)   All such structures above the heights otherwise permitted in the
district shall not occupy more than 25% of the area of the lot and shall be
distant not less than 25 feet in all parts from every lot line not a street lot
line.
(Ord. passed 8-11-1997, § 4.100; Ord. passed 11- -1997; Ord. passed 2- -2000;
Ord. passed 6- -2001; Ord. passed 7- -2006; Ord. passed 8- -2010)
§ 154.250 FENCES AND WALLS.
   (A)   Residential fences and walls, including retaining walls, are permitted
in any yard and may be constructed on the lot lines in residential districts,
except as herein provided. However, when fences or walls are constructed on the
lot line, the owner must provide evidence of the exact location of the lot
line. Fences or walls shall not in any case exceed a height of six feet; except
the fences or walls shall not exceed a height of four feet in the front yard.
Privacy fences and wood fences that require painting, staining and maintenance
shall have a minimum of two feet setback from all adjoining side and rear lot
lines, unless a written mutual agreement as prescribed by the city between
adjoining property owners is filed with the Zoning Administrator.
   (B)   Security or screening fences are permitted on the lot lines in all
districts, except residential districts, but shall not exceed ten feet in
height. No fence or wall shall interfere with the maintenance of overhead or
underground utilities.
(Ord. passed 8-11-1997, § 4.110; Ord. passed 11- -1997; Ord. passed 2- -2000;
Ord. passed 6- -2001; Ord. passed 7- -2006; Ord. passed 8- -2010)
§ 154.251 VISIBILITY AT INTERSECTIONS.
   (A)   On any corner lot in all districts, except C-CBD District, there is
established a traffic visibility zone at the intersection of two or more
streets.
   (B)   The traffic visibility zone shall include that part of a corner lot
that is within the area bounded by the intersecting street right-of-way lines
and a diagonal line intersecting the street right-of-way lines at a distance of
20 feet from the corner (the point of intersection of the right-of-way lines of
two intersection streets).
   (C)   In any traffic visibility zone, no fence, structure, earth bank,
hedge, planting or other obstruction shall be erected, planted or maintained
which materially impedes vision in the visibility zone between the heights of
three feet and ten feet above the established curb grade.
(Ord. passed 8-11-1997, § 4.120; Ord. passed 11- -1997; Ord. passed 2- -2000;
Ord. passed 6- -2001; Ord. passed 7- -2006; Ord. passed 8- -2010)
§ 154.252 PRIVATE SWIMMING POOLS.
   A swimming pool, as regulated herein, shall be any pool or open tank not
located within a completely enclosed building and containing or normally
capable of containing water to a depth at any point greater than one and one-
half feet. No swimming pool shall be allowed in any residential district,
except as an accessory use and unless it complies with the following conditions
and requirements.
   (A)   The pool is intended and is to be used solely for the enjoyment of the
occupants of the principal building of the property on which it is located and
their guests.
   (B)   The swimming pool shall not be located in a required side yard or
closer than ten feet to any lot line of the property on which it is located.
The pump and filter installations shall be located not closer than 20 feet to
any lot line.
   (C)   The swimming pool or the entire property on which it is located, shall
be so walled or fenced as to prevent uncontrolled access by children from the
street or from adjacent properties. The wall or fence shall be not less than
five feet in height.
(Ord. passed 8-11-1997, § 4.130; Ord. passed 11- -1997; Ord. passed 2- -2000;
Ord. passed 6- -2001; Ord. passed 7- -2006; Ord. passed 8- -2010)
§ 154.253 HEDGES, LANDSCAPING AND PLANTINGS.
   Hedges, landscaping and plantings requiring upkeep, trimming or maintenance
from the side facing an adjoining side or rear lot shall have a two-foot
setback from all adjoining side and rear lot lines, unless a written mutual
agreement as prescribed by the city between adjoining property owners is filed
with the Zoning Administrator.
(Ord. passed 8-11-1997, § 4.140; Ord. passed 11- -1997; Ord. passed 2- -2000;
Ord. passed 6- -2001; Ord. passed 7- -2006; Ord. passed 8- -2010)
§ 154.254 BOULEVARDS.
   (A)   Boulevards are city property, and the adjacent property owner is
responsible for maintenance for lawn, plantings and landscaping in a manner
that does not affect the health and safety of residents.
   (B)   (1)   No permanent structures other than mailboxes may be placed on a
boulevard without the written permission of the adjoining property owner and
Zoning Administrator.
      (2)   Mailboxes must be placed in accordance with U.S. Post Office
regulations.
(Ord. passed 8-11-1997, § 4.150; Ord. passed 11- -1997; Ord. passed 2- -2000;
Ord. passed 6- -2001; Ord. passed 7- -2006; Ord. passed 8- -2010)
NON-CONFORMING BUILDINGS AND USES
§ 154.265 INTENT.
   (A)   Within the districts established by this chapter, there exists lots,
structures, uses of land and structures and characteristics of use which were
lawful prior to adoption or amendment of this chapter but are prohibited,
regulated or restricted under the terms of this chapter or future amendments.
It is the intent of this chapter to permit these non-conformities to continue
until they are removed, but not to encourage their survival. It is further the
intent of this chapter that non-conformities shall not be enlarged upon or
extended, nor be used as grounds for adding other structures or uses prohibited
elsewhere in the same district.
   (B)   Non-conforming uses are declared by this chapter to be incompatible
with permitted uses in the districts involved. A non-conforming use of
structure, a non-conforming use of land, or a non- conforming use of structure
and land in combination shall not be extended or enlarged after the passage of
this chapter by attachment on a building or premises or additional signs
intended to be seen from off the premises or by the addition of other uses of a
nature which would generally be prohibited in the district involved.
   (C)   To avoid undue hardship, nothing in this chapter shall be deemed to
require a change in the plans, construction or designated use of any building
for which a zoning permit was lawfully issued prior to the effective date of
adoption or amendment of this chapter and upon which a total building
construction has been diligently carried on.
(Ord. passed 8-11-1997, § 19.10; Ord. passed 11- -1997; Ord. passed 2- -2000;
Ord. passed 6- -2001; Ord. passed 7- -2006; Ord. passed 8- -2010)
§ 154.266 NON-CONFORMING LOTS OF RECORD.
   In any district in which single-family dwellings are permitted, where a lot
has less area or width than required by the district and was a lot of record at
the effective date of adoption or amendment of this chapter, a single-family
dwelling and customary accessory buildings may be constructed on the lot.
(Ord. passed 8-11-1997, § 19.20; Ord. passed 11- -1997; Ord. passed 2- -2000;
Ord. passed 6- -2001; Ord. passed 7- -2006; Ord. passed 8- -2010)
§ 154.267 NON-CONFORMING STRUCTURES.
   Where a lawful structure exists at the effective date of adoption or
amendment of this chapter that could not be built under the terms hereof by
reason of restrictions: on area, lot coverage, height, yard, its location on
the lot or other requirements concerning the structure, the structure may be
continued, so long as it remains otherwise lawful, subject to the following
provisions.
   (A)   No such non-conforming structure may be enlarged or altered in such a
way which increases its non-conformity, but any structure or portion thereof
may be altered to decrease its non-conformity.
   (B)   Should the non-conforming structure or non-conforming portion of a
structure be destroyed or deteriorated to an extent of more than 50% of its
replacement cost at time of destruction, it shall not be reconstructed, except
in conformity with the provisions of this chapter.
   (C)   Should the structure be moved for any reason for any distance
whatever, it shall thereafter conform to the regulations for the district in
which it is located after it is moved.
(Ord. passed 8-11-1997, § 19.30; Ord. passed 11- -1997; Ord. passed 2- -2000;
Ord. passed 6- -2001; Ord. passed 7- -2006; Ord. passed 8- -2010)
§ 154.268 NON-CONFORMING USES OF LAND.
   The lawful use of land, where the use involves no individual structure with
a replacement cost exceeding an amount as set by City Council from time to
time, which becomes non-conforming under the terms of this chapter as adopted
or amended, may be continued so long as it remains otherwise lawful, subject to
the following provisions.
   (A)   No non-conforming use shall be enlarged or increased, nor extended, to
occupy a greater area of land than was occupied at the effective date of
adoption or amendment of this chapter.
   (B)   No non-conforming use shall be moved in whole or in part to any other
portion of the lot or parcel which was not occupied by the use at the effective
date of adoption or amendment of this chapter.
   (C)   If any such non-conforming use of land ceases for any reason for a
period of more than six months, any subsequent use of the land shall conform to
the district regulations for the district in which the land is located.
   (D)   No additional structure not conforming to the requirements of this
chapter shall be erected in connection with the non-conforming use of land.
(Ord. passed 8-11-1997, § 19.40; Ord. passed 11- -1997; Ord. passed 2- -2000;
Ord. passed 6- -2001; Ord. passed 7- -2006; Ord. passed 8- -2010)
§ 154.269 NON-CONFORMING USES OF STRUCTURES AND PREMISES IN COMBINATION.
   If the lawful use involving individual structures with a replacement cost of
greater than an amount as set by City Council from time to time or of such a
structure and premises in combination, becomes non-conforming under the terms
of this chapter as adopted or amended, the lawful use may be continued so long
as it remains otherwise lawful, subject to the following provisions.
   (A)   No existing structure devoted to a use not permitted by this chapter
in the district in which it is located shall be enlarged, extended,
constructed, reconstructed, moved or structurally altered except in changing
the use of the structure to a use permitted in the district in which it is
located.
   (B)   Any non-conforming use may be extended throughout any parts of a
building which were manifestly arranged or designed for the use at the time of
adoption or amendment of this chapter. No such use shall be extended to occupy
any land outside the building.
   (C)   If no structural alterations are made, a non-conforming use of a
structure may be changed to another non-conforming use of similar nature within
the same or a more restricted classification. Whenever a non-conforming use has
been changed to a more restricted use or to a conforming use, the use shall not
thereafter be changed to a less restrictive use.
   (D)   In the event that a non-conforming use of a structure, or structure
and land in combination, is discontinued or abandoned for a period of one year,
the use of the same shall thereafter conform to the uses permitted in the
district in which it is located. Where non-conforming use status applies to a
structure and land in combination, removal or destruction of the structure
shall eliminate the non- conforming status of the land.
   (E)   Any structure devoted to use made non-conforming by this chapter that
is destroyed or deteriorated to an extent of 50% or more of its original cost
or market value at the time of destruction or deterioration, exclusive of the
foundations, shall not be reconstructed and used as before the happening. If
the structure be less than 50% destroyed above the foundation, it may be
reconstructed and used as before; provided, it be done within six months of the
happening, and be built of like or similar materials.
   (F)   When non-conforming use status applies to a structure and premises in
combination, removal or destruction of the structure shall eliminate the non-
conforming status of the land. DESTRUCTION, for the purpose of this division
(F), is defined as damage or deteriorated to an extent of more than 50% of the
replacement cost at the time of destruction.
(Ord. passed 8-11-1997, § 19.50; Ord. passed 11- -1997; Ord. passed 2- -2000;
Ord. passed 6- -2001; Ord. passed 7- -2006; Ord. passed 8- -2010)
§ 154.270 REQUIRED REPAIRS.
   Nothing in this chapter shall be deemed to prevent the strengthening or
restoring to a safe condition of any building or part thereof declared to be
unsafe by any official charged with protecting the public safety, upon order of
the official.
(Ord. passed 8-11-1997, § 19.60; Ord. passed 11- -1997; Ord. passed 2- -2000;
Ord. passed 6- -2001; Ord. passed 7- -2006; Ord. passed 8- -2010)
§ 154.271 UNAUTHORIZED NON-CONFORMITIES.
   Any use of land, use of structure or any structure in existence at the time
of adoption of this chapter which was not an authorized non-conformity under
previous zoning ordinances, shall not be authorized to continue its non-
conforming status pursuant to this chapter or amendments thereto.
(Ord. passed 8-11-1997, § 19.70; Ord. passed 11- -1997; Ord. passed 2- -2000;
Ord. passed 6- -2001; Ord. passed 7- -2006; Ord. passed 8- -2010)
FLOODPLAIN MANAGEMENT
§ 154.285 STATUTORY AUTHORIZATION.
   The legislature of the state has, in M.S. Chapter 103F and Chapter 462, as
they may be amended from time to time, delegated the responsibility to local
government units to adopt regulations designed to minimize flood losses.
(Ord. passed 6-24-2019)
§ 154.286 PURPOSE.
   (A)   (1)   This subchapter regulates development in the flood hazard areas
of the city.
      (2)   These flood hazard areas are subject to periodic inundation, which
may result in loss of life and property, health and safety hazards, disruption
of commerce and governmental services, extraordinary public expenditures for
flood protection and relief, and impairment of the tax base.
      (3)   It is the purpose of this chapter to promote the public health,
safety and general welfare by minimizing these losses and disruptions.
   (B)   This subchapter is adopted to comply with the rules and regulations of
the National Flood Insurance Program codified as 44 C.F.R. parts 59 through 78,
as amended, so as to maintain the community’s eligibility in the National Flood
Insurance Program.
   (C)   This subchapter is, in part, intended to preserve the natural
characteristics and functions of watercourses and floodplains in order to
moderate flood and storm water impacts, improve water quality, reduce soil
erosion, protect aquatic and riparian habitat, provide recreational
opportunities, provide aesthetic benefits and enhance community and economic
development.
(Ord. passed 6-24-2019)
§ 154.287 LANDS TO WHICH SUBCHAPTER APPLIES.
   (A)   This subchapter applies to all lands within the jurisdiction of the
city within the boundaries of the Floodway, Flood Fringe and General Floodplain
Districts. The boundaries of these districts are determined by scaling
distances on the Flood Insurance Rate Map, or as modified in accordance with §
154.294(A)(2) of this chapter.
   (B)   The Floodway, Flood Fringe and General Floodplain Districts are
overlay districts that are superimposed on all existing zoning districts. The
standards imposed in the overlay districts are in addition to any other
requirements in this subchapter. In case of a conflict, the more restrictive
standards will apply.
   (C)   Where a conflict exists between the floodplain limits illustrated on
the official floodplain maps and actual field conditions, the flood elevations
shall be the governing factor in locating the regulatory floodplain limits.
   (D)   Persons contesting the location of the district boundaries will be
given a reasonable opportunity to present their case to the Planning Commission
and to submit technical evidence.
(Ord. passed 6-24-2019)
§ 154.288 INCORPORATION OF MAPS BY REFERENCE.
   The following maps together with all attached material are hereby adopted by
reference and declared to be a part of the Official Zoning Map and this
chapter. The attached material includes the Flood Insurance Study for Fillmore
County, Minnesota, and Incorporated Areas and the Emergency Management Agency.
These materials are on file in the office of the City Clerk.

27045C0142E
